  find, by group / by vendor, columns — plus **+** on every item, **Add all
  N** on every group and **Load** on every assembly, into the area picked at
  the top. A tapped **+** turns into a green **✓** (✓2 when added twice) and a
  green toast pinned at the top of the book says "Added X to the budget ·
  N added so far". A bid line's own area ("1,769.80 SY") becomes the section's area
  (`itemQuantity()` in `bid-intake/rules.ts` — the number is on the line the
  bid wrote; a count like "8 EA" isn't an area and stays for the line).
  Deployed in `bid-intake` v12 (2026-10-02).
  **Fixed 2026-10-02:** every card from the JobTread import carries
  `price_source='import'`, which a budget line's check (048) does not
  allow, so no card could be added. `lineSource()` (`budget-server.ts`)
  leaves a source the line can't hold blank on the line (the card keeps
  it).
- **A section measured by count (migration 065, applied 2026-10-02 on
  Coy's "Apply 065").** Coy: *"it's actually a quantity per item. So it's not
  an area, it's a quantity."* `job_sections.count_qty` / `count_unit`
  (nullable, checked > 0, unit ≤ 12 characters; additive, RLS unchanged). A
  section is **Measured by** Length × width · Area only · **Count** — "How
  many" and its unit (EA by default); a bid line's "8 EA" is one tap. A
  counted section has no area and no pour, so nothing on it is missing; a
  line on it priced per each (typed-quantity rule, unit EA / each, or the
  same unit word) takes the count as its quantity unless Coy types one
  (`ruleQty()`), and its items and groups sit under it like any other area.
  The budget shows "8 EA · $X/EA"; the proposal shows "8 EA"; Dusty sees
  "counted 8 EA". A section set to Count shows no Thickness or Concrete
  cells (Coy: "thickness isn't required for counted items").
- **A section measured in linear feet (Coy, 2026-10-02: "in addition to
  length/width, area and counts, I need linear ft").** **Measured by** gains
  **Linear ft**: the length is stored as the count with `count_unit='LF'`
  (no migration); a line on it priced per LF (LF, lin ft, ft) takes it. For a
  pour — a grade beam, a curb — the beam's **width (in)** is kept in
  `width_ft` and its **depth** in `depth_in`: plan area = LF × width, yards =
  area × depth (`quantities()`, tested on Walker High's 430 LF of 8" × 18"
  grade beams). Without them the yards name what they need; nothing per LF
  waits. A bid line's "430 LF" is one tap. The proposal shows "430 LF";
  Dusty sees the LF, the plan area and the yards.
- **A draft budget from the job's calls and texts (Coy's handoff,
  2026-10-07: job → draft budget; migration 087, applied 2026-10-07 on
  Coy's "Apply 087"; checked after: RLS on both tables, 5 policies, anon no
  grants, approvals insert and read only, the origin check takes 'draft').** **Build draft budget** on the job page (`DraftStrip`,
  `draft-strip.tsx`; `/api/jobs/[id]/budget/draft` GET · POST · PATCH, `…/approve`)
  reads the job's person and their customer's people — texts, call transcript
  lines (speaker by phone number), voicemails, Quo's summaries (weaker),
  Coy's notes on the job and its description — inside the job's own window
  when the person has other jobs (messages carry no job id). **The reader is
  plain code** (`src/lib/draft/extract.ts`, tested in `tests/draft.test.mjs`):
  sizes (30x40, "30 by 40", "N feet long … N wide", sq ft), thickness, base
  and old slab kept apart, psi, the kind of work, tear-out or new; each fact
  keeps its line, who said it and when. Coy's word beats the customer's, a
  correction beats an earlier line, otherwise two values are a **conflict**
  and neither is used — a question, never a guess or a zero. With
  `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; `DRAFT_MODEL` optional) a
  second reading comes from a direct, tool-less JSON call — never Hermes; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set in Vercel 2026-10-07 on every environment; a key not made inside a workspace needs `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID`, sent as `anthropic-workspace-id` — and
  a fact of it is kept only if its line exists, its quote is in that line and
  its number is in the quote (`modelSeen()`); where it differs from the plain
  reading it is shown as "Heard differently", Coy's tap to use. The
  messages are data: nothing in them is followed. **What it writes:** only
  when the job has no areas, an area per kind of work heard
  (`job_sections.origin='draft'`, the line as `source_ref`), sized only from
  what was said, and Coy's auto-loading assembly fills its lines as for any
  new area; on a job with areas it only suggests. **The driveway recipe**
  (`src/lib/draft/recipe.ts`, `driveway-replacement-v1`): tear-out, haul-off,
  base, forming, reinforcement, concrete, finishing, equipment,
  mobilization — each covered by named budget lines, left off with Coy's
  reason ("Not on this job", `estimates.recipe_exclusions`) or by a quoted
  "new" line, or open. Outcome: Ready for your review · Needs information ·
  Needs you (not a driveway job — anything non-residential or pool, wall,
  foundation… goes to manual review, nothing made). **Approve** is refused
  while a line is unpriced, an area unsized or a step open; it saves a frozen
  copy (`budget_approvals`, insert only) and sends nothing. **Idempotent:**
  `budget_runs.idem_key` = job + a hash of what was read + the recipe, one
  queued or running build per job, a stale or failed one reclaimed. **On job
  creation** when `BUDGET_DRAFT_AUTO=on` (off by default): `POST /api/jobs`
  queues a run and builds it after the response (`after()`), so a failed
  build never blocks the job. The concrete card's rule takes "Order in steps
  of" (`rule_params.round_cy`, e.g. 0.25 — 14.67 CY orders 14.75). Had 087 been
  missing, the strip would say to apply it.
- **Scope of work first, then Market rate by cost type (Coy, 2026-10-11: "it should start with the Scope of Work in each of the sections … a bulleted list of items from start to finish … Then I should be able pull those sections, SOW's, Notes, ect into the market rates page to do research on what are some cost ranges for these costs types (labor, materials, equipment, management, Overhead, misc.)"; no migration).** The Scope of work emblem (budget and proposal) sends `sowQuestion()` (`src/lib/scope-of-work.ts`): bullets in the order the work is done, built from his job context (calls, texts, emails, file and photo reads, the plans' words, takeoffs, the section's size, notes and items), an unknown said as a bullet, no prices. Market rate → By section carries each section's bullets and Notes (`MarketSection.sow` / `notes`, from `prep_notes` / `access_notes`) and shows them when a section is opened. Its lookup searches two queries a section (the installed price in its unit, and a labor / material / equipment breakdown; at most four sections, 20 results), still with the section's name and the city only — the scope and notes can hold an address and are never searched. They go to the model (`renderAsks()`), which gives a price to the step it is for and files it by cost type: `RATE_SPLITS` adds management, overhead and misc. `typeRanges()` (`src/lib/budget-v3.ts`, tested in `tests/market-sow.test.mjs`) shows one range per cost type, kind and unit — Labor · Materials · Equipment · Management · Overhead · Misc. · All-in — with its sources and which steps. Nothing is priced from them; the old lookups read as before.
- **The emblem researches a section (Coy, 2026-10-11, five toolbar notes on Gulfline's Curb and Tree Removal: the Draft budget box should go, "Look up again" — what does it do?, the curb rates were for installing when the job is removal only, and the steps should come from the scope of work, open to their parts — skid steer with breaker a day, operator, delivery, fuel, dump truck a day, superintendent a day; no migration).** The Draft budget box is off the job page (`DraftStrip` kept, unused). On Market rate the top Look up button and By section's Look up are gone; By step shows only an older lookup. Each section has the emblem: one tap runs `researchSection()` (`build-server.ts`) — a tool-less model call itemizes the section's name, size, scope of work and Notes into 3–10 costs, each with its cost type and unit (`ITEMIZE_SYSTEM`, told to follow the scope exactly — demolition is removal, never installing; `sanitizeItems()`), one search per item, at most eight (`itemQueries()`: the item's words and the city, with numbers of three digits or more and every word of the job's address, customer and person taken out), and a second read files each price on its item (`ITEM_RATES_SYSTEM`; a rate's `item`, its split the item's cost type). Kept as a "sections" lookup with `items` beside the rates; opened, a section lists its costs as sub-items — name, cost type, the range and its quotes — then the ranges by cost type. Nothing is priced from them. Tested in `tests/market-sow.test.mjs`.
- **A section's costs, priced and acted on (Coy, 2026-10-11, toolbar notes on Gulfline's Curb and Tree Removal: "what the status button is good for if you can't find the going rate", a price range on each line, the cost types to the left, an AI icon to dig deeper, Dusty making items, groups and assemblies from it, and the section's total reconsidered; no migration).** `researchSection()` searches each cost, then gives up to four costs still bare a second, wider look (`deepQueries()`: by cost type — a wage, a day's rental, a price — private words stripped as `itemQueries()`), then one tool-less model call adds the priced costs up into **the section all in** (`ROLLUP_SYSTEM`, `sanitizeTotal()`: low ≤ high, a per-unit only with a size, its basis naming the quantities assumed), kept on the lookup as `totals`. A cost's row reads cost type · name · range; a bare one has the emblem, which digs deeper on it alone (`POST …/budget/market {sections:[id], item}` → `researchItem()`, the section's other prices kept and its total added up again); a priced one has **+**, and the costs' header **Make items, group & assembly ›**, both opening Dusty with the costs and ranges to plan `new_item` / group / assembly steps marked estimate — Coy's Apply. The section's going rate shows "≈" Dusty's estimate when nothing was found for the section whole; its "No rate" is a button — **Research**, or **Estimate** once there is one. Nothing is priced from them. Tested in `tests/market-sow.test.mjs`.
- **The Price book drop-down and Market rate's steps (Coy, 2026-10-11, toolbar notes on Gulfline's Curb and Tree Removal; no migration).** The budget's Price book drop-down is titled a small "Price Book" (`Drawer small`), with no "Whole job" line; under it one small line, "Add Cost Items →" and the area picker. + Group and + Card are gone from it, and "Not in the Price book? Make it here" is the emblem, which opens Dusty with a request for a new cost item or group to finish. Market rate's By step lists only steps with a line on the budget (an older driveway lookup's concrete and finishing steps don't show on a removal job), its hint line is gone, and a Use tap says on its row that it is writing, or why it can't (no single line, no O.H. or margin, a unit it can't turn into the line's).
- **Any job, drafted from its scope (Coy, 2026-10-11: "Why can't Dusty draft commercial budgets along with residential? It needs to be able to draft any type of budget from the scope. It starts with the scope of work then drafts a budget from costs associated with that"; no migration).** The driveway recipe stays for residential driveways and flatwork. Every other job (job type not residential, or work the recipe doesn't know — a pool, a wall, curbs, bollards) is kind `scope` (`extract()`), no longer "Needs you — not a driveway job". Its checklist is the job's own sections, one step each (`s:<section id>`), plus mobilization for the whole job (`scopeSteps()`, `src/lib/draft/scope.ts`, tested in `tests/draft-scope.test.mjs`): covered when the section has cost items, left off with Coy's reason, else open. A section's words are its name, each scope-of-work bullet and the bid's line (`sectionPhrases()`); Coy's Price book items whose own name shares them are its candidates (`itemsForPhrase()`, filler like "remove" and "existing" left out). On a draft that started empty, each phrase whose best match is priced and clearly ahead goes on that section by itself (`fillScopeSteps()`), after Coy's auto-loading assemblies; toss-ups and unpriced items are chips; nothing is Make it, named for the section. A tap puts an item on that section only. No prices are invented; going rates for a scope job are per section (Market rate → By section). A scope job with no sections asks for them.
- **The draft fills its steps from the Price book, and looks up going
  rates (Coy, 2026-10-07: "intelligently find items … if he is unsure and/or
  needs to create the items, he prompts me … look up the costs … and the
  going rate per sqft or linear ft or sqyd"; no migration).** Each open
  recipe step looks for Coy's own items (`src/lib/draft/fill.ts`, tested in
  `tests/draft-fill.test.mjs`): by `stepsOf()`, his most used first. **The
  one he always uses** — priced, and on more of his budget lines than any
  other — goes onto a draft that started empty by itself (Form up and Finish
  closes forming and finishing at once), on every area, or once for the job
  for mobilization and equipment, copied as any + Add. A toss-up (two mesh
  mats used once each) or an unpriced item is **Which one?** chips under the
  step; nothing at all is **Make it** — a name the recipe recognises, a unit
  that counts itself, and Coy's price or none, saved in the Price book and
  put on (`PATCH …/budget/draft {step, item_id}` / `{step, make}`). The
  job's measured areas answer "how big" and "how thick" (`withJobAreas()`:
  same kind, or the only area of each), so a measured job is not "size not
  said", and an area drawn as a slab that they called a driveway takes the
  driveway's assembly when its own kind has none. **Going rates** (`POST
  …/budget/draft/rates`, `src/lib/draft/rates.ts`, tested in
  `tests/draft-rates.test.mjs`): up to four web searches (Tavily) with the
  kind of work and the job's city only — never the customer — then one
  tool-less model call (`modelJson()`) lists the prices; `sanitizeRates()`
  keeps one only if its page is a result, its quote is in that page and its
  numbers are in the quote. Kept on the build (`made.rates`), shown as
  ranges per sq ft / lin ft / sq yd / cu yd, labor / material / equipment
  when the page says so, what customers pay vs your cost, each with its
  site, and the whole-driveway range beside the budget's own $/sq ft.
  A rate never prices anything: **Use** on a cost opens Make it at the
  middle of the range, its source in the description, for Coy to change and
  save.
- **Several budgets on a job, each named (migration 088, applied
  2026-10-07 on Coy's "apply 088"; checked after: `estimates.name`, the
  status check takes 'saved', `budget_runs.estimate_id`, `sc_open_budget`
  invoker and not for anon, Wilson Driveway's one budget and 7 lines
  untouched; Coy, 2026-10-07: "save and name the budget (give a
  name suggestion like draft 1 or draft 2). This way when i click on build
  draft budget, it can build a new budget for me to look at and I can open
  either one").** A job still has ONE **open** budget — the estimate with
  status `draft` (048's unique index), which the budget screen, the
  proposal, the Dashboard, job totals and Dusty read, unchanged. Every other
  budget on the job is `saved`: kept whole with its own lines, O.H., margin,
  tax switch, recipe exclusions and approvals; nothing is copied.
  `estimates.name` (1–60). **Budgets ▾** on the Draft budget strip lists
  them (items, total at its own O.H. and margin, last changed), names or
  renames one, and **Open** opens a saved one — `sc_open_budget()` (SECURITY
  INVOKER) puts the open one aside in the same transaction, named "Draft N"
  if it had no name (`suggestBudgetName()`, `src/lib/budgets.ts`, tested), so
  a job never has none open. **Build draft budget never writes over a
  budget with items on it**: it asks what to keep the open one as and what to
  call the new one (Draft 1 / Draft 2 suggested), opens a new empty budget
  (the job type's O.H. and margin, as any new one) and builds into it; on a
  job that already has areas, Coy's auto-loading assembly fills each area of
  the new budget and the sizes heard stay suggestions. "Read again into this
  one" keeps the old in-place build. **The areas are the job's, shared by
  every budget** — a size changed shows in all of them; deleting an area
  takes its items off the open budget only (a saved one's lines become
  whole-job lines). `budget_runs.estimate_id`: each budget shows its own
  build and approval, and the same calls and texts can be built again into a
  new budget (`idem_key` = job + budget + input). APIs `GET/POST
  /api/jobs/[id]/budgets` (rename, open, new), `POST …/budget/draft
  {new_budget, name, park_name}` — session client, org from the session.
  Rehearsed (rolled back) on Wilson Driveway as its owner: parked as Draft 1,
  a new Draft 2 opened, Draft 1 reopened with its 7 lines. Without 088
  Budgets ▾ says so and Build works as before. Not built: deleting
  a budget.
- **Dusty plans budgets; Coy applies them (Coy, 2026-10-02: "chat with Dusty
  to analyze the list of items in my cost book and apply the items that I
  might need or create new items or modify cost items and organize by
  groups").** On a job Dusty's context carries every area by an 8-character
  ref (with its SF and SY) and the whole Price book by item ref, group, unit,
  cost and rule. Asked to build or fix the budget he answers in words and ends
  with one ```` ```scopecrete-plan ```` block — add an item or a whole group to
  an area (a qty only when written or said), make a new item (and put it on
  the budget), change an item's cost / unit / name / group, make a group
  (`src/lib/dusty/plan.ts`, tested: refs only from the context, a made-up ref
  or step is dropped, no cost stays null). The panel shows it as numbered
  steps with **Apply** (`plan-card.tsx`); the tap runs them through the same
  APIs Coy's own taps use, under his session (`apply-plan.ts`), and the
  budget reloads. **Dusty still changes nothing himself.**
  **Since 2026-10-03 a plan can also make areas and count from a takeoff:**
  `new_area` (a key — new1, new2 — a name, a kind of area, and a size in
  sq ft, LF or a count and a depth only when written in the context) which
  later steps put items on by its key; and `add` with `takeoff: <group ref>`,
  a line counted from a Plan Room group (the aerial or a sheet) that moves
  when Coy re-measures. The context lists takeoff groups by ref.
  **And the Price book and its assemblies (Coy, 2026-10-03: "it would be
  good if dusty could edit my cost book (add, remove, edit cost items/groups
  and build out cost assemblies for specific jobs … pull in an Assembly for
  a driveway breakout and pourback and build an assembly if there isn't
  one").** New steps: `retire_item` ("remove" is retire — budgets that used
  it keep their lines; Show retired brings it back), `rename_group`,
  `remove_group` (only an empty group; otherwise kept, saying how many items
  are in it), `new_assembly` (a key — asm1 — name, job type, kind of area,
  `auto` only when Coy asks and only with a kind of area, and its items with
  a quantity only for items counted by hand), `add_to_assembly`,
  `remove_from_assembly` and `load_assembly` (onto an area, through
  `/api/jobs/[id]/budget/assembly`). A `new_item` may take a key (item1) so
  a later step can put it in an assembly. His context lists every assembly
  by ref with what it applies to and its items, and he is told to load one
  that fits before building another. **Price book steps need no job:** on
  the Price book page Dusty is HOT on "Price book & money" (the `cash`
  context, which now carries the whole book and its assemblies — no new
  context kind, so `dusty_conversations` needed no migration), and a plan's
  budget steps there are skipped by name ("needs the job open"). Tested in
  `tests/dusty-plan.test.mjs`. Still Coy's tap; Dusty changes nothing.
  **And a job (Coy, 2026-10-04: "Or I can ask dusty to create it; he should
  have that ability").** `new_job` {contact (full id from his context),
  title, job_type, address, description — only as written or said} → `POST
  /api/jobs` on Coy's Apply (which now also takes `description`); the card
  names the person. In every context (`RECORD_RULES`); tested in
  `tests/dusty-plan.test.mjs`.
  **And a parish's sales tax (Coy, 2026-10-03: "Dusty needs to be able to
  update the sales tax for the parish the address found").** Asked "add a tax
  rate for East Baton Rouge", Dusty said he had no step for it. `set_tax`
  {parish, rate_pct (under 30), for_job, source} — on Coy's Apply it is saved
  through `POST /api/tax-parishes` (Price book → Defaults, noted "From
  Dusty's plan: …") and, with a job open, sets the job's parish
  (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {tax_parish}`). His job and Price book contexts list
  the parish rates on file next to the job's "no rate for <parish>". The rate
  is never assumed: Dusty says where it came from in `source` (Coy's words, or
  "Dusty's figure — check the parish's published rate") and the card says
  **Check the rate before you apply**; Coy's tap is the entry. Looking the
  rate up by address (Louisiana's own lookup) still waits on API access.
- **Units and how a line is counted (Coy, 2026-10-03: "Need to change unit
  types. Sometimes it's days/sqft. I need to be able to add and edit unit
  types from this panel and inside the costbook").** A budget line's sheet
  has **How it's counted** (the card rules — by the day, by the area, once
  per job, typed …; "Measured in the Plan Room" kept for a takeoff line) and
  **Unit**, on that line only (`PATCH …/budget/lines/[id] {qty_rule, unit}`;
  a rule other than typed counts from the area again). The Price book card
  sheet's Unit is the same **unit picker** (`unit-picker.tsx`): the units
  already on the org's items, most used first, then the common ones
  (`src/lib/units.ts`, tested — case never doubles), **+ New unit…** to type
  one (1–20 characters), and **Edit units…** — each unit with how many items
  use it and **Rename**, which changes it on every item and vendor price
  (`GET/PATCH /api/price-book/units`, session client); budgets already made
  keep their copies. Checked in the harness: a line set to by the day, a new
  unit "Truck", LS renamed "Lump sum" on 9 items. **A unit is removed (Coy,
  2026-10-10: "I need the ability to remove units")** in the same list
  (**Rename or remove units…**): Remove asks which unit its items go to and
  moves them there through the same PATCH, so the unit leaves the list — an
  item is never left without a unit.
  **Superseded 2026-10-05 — one Unit type (Coy: "if it's just the unit type
  then it needs to be renamed 'unit type'. And the dropdown list should be
  simple: SqFt, SqYd, LnFt, CuYd, Each, + (to add my own)").** "How it's
  counted" is gone from the line sheet, the new-line form and the Price book
  card: one **Unit type** picker — SqFt · SqYd · LnFt · CuYd · Each, then Coy's
  own units ("Your own": Days, Lump Sum …), then **+ Add my own unit type…**
  (Rename units… only in the Price book). **The unit decides the quantity**
  (`ruleForUnit()`, `src/lib/units.ts`, tested): SqFt / SqYd → the area (SY ÷
  9), LnFt → the edge, or the length of an area measured in LF (`ruleQty()`
  now takes it), CuYd → yards of concrete with the card's waste, Each and
  anything else → typed (or an area's count); of his own, Days → days on the
  job, Lump Sum → once; Sheet / Stick count by the sheet / stick on a card
  (its coverage and spacing). A Plan Room line stays measured. Old names show
  as their type ("Square Feet" is SqFt) and nothing stored was rewritten; a
  line still typed whose unit would count itself says "Quantity typed · ↺
  Count it from the area". **A typed line with no number counts from its area (Coy, 2026-10-09:
  demolition in SqFt on an 894 SF area said "needs quantity").** In `ruleQty()`
  a typed line whose unit is SqFt, SqYd, LnFt or CuYd and has nothing typed
  takes that unit's rule; a number typed still wins, Each still waits for one,
  and the "Quantity typed" button shows only when a number is typed.
- **A budget line's panel, laid out like JobTread's (Coy, 2026-10-07).**
  The line's sheet (`LineSheet`, `job-budget.tsx`) is three boxes: **Item**
  (name, group, cost type, area, description), **Cost and price** — one grid,
  Quantity (ƒ from the rule, ✎ typed) · Unit type · Unit cost · Ext. cost ·
  O.H. · Margin % · Unit price · Ext. price, the worked-out ones shown, not
  typed — with Sales tax under it, and **Vendor**; then Show on the proposal,
  Optional add-on and Take off the budget. The "Quantity: the days on the
  job." line is gone; only "Quantity typed · ↺ Count it from the area" shows,
  when it applies. **The vendor is searched (Coy: "search and add a
  vendor")**: Change lists every vendor (`GET /api/vendors`), those pricing
  the item first with their price; a pick of one pins this line to their
  price (`{source_id}`), of one who doesn't price it yet gives them this
  line's unit cost on the item (`POST /api/cost-items/[id]/sources`, their
  first price becomes the default only when the item has none) and pins it;
  a typed name that isn't a vendor shows **+ Add vendor "…"** (`POST
  /api/vendors`, Coy's tap; a duplicate number or email is still 409). A
  one-off line has no item to hold a vendor price, and says so.
  **Its cost type too (Coy, 2026-10-10: "I had to go back to the cost book to change it"):** the
  Item box's Cost type is a picker; the change is on that line only (`PATCH …/budget/lines/[id]
  {cost_type}`), the Price book item keeps its own, and Cost analysis follows the line.
- **A line's group follows its Price book item (Coy, 2026-10-04: "I have an
  uncategorized cost item and I can't change it inside the budget … if I
  update it in the price book, it doesn't update in the budget").** The group
  is where an item sits, not a price, so it is not a frozen copy: moving an
  item in the Price book moves every budget line of it
  (`syncLineGroups()`, `budget-server.ts`), a line's sheet has **Group**
  (from a Price book item it moves the item too; a one-off line moves
  alone), and lines copied before an item was moved catch up when the budget
  is opened. Sent proposals keep their frozen snapshot.
- **Base, vapor barrier and day-rate equipment** are cards like anything
  else (by the sq ft, by the day); nothing is priced that has no card.
- **Dictating a section (2026-09-28).** Dictate on a section card records up
  to 60 s; `/api/jobs/[id]/sections/[sectionId]/dictate` sends the audio to
  Groq Whisper (`GROQ_API_KEY`, server side — only audio goes to Groq), asks
  Dusty (Hermes) to put the words into fields, and `sanitizeDictation()`
  (`src/lib/dictate.ts`) keeps a number only if it was **said** — digits or
  words, feet-and-inches included — and a work type or reinforcement only if
  it is a real one (a reinforcement card, since step 3b). The card applies it through the normal section PATCH and
  shows "Heard: …" with Undo. Nothing is estimated.

## 4. Overhead, the job minimum, and burn (rewritten 2026-09-30)

Coy's notes on the old Markups & overhead page replaced this section's
off-the-top divisor (11%) and the crew-day floor:

- **Overhead is a % on every job** — *"This is really considered Overhead
  (O.H.). 15% overhead can be applied to every job."* It is in the price
  (§3: cost × (1 + O.H.) ÷ (1 − margin)), not a separate floor.
- **The job minimum.** *"There are no default crew days. And there is no
  default number of people on a crew. There is only a minimum of $ per day
  that people require to show up and work. And therefore there is a
  minimum for a job."* `org_money_settings.day_minimum` × the days Coy sets
  on the job (`jobs.duration_days`, blank until he does) =
  `jobMinimum()` in `src/lib/engine`. The budget footer and the scope rail
  show it and say "under the job minimum" when a tier's total is below it;
  with no days or no day minimum they name what is missing.
- **Burn and pay-yourself live on the Cash page** — *"Our monthly burn rate
  can be calculated in the 'cash' page."* Recurring overhead items
  (`overhead_items`; quarterly ÷ 3, annual ÷ 12, one-time excluded) plus a
  fixed draw = the monthly burn; runway = cash ÷ burn. `pct_profit` comes
  out after a job clears. Both are edited there (`CashSettings`,
  `/api/settings/money`).
- **Production days are still DISCOVERED, not entered** (receipts, daily
  logs) — when they exist they will replace the days Coy types.
- The old columns (`owner_fee_pct`, `estimator_fee_pct`, `tax_reserve_pct`,
  `assumed_billable_days`, markups per type, `sales_tax_pct`, waste and
  short load) stay in `org_cost_settings`, unread. Nothing was dropped.

## 5. Cash-flow dashboard

**Not a bookkeeping tool.** Big-rocks planning — jobs, invoices, draws, the
nut. Precise on big money, deliberately loose on small stuff.

**Three layers on one timeline:** COLLECTED (cash landed), COMMITTED (earned
and owed — the receivables gap), PROJECTED (quoted × close rate). Money in
above the axis, money out below.

**Cash vs accrual is a toggle, not a choice.** The gap between them is "owed
to you."

**Chart structure — settled, do not redesign without cause:**

- ONE continuous ribbon of MONTH bars, wider than the screen, swiped left and
  right across months and years. Current month centered on open.
- The ribbon is always months. No pinch, no grain toggle.
- Tapping a month opens a BAND showing that month's WEEKS — two roads,
  revenue above the line, expense below, net printed per week.
- Tapping a week opens that week's DAYS.
- Depth lives in the band, not the ribbon.
- Weeks first, days on demand. The question a week answers is "was that week
  positive or negative."
- The band sits ABOVE the ribbon so both are on one screen.

**Runway** = cash ÷ monthly burn, shown big. Red under 1 month, orange under 2.

**The green line is cash position** — what's left in the account, running
forward from the last balance check. Bars are flow, the line is level. It
carries its own scale, not the bars'; say so in the UI.

**Starting cash is a manual balance checkpoint (v1).** Later, read-only bank
sync **reconciles the ANCHOR, not transactions** — compare actual against
model-expected, show the gap as drift (untracked burn) as one lump, quietly
correct the anchor. Never import a transaction ledger.

**Known gap:** projection currently spreads pipeline evenly. It needs an
expected start date per quoted job before it means anything. Add the field.
