## 6. Interface rules

- ScopeCrete header is sticky on every page.
- **Threads v2 — contacts → threads → channels (migration 073, applied
  2026-10-03 on Coy's "Apply 073"; Claude Design handoff
  `design_handoff_threads_v2`).** A **thread** is a saved set of contacts;
  the five role buckets are the system threads (worked out from
  `contact_type`, never stored), and Coy makes his own (`threads`,
  `thread_contacts`, RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`, anon nothing; retired with
  `archived_at`). **+ New thread**: a name, then pull its people from **a
  job** (its contact, its customer's people, its crew & suppliers —
  `sc_job_people()`), **a customer** (its people), or **hand-picked**; a job
  or customer thread **follows them** — anyone added later joins
  (`added_by` job-sync / customer-sync, triggers on `job_vendors`, `jobs`,
  `contacts`, `threads`); it never takes anyone out. Being in a thread never
  moves anyone out of Customer or Vendor. APIs `/api/threads/custom`
  (GET; `?job=` / `?customer=` previews who it starts with; POST),
  `…/custom/[id]` (rename, retire), `…/custom/[id]/contacts` (add, take one
  out); `/api/threads?thread=<id>` lists its members, including people with
  no calls or texts yet; `/api/threads/counts` adds `custom`. Rehearsed
  (rolled back): a job thread filled from its job, a supplier put on the job
  joined it, a stranger sees none, anon refused.
  **Level 1** — a 72px rail on a desktop (Customer · Unsorted · Vendor ·
  Personal · Spam, a rule, **Yours** with initials and counts, **+ New**);
  a drawer behind ☰ on a phone (the bucket chips are gone). `?t=` picks the
  thread (`?f=` still works); `threads-ctx.tsx` holds it, the threads Coy
  made and the counts. **Level 2** — the thread's name in its colour, its
  count, **Sort ▾** (Recent → A–Z → By role, remembered per thread on the
  device), a job thread's job line and **+ Add contact** (a floating + on a
  phone). Rows: initials (a dashed # for no name), name, **role tag**
  (Customer · Cust. contact — anyone on a customer other than its primary —
  · Vendor · Sub · Personal · Spam · Unsorted; not repeated in its own
  bucket), age; "Homeowner · 2 numbers · email"; Dusty's line or the last
  message. A tap opens them; **▾** (only while something about who they are
  is undecided) drops down the filing tools as before; swipe, Select and
  Archive are unchanged. **Opening a contact folds the list to 56px of
  initials** (› brings it back; another thread un-folds it).
  **Level 3** — every contact opens here, customers included (no more jump
  to the customer page): name and role, **Customer page ›** / **Vendor page
  ›** / **Contact ›** (their own page, `?person=1`: next steps, jobs, About),
  **Ask Dusty**, ⋯ (File as, Same person as, New scope / the job, Archive);
  "Homeowner · 2 numbers · email · in Example Driveway Repair · last heard 2h
  ago"; then the **channels** — **each phone number its own**, calls,
  voicemails and texts on it in one timeline (`messages.counterparty`
  decides; `buildChannels()` / `inChannel()`, `src/lib/v34/threads.ts`,
  tested), then **Email** (the mirrored emails filed on them) and **Chat**
  (not built — "Not joined · invite"). **All · Calls · Texts** filter a phone
  channel; **Call** dials that number; the composer texts or emails it.
  **Search** (the glass) drops down at the top of the chat, its full width
  (Coy, 2026-10-05: "a simple search bubble … I don't need the 'this
  number, john, whole thread' buttons or the other buttons if the search
  features have intelligence built in") — one box, no scope or type
  buttons: it searches everything with the person (every number and
  channel), and when nothing of theirs matches, the whole thread, saying
  so; a word like "photos", "voicemail" or "pdf" picks the kind
  (`readThreadQuery()`, `src/lib/v34/threads.ts`, tested; types from
  `message_media.kind`) — `/api/threads/search`; a result opens its
  channel at the message. The line under the channel tabs ("Everything on
  this number, oldest first" …) is gone (Coy). **The list's header stays
  put** while the list scrolls, and its own 🔍 searches that list by a word,
  a name or a place — names, numbers, Dusty's lines, their address, their
  customer's and their jobs' addresses and titles (`places` on
  `/api/threads`, searched, never shown), and every word said in their
  calls and texts (`/api/threads/search` on the thread's people).
  **Live (Coy, 2026-10-05: "I sent a test text message … it's not showing up
  in threads … I would like the webhooks to be near instant from quo").**
  Quo was never the delay: Bubba's text reached `quo_events` and `messages`
  2.1 s after Quo stamped it. The Threads list, the open conversation and a
  person's page read once when opened, so a text that landed while they were
  on screen waited for a reload. Now one watcher per tab
  (`src/lib/v34/live-threads.ts`, `useLiveThreads()`) tells them something
  changed and they refetch their own reads (counts included): Supabase
  Realtime on `messages` INSERT — instant since **migration 082** (applied 2026-10-05 on Coy's "Apply 082") put
  `messages` in the `supabase_realtime` publication (RLS decides who hears;
  the payload is never drawn) — and, working without it, a poll of
  `/api/threads/latest` (the newest message id and Dusty's newest filing,
  two one-row reads) every 15 s while the tab is visible and at once when it
  comes back into view. Don't poll `/api/threads` itself: it reads up to
  4,000 messages.
  **On a phone (Coy, 2026-10-05)** the thread's name and the person share one
  row ("‹ Unsorted  (225) 446-2212"), with no role tag (the thread's name says
  it). The channel tabs are one line each, an icon and a short name — ☎ Cell
  (a first number's default name, was Mobile; ✎ renames it Office …), an
  envelope alone for Email, **Notes** (was Internal notes) —
  and + adds a number (was "+ Number"). **AI Chat is hidden (Coy,
  2026-10-05: "a coming soon feature")**: `/api/threads/[id]` still returns
  the chat channel, the page leaves it out. **+ adds, never replaces (Coy,
  same day: "it just replaces the existing one")**: the number on the
  contact row is always a channel, listed or not; adding a second number
  lists the first too (primary when none is); and a number they already have
  is refused with "✎ beside its tab renames it" (`add: true` on `PATCH
  /api/contacts/[id]/phones`) instead of quietly renaming that tab. A ▴ beside ⋯ folds the whole header
  to the name's row (facts, tabs and toolbar hidden; ▾ brings them back),
  remembered on the device (`sc:thread-head-folded`). Only on a touch screen (Coy, 2026-10-07: a mouse never folds it; `(pointer: coarse)`). An open thread covers
  the dashboard header (`.sc-pinned-full`; the footer stays), its tabs'
  icons centred; the toolbar is one row — **All · Calls · Texts** and the
  line filter as drop-downs, Call from ▾ and search beside them — and the
  line a text goes from is picked there only (the composer's own line chip
  is gone). A call card says "● via Quo" (the provider; the line's name on
  hover — "batonrougeconcreters.com" pushed it out of the card).
  **Internal notes is the first tab on the left (Coy, 2026-10-04)** — a
  person still opens on their conversation; the tab's "@ to tag" line is gone
  (the box below says it). **+ Number adds a number (Coy: "should also allow
  me create a new phone number and save it and label it")** — a dialog for
  the number and what it is (Office, Work …), saved on `contact_phones`
  (`PATCH /api/contacts/[id]/phones`) as its own channel; a number that is
  already someone else's thread answers 409 with who, and offers "Join their
  thread to this one" (the Same person as… merge); "Or join another thread of
  theirs…" opens that merge directly. **"US" and "THEM" are "We" and
  "Customer" (Coy: "'US' is confusing … 'Them' … should be replaced by
  'Customer'")** — the background reader is shown transcripts labelled
  US/THEM and its summaries and quotes kept the words; `plainSpeakers()`
  (`src/lib/speakers.ts`, tested; "Vendor" for someone filed vendor) turns them
  round wherever Dusty's status lines, recent summaries, quotes and next-step
  lines are sent out — Threads, the person and customer pages, the activity
  line, Why this job — and in Dusty's own context. Stored text is unchanged.
  **Name a number (Coy, 2026-10-03: "instead of 'phone 2' it can say
  'office'")**: ✎ beside the open phone channel's name saves its label on
  `contact_phones` (`PATCH /api/contacts/[id]/phones`, 30 characters, blank
  = the default; a number they used that isn't listed is added with it).
  **The tab reads the number, then its name (Coy, 2026-10-09: "(225)
  320-4143 Cell")**. The tab's ✎ is gone (Coy, 2026-10-09: "that should be
  edited in the contact information") — a number's name is changed on the
  contact card's Edit.
  **On a phone the header is two rows (Coy, 2026-10-09):** the buttons (fold,
  Notes, the emblem, ⋯), then a breadcrumb in the thread's small capitals —
  "‹ All | Jeremy Wright | Gulfline Paving": the thread back to its list, the
  person to their contact card (`?person=1`), then their customer's page
  (`customer_name` on `/api/people/[id]`; a homeowner's name again) or, for a
  vendor, their company (else their name) to the vendor page. Each link
  says what it opens — "Contact Joey | Vendor Joey" (or Customer …), the word
  in grey, the contact in green, the customer or vendor in the link colour,
  the thread in ink (Coy, 2026-10-09). The desktop keeps the name (✎) on the
  buttons' row. **Stacked since later that day (Coy):** "‹ All" (the thread)
  sits top left on the buttons' row, a little bigger (14px); under the
  buttons "Contact Joey" on its own line, then "Vendor Joey" / "Customer …"
  on the next — three rows, each "Contact | Joey", "Vendor | Joey" (Coy,
  later still). **Later again (Coy, 2026-10-09):** the back link reads "‹ Threads"
  whichever list it returns to; the labels sit in one column with the names
  lined up beside them ("Contact | Joey", "Customer | Icon Technologies"); the
  numbers and email are rounded buttons, the open one tinted; and ⋯ has
  **Text size** (Small · Normal · Large · Extra large, 0.88–1.3× by CSS zoom
  on the conversation and notes, saved per account as `thread_font` —
  `src/lib/v34/text-size.ts`, shared with Dusty's `dusty_font`). **Later still (Coy, 2026-10-09):** the fold button (▴) is gone on every size
  — the header no longer folds (`sc:thread-head-folded` is not read); the search
  is a button on the top row beside Notes on every size (no longer in the
  toolbar), with a little more room between the phone's buttons; and on a
  phone a second tap on the open number's tab drops down **All · Calls ·
  Texts** with their counts (the tab shows ▾ and "· Calls" while filtered) —
  the toolbar's select is the desktop's only. **Then (Coy, same night):** on a
  phone the toolbar row is gone — **Call** (that number, from the line in ⋯) is
  a button on the top row beside search and Notes, and "Which of your lines"
  joins All · Calls · Texts in the number tab's drop-down. The desktop keeps
  its toolbar.
  **One number on a phone (Coy, 2026-10-09: "not enough room on the header
  for two phone numbers").** Only the open number's tab shows (else the
  first number's), beside Email; the open tab's drop-down lists **Their
  numbers** above All · Calls · Texts, a pick opening that number. The
  desktop keeps every number as a tab.
  **The line a call or text goes from is in ⋯ on a phone**
  ("Call or text from", the lines with ✓ on the one in use — Coy: "less
  cluttered"); the toolbar's Call keeps only the dial button there, and the
  desktop keeps its drop-down.
  **The contact's name in the thread's header is 15px on a phone** (19 on a
  desktop; the company beside it 12px — Coy, 2026-10-09: "smaller").
  **The company beside the name (Coy, 2026-10-09: "John Freeling · Icon
  Technologies")** — the header shows the person's company, else their
  customer's name when it is a business (`company` on `/api/people/[id]`).
  **A Threads row is the name and its facts line; the summary is behind ▾
  (Coy, 2026-10-09).** The bucket tag (Customer, Vendor …, Dusty's guess) and the
  job type sit there too, at the top of what ▾ opens, not on the row. On a
  touch screen a small grip at each end of a row says it swipes
  (`.sc-swipe-grip`). Two icons on the right of every row: ▾ drops down
  Dusty's where-it-stands line, the latest conversation and the filing tools
  (orange while who they are is undecided), and the chat icon opens the thread
  (the row's tap still does).
  **Sort** is a drop-down (Recent · A–Z · By role), and a system thread
  shows no description line under its name (Coy: "remove this line").
  **Archived is a tab of its own (Coy, 2026-10-04: "The archived threads
  needs it's own tab in the tab menu below Vendor, that is filterable").** In
  the rail (and the phone drawer) under Vendor, with its count; the list has
  chips by bucket (All · Customer · Unsorted · Vendor · Personal · Spam, each
  with its count), the search box searches the archive, and Sort applies.
  The old "Archived · N" link above the list is gone.
  **A colour per thread (Coy, 2026-10-10: "a settings icon that opens a side panel that allows me to
  change the color of any thread, which will color all the contact boxes" and "the color of the box to
  match the thread … personal is purple").** Every initials box in Threads (the list and the folded
  column) is the colour of the person's bucket — or of the thread Coy made when that one is open —
  instead of a colour per name. ⚙ **Colours** at the foot of the thread rail (and the phone's drawer)
  opens a panel: each thread with twelve swatches and Default. A bucket's colour is its
  `--sc-bucket-*` variable set on the page's root, so its tags everywhere follow; a thread Coy made
  colours its rail cell. Saved on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.thread_colors`, no migration;
  `src/lib/v34/thread-colors.ts`, tested). Other pages keep a colour per name.
  **All is the first tab (Coy, 2026-10-04: "an 'ALL' thread as a catchall
  for anything that isn't archived or labeled spam").** Everyone in every
  bucket, Unsorted included, newest first, each row with its bucket tag;
  spam and the archived are left out (the count is `/api/threads/counts`'s
  `all`); Whole thread search covers the same people (`bucket=all`).
  **Threads opens on All (Coy, 2026-10-05: "it needs to default to the all
  bucket")** on every size, with no `?t=` (`DEFAULT_THREAD`,
  `threads-ctx.tsx`); a retired thread falls back to it too. It was Unsorted
  on a phone and Customer on a desktop.
  **Why it was archived (Coy, 2026-10-04: "When i select to archive a
  thread, i would like to make a note as to why … so that dusty or any
  internal user can see the reason … if the customer contacts me again we
  will have all of that context").** Archive and Delete ask in a small dialog
  (`ArchiveDialog`, `archive-actions.tsx`; it replaced the browser's
  confirm) with an optional **Why?** box — on a thread's ⋯ Archive, bulk
  Archive N, Archive lost · N, and Delete on a vendor, customer or job. The
  reason is saved as the row's `archived_reason` (no migration — nothing reads
  a contact's reason as a fixed word) and, for each person, as an internal
  note "Archived — <reason>" (`archiveRecords()`), so it is in their Internal
  notes tab and Dusty's context for good, even after Undo. Dusty's own archive
  steps give their label as the reason. **When they come back** (a call or
  text after the archive): their row in Threads says "↩ Back · archived
  Oct 3: <reason>", their thread opens with a banner "Back — they got in touch
  after you archived them on … Why: …" (still archived: the same banner with
  Unarchive), the activity line says "<name> is back — archived Oct 3:
  <reason>" for a week, and Dusty's context on them carries "Archived by Coy
  on … : <reason>". Checked in the harness: the reason posted with the
  archive; the banner on a returning person.
  **The archive note stands out (Coy, 2026-10-04).** A note "Archived — …"
  or "Deleted — …" (`archiveNote()`, `src/lib/archive.ts`, tested) is drawn as
  a grey card with a box icon and ARCHIVED (DELETED in red), the date, by
  whom ("Dusty's plan, your tap" when Dusty proposed it) and "Why: …" — in
  Internal notes and in the timeline (`ArchiveNoteCard`).
  **Asking Dusty to archive (Coy: "I want to be able to prompt dusty to
  archive a thread").** His archive step takes a `reason` — Coy's words when
  he gave one, else the plain fact from the context, never a guess — shown on
  the plan card ("Why (saved as an internal note): …") and saved as the
  archive note on Apply. "This thread" on a person's page is the contact in
  his Ids block. Still Coy's tap.
  **The note box is one line, the Save button's height, growing as it fills,
  with a mic beside it (Coy, 2026-10-09)** — the chat's recorder, Groq writes
  it down (`/api/dusty/transcribe`), the words are added to the box.
  **Internal notes (Coy, 2026-10-04: "I need to be able to create internal
  notes. The internal notes tab can also allow me to tag other internal
  members in the Org").** A tab after the person's numbers — **Internal
  notes** (`internal-notes.tsx`): their `contact_notes` with who wrote each,
  and a box where **@** offers the org's members (`sc_org_people` via
  `/api/org`). A tag is written into the note itself as `@[Name](u:<user
  id>)` (`src/lib/mentions.ts`, tested) — no migration; shown as a chip, and
  as plain "@Name" in the timeline and Dusty's context. A tagged member sees
  **"Tagged you on …"** on their activity line for a week, opening the tab
  (`?channel=notes`). Notes are never sent.
  **Whole thread search works in the buckets (Coy: "whole thread button isn't
  clickable").** A system thread has no saved list, so the button was off;
  `/api/threads/search?bucket=customer|vendor|personal|spam|unsorted` now
  works out its people the way Threads files them (400 most recent).
  **Done and lost customers (Coy: "a way to hide completed customer threads
  and archive lost thread").** `/api/threads` gives each person `job_state`
  — open while any job is open, done when every job is complete (or some
  lost), lost when every job is lost. In Customer, done ones are hidden
  behind **Done · N hidden — show** (remembered on the device), and **Archive
  lost · N** archives every all-lost customer in one tap with Undo (Select
  also has a **Lost · N** pick); like any archive, they come back if they
  call or text again.
  Not built: Dusty reading a whole custom thread (his context is still one
  contact), and "Jump to date".
  **Threads made from rules (migration 089, applied 2026-10-07 on Coy's
  "Apply 089"; checked after: `threads.rule` jsonb, the source check takes
  'rule', the shape check in place, RLS and its policy unchanged; Coy, 2026-10-07: "I want ultimate flexibility for the threads
  channels I create … all the emails coming into intake-concrete@agentmail.to
  … todays calls from quo phone number … all my VOIP numbers … bid requests
  sent out to vendors" — "as long as it doesn't break anything else").** + New
  thread's fourth choice, **A rule**: Calls · Texts · Emails; on which lines
  (any when none ticked); into which inbox; Today (midnight Central) · 7 · 30
  days · any time; coming in, sent out or both; with Customers · Vendors ·
  Personal · Unsorted (anyone but spam when none); words that must all
  appear (a text, a call's summary, an email's subject or text). The thread
  is `threads.source='rule'` with the filter in `threads.rule` (jsonb,
  `sanitizeRule()`, `src/lib/rule-threads.ts`, tested) and **no people**:
  `/api/threads/rule?thread=` works it out on each open — `messages` and
  `emails` under RLS, newest first, at most 200 of each read (says so when
  capped), spam left out — and the list pane shows each call, text and
  email (`RuleFeed`, `rule-feed.tsx`), a row opening the person's thread or
  the email in Files, refreshing as texts land. **Edit rule** changes it
  (`PATCH /api/threads/custom/[id] {rule}`). The rail shows no count for one.
  Nothing else changed: job and customer threads' sync triggers act only on
  their own sources, and the people threads read as before (without the
  column they still read; A rule would say it waits for 089). Rehearsed (rolled back): a rule thread inserted; a rule thread with
  no rule, another thread with one, and a non-object rule refused.
- **Threads on a desktop (2026-10-01, Coy):** the five buckets are a second
  icon rail beside the main one (`SubRail`, with counts), **Customer**
  first (the default until 2026-10-05, now All); the list follows `?f=` and says its bucket.
  Every bucket lists **newest first** (Coy, 2026-10-03: "it should be latest
  at the top" — Johnette Wilson had sunk to J under the old A–Z order);
  Customer, Vendor and Personal have an **A–Z ⇅** toggle (a bare number
  last), remembered on the device. The phone keeps the chips.
- **Threads is people, Jobs is work (2026-09-27; reshaped 2026-09-28).**
  Five chips, all on screen, no funnel: **Unsorted · Customer · Vendor ·
  Personal · Spam**. Unsorted is the inbox and opens first; filing someone
  moves them out of it into their bucket (the row leaves at once). A search
  looks in every bucket. Each bucket has one colour — Unsorted hi-viz, Customer
  green, Vendor blue, Personal violet, Spam red — used by its chip AND its tag
  on the row. The tag says how sure: **solid with ✓** Coy confirmed it,
  **outlined · auto** Dusty filed it, **dashed ?** Dusty's guess. A customer
  also shows the job type: the job's own when there is one, otherwise Dusty's
  `job_type_hint` with a "?". **Rows no longer swipe (Coy, 2026-10-17: "I'm still seeing the swipable rows. That needs to be removed")** — a swipe opens the Spaces drawer; the threads and email inbox rows are filed and archived under ▾, ⋯, Select and the email's ☰, and their grips are gone (the Price book's, the budget's and a customer's jobs' swipes stay). Before that, **swipe on a phone:** right opened the four buckets behind the row (Dusty's
  guess marked); left opens **Archive** (Coy, 2026-10-05 — it used to
  accept the guess; Spam left it 2026-10-10, Coy: "Remove the spam button from the left swipe" — it is one of the buckets on the right). Filing shows "Filed as X · Undo" for 5 s; Archive asks
  Why? first (`src/lib/swipe.ts`). **Select** is an icon beside Sort and the
  search in the list's header (Coy, 2026-10-05), not a word on a row of its own.
  **The tag is not repeated (Coy, 2026-09-28):** a row in a bucket's own
  chip shows no bucket tag, and the customer / vendor page shows none (only
  a small "auto" when Dusty filed them); Dusty's guesses on Unsorted rows and
  tags in search results (which cross buckets) stay.
  **The row, collapsed:** name, tag, job type, and Dusty's one sentence on
  where things stand (`contacts.status_summary`), falling back to the last
  message until he has read them. **Expanded:** his short summary of the
  latest conversations (`recent_summary`), the sorting buttons only while they
  are unsorted or auto-filed ("Change bucket" otherwise), the name question
  only for a number with no name, and ONE button through to their page. No
  call / text / email / composer in the row (Coy, 2026-09-28) — engaging on
  every channel belongs on the person's page.
- **Contacts — the book (Coy, 2026-10-05: "a Rolodex or contacts book of
  some kind for pure contact info that can be pulled into any thread and any
  channel and any vendor contact and any customer contact" — "Call it
  contacts").** No second table: the book IS `contacts` (one row per person,
  §2c), their extra numbers `contact_phones` and emails `contact_emails`;
  customer, vendor, personal and spam stay how a person is filed. A copy
  elsewhere would drift (§2b's `relation` vs `contact_type`) and Threads, Quo
  intake and email filing would not see it. No migration.
  **The page** `/dashboard/contacts` (rail after Cash, Toolbox on a phone):
  everyone not merged or archived, spam left out, each with every number and
  email, company, address, their customer and role (Customer · Cust. contact ·
  Vendor · Sub · Personal · Unsorted — `bookRole()`, only a bucket Coy or Dusty
  filed counts), last in touch; search by any word, and 3+ digits find a
  number however typed (`matchesBook()`); role chips with counts; A–Z or
  Recent (kept on the device); a table when wide, rows on a phone; a row opens
  their contact card (vendors too, since 2026-10-22). `src/lib/contacts-book.ts` (tested,
  `tests/contacts-book.test.mjs`), `src/lib/contacts-server.ts`,
  `src/components/v34/contacts.tsx`.
  **+ Contact** (Dusty's + menu → New contact; on the page since 2026-10-10 the ScopeCrete emblem, opening Dusty with one quick answer, **Add contact**, that opens the form — fixed words, no model call, event `sc:dusty-contacts`, as Jobs' emblem; the form fills the screen, `Sheet cover`): someone who
  hasn't called — name, company, any numbers and emails with labels (Mobile,
  Office…), address, and optionally what they are (Unsorted by default;
  customer / vendor / personal filed by `sc_classify_contact`, Coy's tap).
  `POST /api/contacts` writes every number and email into the lists Threads
  and email-intake read (the first is primary and also on the contact row),
  `address_source='coy'`. A number or email already on someone (their row or
  their lists, merges followed — `ownerOf()`) is 409 naming them, never a
  second person. Rehearsed (rolled back) on the live data: the insert and its
  lists pass the checks and no job is made.
  **One contact picker** (`ContactPicker`): search the book, + New contact at
  its foot (seeded with what was typed, made inline, then picked; a duplicate
  offers "Use them"). Used by the customer page's + Add contact, + Add contact
  on a thread Coy made (pick several), a job's Crew & suppliers ("Someone else
  from Contacts", made as a vendor) and an email's filing. **On a customer's Contacts tab (Coy, 2026-10-10)** + New contact is first, right under the search, and opens the full-screen New contact form; the contact made (or the one a duplicate number names) is put on that customer, and closing the form leaves the tab where it was (`onNew` on `ContactPicker`).
  **An email's person (the email-drafts design asks for it).** The email sheet
  has **Person** — Pick from Contacts / Change / Take off — beside Customer and
  Job, and an email filed on a customer with no person goes on its primary
  person (`PATCH /api/emails/[id]`), so it reaches their Email channel. When
  the sender's address isn't on that person: "… isn't on Amber yet. **Add
  it**" → `POST /api/contacts/[id]/emails` (onto `contact_emails`, and the row
  when it has none; someone else's → 409), so the next email from it files
  itself (§2h exact match). Onsite estimators (§2j) read no contacts, so the
  page is not theirs (`redirectFor()`). Not built: importing a phone's or
  Google's address book (later, Coy).
- **Archive and delete, restorable (migration 066, applied 2026-10-03 on
  Coy's "Apply 066").** Coy: *"I have a lot of these calls where nothing was
  said … I need to be able to archive these threads … in bulk on the threads
  home page and inside the thread itself. Also there needs to be a way to
  delete customers and vendors and jobs. Also Dusty needs the ability to do
  all of it for me if I ask."* His answers: **Delete removes and is
  restorable** (calls, texts and files stay on record — §2's never-wiped
  rule); **an archived number comes back when it calls or texts again**,
  like email. `contacts.archived_at/_by/_reason`, `customers.archived_*`,
  `jobs.archived_by`, and `archive_log` (one row per tap: kind, action,
  `via` coy | dusty, and the **stamp** given to every row touched, so Undo
  puts back only rows still carrying it). RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`;
  members insert the log and may set only `undone_at` (Supabase's default
  table grants were revoked first — the rehearsal caught them); anon
  nothing. **Threads:** **Select** → tick rows, **Nothing said · N** ticks
  every thread that is only calls with no summary, voicemail or words
  (`isSilent()`; checked 2026-10-03: none of them has a transcript or
  summary artifact either), **Archive N** with Undo; **Archived · N** lists
  them with **Unarchive**. A thread is out of the lists and counts while
  `archived_at` is later than their last inbound message
  (`isArchivedNow()`, worked out on read — nothing re-writes it). Inside a
  thread: **Archive** by Call · File as · Same as. **Delete** on a vendor's
  page (the contact), a customer's page (the customer, its people and its
  open jobs, `archived_reason='deleted with their customer'`) and a job's
  header (`jobs.archived_at`, which every list already leaves out); each
  confirms, says it is restorable, and shows Undo in a toast that survives
  leaving the page (`ArchiveToast` in the layout). A deleted job or customer
  opened by link shows **Restore**; **Archived & deleted**
  (`/dashboard/archive`) lists every deleted customer and job. The Customers,
  Vendors and person lists leave archived rows out. **Dusty** proposes it
  (`RECORD_RULES`, in every context): his context lists every thread,
  customer and job by full id, threads with nothing said marked NOTHING
  SAID; his plan's `archive` / `restore` steps take full ids only, and the
  card lists the REAL names they touch, read from the database
  (`/api/archive/names`), before Coy taps Apply — Dusty still changes
  nothing himself. APIs: `/api/archive` (GET lists, POST archive/delete),
  `/api/archive/[logId]/undo`, `/api/archive/restore`,
  `/api/threads?view=archived` — session client, org from the session,
  every id checked against it. Tested: `tests/archive.test.mjs`,
  `tests/dusty-plan.test.mjs`. Rehearsed (rolled back): a member archives
  and undoes; can't rewrite the log; a stranger sees no log and can't
  un-archive; anon refused.
- **Bulk edit and delete (Coy, 2026-10-22: "i need to be able to bulk edit / delete vendors,
  customers, jobs and contacts"; no migration).** Vendors, Customers (table and cards), Jobs (the
  Scope Builder list) and Contacts have a tick on each row and a tick-all; any ticked brings one black
  bar (`BulkBar`, `bulk-bar.tsx`): "N selected", their names, Clear, **Edit ▾** and **Delete**. Edit
  sets one field on every ticked record through that record's own PATCH, four at a time, a failure
  kept and named ("Moved 3 of 4 jobs to Won — 1 couldn't be changed: …"; `runEach()` / `bulkLabel()`,
  `src/lib/bulk.ts`, tested): a vendor's **Kind**, **Add a trade**, **Take a trade off**
  (`/api/vendors/[id]`); a customer's **Type** and **Source** (`/api/customers/[id]`); a job's
  **Stage** (each stage's entry status) and **Type** (`/api/jobs/[id]`); a contact's **File as**
  Customer · Vendor · Personal · Spam · Unsorted (`/api/contacts/[id]/bucket`, Coy's tap so it is a
  confirmed filing). Delete is the restorable delete above, one archive_log row with its Why? box and
  Undo; vendors ask whether to archive the contacts too, as one vendor's Delete does. Vendors keep
  Group text and Send a bid request on the bar.
- **Customer page and Vendor page (2026-09-28)** — `/dashboard/messages/[id]`
  renders `src/components/v34/person-view.tsx` for anyone filed vendor (and a
  customer's person with `?person=1`; since 2026-09-30 a customer's person
  otherwise opens the customer page, below); unsorted, personal and spam keep
  the plain conversation. Header: bucket tag
  (✓ / auto), subtype or job type, Dusty's status line, numbers, email,
  address. **Call · Text · Email · Ask Dusty** — Text writes a draft to copy
  into Quo (no send path, below); Email is mailto: and off when no address is
  known; Ask Dusty opens the panel on this person (`sc:dusty-open`). **Next
  steps**: Dusty's items marked with "why" (the line), Coy's own, due dates,
  overdue in red; tick, dismiss, add. Tabs **Activity** (the timeline, filter
  All · Texts · Calls; Email shown off until email is connected; composer;
  Same as…) · **Jobs** (customer: their jobs with stage, type, flags and Crew
  & suppliers, + Start a job; vendor: the jobs they're on, + Put them on a
  job) · **About** (Dusty's latest summary and what they said about the work).
  The job page carries the same Crew & suppliers row. APIs:
  `/api/people/[id]`, `/api/people?bucket=`, `/api/people/[id]/tasks`,
  `/api/tasks/[id]`, `/api/jobs/[id]/vendors` — session client, org from the
  session. Job stages — New
  lead, Consultation, Estimating, Follow up, W.I.P., Complete, Lost — are the
  Jobs board (Scope renamed Jobs), a Kanban, not Threads chips.
- **Jobs board (migration 034, 2026-09-27)** — `/dashboard/scope`, stages in
  `src/lib/jobs/stages.ts` mapped onto `jobs.status` (`consultation` added;
  W.I.P. holds won → scheduled → in_progress, shown as a tag). Phone: stage
  strip + columns that swipe one per screen; desktop: all seven side by side.
  Cards: customer, address, type (residential / commercial / government =
  `public`), source, sections; Move sheet or one-tap next stage; unreviewed
  machine jobs shown with Accept, never counted. **Customer vs source:**
  `contact_id` / `customer_id` is who pays; `lead_source` (website, referral,
  gc_invite, lead_gen, repeat, direct, other) and `referred_by_contact_id` are
  how it reached us — Jeremy's referral: homeowner is customer, Jeremy the
  referrer; LA Contracting's bid invite: LA Contracting is customer.
  `jobs.job_type` is nullable since 038 (Coy, 2026-09-28): the New job sheet
  still requires a type; an automatic job with no hint shows **Type?**.
  **Sort and filter (Coy, 2026-10-04: "a sort button that sorts by date and
  name. And also a filter button that filters for job types and city").**
  **Sort** (Newest · Oldest · Last moved · Name A–Z) and **Filter** — job type
  (with Type?) and city, each with counts, any of each — with the filters in
  use as chips (×, Clear) and "N of M jobs"; remembered on the device
  (`src/lib/jobs/board-view.ts`, tested: a job's city is its own, else read
  from its address after the street).
  **On a phone since 2026-10-09 (Coy):** Sort is the first group in the
  filter panel, and + New job is the ScopeCrete emblem: it opens Dusty on a
  fresh chat where he asks "Do you want to create a new job, or edit an
  existing one?" (fixed words, no model call) — **A new job** opens the New
  job form, **Edit an existing one** lists the jobs to search and opens the
  one picked (`JobAsk`, `dusty-panel.tsx`, event `sc:dusty-jobs`). The
  desktop keeps Sort and + New job on the row.
  **One row (Coy, 2026-10-06, on a phone):** ☰ · JOBS · Sort (↕ Newest
  first …) · Filter (a funnel with a count) · the view · + New job; the chips
  row shows only while a filter is on. A card's Move is a four-way arrow and
  its next stage reads "Consultation »". **A list on a phone:** the icon
  beside + New switches the swiping board to one stage at a time as a list;
  ☰ lists every stage with its count and picks which (in the board it swipes
  there), remembered on the device (`sc:jobs-phone-view`). The desktop keeps
  every column.
  **A table on a desktop (Coy, 2026-10-07).** Board · Table beside + New job
  (`sc:jobs-desk-view` on the device): one row per job — job and its
  description, customer and city, stage (its colour; Suggested when Dusty's),
  type, source, sections, created, how long in its stage, Move / Accept —
  sorting by Job, Created or In stage with the same Sort, the same filters,
  a row opening the job (`JobsTable`, `scope/page.tsx`).
  **Won is its own column (Coy, 2026-10-09: "I need a way to change the status of a project
  that was quoted to active or won … it's not in progress yet").** The board is New lead ·
  Consultation · Estimating · Follow up · **Won** (won "Not started" · scheduled) · **In progress**
  (in_progress, the old W.I.P. key `wip`) · Complete · Lost (`src/lib/jobs/stages.ts`, tested). A
  job moved into any won status (`WON_STATUSES`) gets `jobs.won_at` stamped once, never over a date
  already there; moved back before the yes (`BEFORE_WON`) it is cleared; `{won_on}` on `PATCH
  /api/jobs/[id]` sets the day by hand (noon Central, today or earlier). No migration. The job's
  Summary has **Mark won** under the proposal when a link is live, and once won a **Won** card: the
  day (✎), and the **contract** — the newest final proposal frozen at or before the win, its total
  as the customer saw it, plus signed change orders (094); a version made after the win is named,
  never taken (`contractFor()`, `src/lib/jobs/won.ts`, tested; `contract` on `GET
  /api/jobs/[id]`). Finals can't change, so reading it this way is the lock — nothing is copied.
  The Dashboard tile reads "Won & in progress". Not built yet: a mobilization checklist, invoices.
  **The customer signs it (migration 097, applied 2026-10-10 on Coy's "apply 097" — checked after: the seven columns and the signature check, both guard triggers on documents, anon and members may run sc_accept_proposal and sc_shared_proposal, anon still has no grant on documents, the four live links return a signature key (none signed); Coy: "one of the ways
  that a job is won is a customer approves a proposal, and we need a kind of approval button and
  the ability to do digital signatures on proposals").** The proposal link (`/p/<token>`) has an
  **Approve and sign** bar at its foot: their full name, a signature drawn with a finger or mouse
  (`sign-sheet.tsx`, a PNG data URL), and a tick on `SIGN_CONSENT` ("I agree to sign this proposal
  electronically …", `src/lib/proposal-links.ts`) → `POST /api/p/[token]` →
  `sc_accept_proposal()` (SECURITY DEFINER, anon may run it, like `sc_shared_proposal`; no
  service role). It takes only a live link, refuses anyone signed in to the org ("the customer
  signs from their own phone"), a blank name, no signature or no tick, and answers "already" to
  a second signing. On the link's `documents` row: status `accepted`, `decided_name` /
  `decided_at`, `signature_image`, `signed_at`, a hashed IP, the browser, the consent text, and
  what they signed — `signed_number`, `signed_total`; an `accepted` event. **The job is won**:
  any status before won (or lost) becomes `won`, `won_at` stamped if empty; and an internal note
  on the job's person (else the customer's primary) — "Proposal P-3 signed by … — $9,650.00 on
  … (Central), from the link. The job is won." **A signed link is kept**: members can't change,
  void, turn off or delete it, and only the functions write the signature columns
  (`sc_documents_signed_guard`, `sc_documents_signed_insert`); a newer version's link leaves it be;
  `PATCH /api/proposal-links/[id]` says "a signed link stays on". The signed version is the
  contract outright (`contractFor()` takes `signedLinks`; the Won card says "✓ Signed by … on
  the link"). The customer's page then says "✓ Signed by … — Save as PDF keeps a copy", the
  signature and date on the Sign line; Coy's own view says the customer sees the button. The
  Summary's proposal card, the Preview ("Signed …" in the openings), the activity line
  ("Proposal signed: …"), and Dusty's proposal lines (`withSigned()`) all say who signed.
  Rehearsed (rolled back) on Wilson Driveway's live P-3 link: Coy signed in refused; a blank name
  and a bad signature refused; anon signed it — the job went scoping → won, the note written, one
  event; a second signing "already"; the link's copy carried the signature; a member voiding it
  or forging a signature on another link refused. Before 097 the page shows no button (the link
  reads `signature` as missing). Not built: the customer declining or asking for changes on the
  page, and a signed PDF filed on the job.
  **Scheduling (Coy, 2026-10-09; no migration).** The Summary of a won, scheduled or in-progress
  job has **Schedule**: Start (a date) and Days of work; the last day is counted in work days,
  Mon–Fri (`workEnd()`, `src/lib/jobs/schedule.ts`, tested). `PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {schedule:
  {start, days, confirmed}}` writes `scheduled_start`, `duration_days`, `scheduled_end` and
  `schedule_state` (tentative when set, **Told the customer** → committed, none when cleared); a
  start moves Won → Scheduled and clearing it moves Scheduled → Won (`statusForSchedule()`);
  changing the days alone moves the end. The board's tag reads "Starts Wed, Oct 14". The
  Dashboard's **Coming up** tile: won or scheduled jobs with a start from a week ago to 60 days
  ahead, soonest first ("Gulfline … · Wed, Oct 14, in 5 days · 1 won without a date";
  `comingUp()`); with none, how many won jobs wait for a date. Not built: a calendar view, crews
  and their days, weather.
  **The created date (Coy, 2026-10-04: "The job needs a created date").**
  Each card shows "Created Oct 2" (Central time) beside its tags; the job
  page's customer line ends "· created Oct 2, 2026". The relative time at
  the card's top right is how long it has sat in its stage. The header is 54px + the 38px activity line = `--sc-head` (92px);
  everything under or beside it measures from that variable. The phone footer
  is 58px = `--sc-foot`. A conversation or person page is `.sc-pinned` —
  fixed between the two on a phone — so only its list scrolls and its name bar
  never slides under the header (Coy, 2026-09-28; the old 72px guess left a
  14px gap and a scrolling body).
- **Correcting a person from their page (2026-09-28).** The pencil by the name
  saves Coy's name (`PATCH /api/contacts/[id]/name`, `name_source='human'`) —
  "Gary Davis" on Vance's number came from a contact card Vance texted. **File
  as… / Change bucket** opens the same bucket picker as Threads
  (`src/components/v34/bucket-picker.tsx`) on the plain conversation and the
  customer / vendor page. The plain conversation header is three rows: name
  (wraps to two lines), the facts at full width, then Call · File as · Same
  as · Scope.
- Cash flow is a page (in the Toolbox by default), not a slide-out drawer —
  a side drawer's horizontal swipe fights the ribbon's horizontal swipe.
- **Settings is a page of its own (Coy, 2026-10-10: "It needs its own dedicated full-screen
  page with sub-pages for different categories of settings").** `/dashboard/settings`, opened by
  the header's ☰ (on Settings the ☰ goes back to the page it was opened from); it was a sheet
  under the header. Its sections, one list in `src/lib/v34/settings-sections.ts` (tested), each
  its own address: **Account** (`/account` — You: your name ✎ and phone, which go on the
  proposals you prepare, email, organization and role; Switch company when in two orgs or more;
  Install the app; Sign out) · **Organization** (`/organization` — blocks for the Name, the Logo
  with Show the logo in the header, the Office and radius, and People and permissions: members,
  + Invite, what an onsite estimator may open; §2g, §2j) · **Lines** (`/lines` — the Lines page,
  §2d; `/dashboard/more/lines` sends there) · **Appearance** (`/appearance` — the color scheme) ·
  **Maps** (`/maps` — Google Maps or Mapbox on the Map page, §6 Map page) · **AI** (`/ai` — Dusty's thought line on a phone and on a desktop) · **Menus** (`/menus` — the
  rail's order, the footer's three pages, the icons' colors; the rail's **Menu settings** opens
  it). On a desktop the sections are a column on the left beside the open one (Account when none
  is picked); on a phone `/dashboard/settings` is the list, each section opening its own page with
  **‹ Settings** back to it (`settings-shell.tsx`, `src/components/v34/settings/`). The page has
  the main pages' subheader; it is not on the menus. A vendor's App access opens Organization at
  People with them picked (`sc:open-settings`). An onsite estimator sees Account and Appearance;
  any other section sends them back to Settings (`redirectFor()`). **Give feedback is gone from
  Settings (Coy: "we already have the roadmaps page icon (button) in the main menu")** — the
  Roadmap's own button sends it. Who is signed in reaches the pages from the layout
  (`MeContext`, `src/lib/v34/me-ctx.ts`), so a rename or a logo reloads the header. Pricing
  defaults (margin, O.H., day minimum, parish tax rates — §3, §4) are only in Price book →
  Defaults; pay-yourself and recurring overhead are on the **Cash** page.
- **The header's + is gone too (Coy, 2026-10-01)** — its menu opened under
  Dusty. **Dusty's + (his header) is the create menu:** New chat · New
  customer · New job · Add a receipt or quote. (New invoice is gone until invoices are built — a Planned card on the Roadmap, 2026-10-06.)
  Each opens its own form on the page beside him (`?new=1` / `?receipt=1`,
  or the `sc:create` event when that page is already open); on a phone he
  steps aside. He creates nothing himself — the form is Coy's tap (§6 Dusty:
  read-only toward the business).
- **Sheets open beside Dusty (2026-10-01).** A drawer or modal
  (`.sc-drawer`) opened while Dusty is docked on a desktop sits in the page
  between the rail and his column, never under him.
- **The header's pipeline button is gone (Coy, 2026-09-30)** — the
  Dashboard carries the pipeline. **On a desktop its place, right of 🔍, is
  Dusty's button**: he opens down from the header on the right, and the
  rail has no Dusty item. On a phone he stays in the centre of the footer.
- **Navigation (2026-09-30, Coy).** Pages are listed once in
  `src/lib/v34/pages.ts`: Dashboard · Threads · Scope Builder · Jobs · Map · Cash · Contacts · Customers ·
  Price book · Vendors · Documents · Logs. **Cash is off every menu since
  2026-10-06** (Coy: "remove the cash page from the menu since it's not
  complete but keep what we have on the backend"): `soon: true` in
  `pages.ts`, so `cleanMenus()` drops it from saved menus too; the page and
  its APIs stay at `/dashboard/cash` (pay-yourself and recurring overhead are
  still edited there, by its address), and a Planned **Cash flow** card on the
  Roadmap describes §4–§5. **Phone footer:** the open page's tab has a tinted pill behind its icon,
  its word in the accent and a 3px rule over it (Coy, 2026-10-09). Three pages Coy
  picks (default Dashboard · Threads · Jobs) around **Dusty in the centre**,
  and **Toolbox** last. The Toolbox is a drawer that rises just above the
  footer (not a list over the page), its icons on the footer's own five
  columns, over everything on the page — an open thread too (Coy, 2026-10-09); a page opens between the header and the footer, so Dusty stays
  one tap away. **Desktop rail:** every page, plus a Dusty item, in Coy's
  order. **Settings → Menus** reorders the rail (and the Toolbox) and ticks
  the three footer pages (`src/components/v34/toolbox.tsx`). The **More page
  is gone** — `/dashboard/more` redirects to the Dashboard; Lines (since 2026-10-10 at Settings → Lines, the old address sending there) and Logs
  keep their addresses.
- **Preferences follow the account (migration 053).** `user_preferences`
  (org, user, `prefs` jsonb < 64 KB), RLS org member AND `user_id =
  auth.uid()`, anon revoked (rehearsed: another user's row refused, a
  stranger sees none). `/api/prefs` GET, PATCH `{key, value}` for `menus`,
  `home`, `pricebook_cols` only; `usePref()` (`src/lib/v34/prefs.ts`) mirrors
  to the browser for the first paint and saves 600 ms after a change.
- **The Dashboard (2026-09-30, Coy: "customizable and will evolve")** is where
  the app lands (`/dashboard`, `src/components/v34/home.tsx`,
  `/api/dashboard/home`). Tiles: **calls today** (inbound calls since
  midnight Central), **new leads** (New lead stage; a job Dusty made counts
  only once accepted), **jobs in progress** and **quoted** (count, and $ from
  each job's budget through the engine — `src/lib/job-totals.ts`: the
  accepted option, else the only one, else "from" the lowest; a job with no
  budget is "no price yet", never $0), **work runway** (crew days of W.I.P.
  work ÷ crew days a month), **cash this month** (payments − receipts) and
  **profit this month** (less the monthly overhead; blank while overhead is
  not entered, since it would overstate). A blank tile shows "—" and says
  why. **Edit** shows, hides and orders tiles (saved to the account).
- **Search (migration 054, 2026-09-30).** The header's 🔍 opens a bar
  across the whole header row on every size, above everything including
  Dusty's docked column (drawn into the page body; Coy, 2026-10-05), with
  the results full width under it (`search-panel.tsx`,
  `/api/search`) over **everything**: people and vendors (name, company,
  email, address, service area, Dusty's status lines), messages (text, call
  summary, transcript), call artifacts, Coy's notes, jobs and files (name,
  note, Dusty's read, the document's text). Stored generated `search_tsv`
  columns with GIN indexes; `sc_search(q, per_kind)` is SECURITY INVOKER
  (RLS decides), execute for `authenticated` only; websearch syntax; 4+
  digits also match phone numbers. Rehearsed: a stranger gets nothing.
- **Files — one place, one name (migration 074, applied 2026-10-03).** Coy:
  *"documents really needs to be renamed 'files' across the board for
  continuity even in the database. The idea is there is one place for
  documents and files and there is a badass search filter system to get
  anything anytime … Jobtread has documents and files. I don't think it's
  needed to have both."* The table every upload lives in is **`files`**
  (was `job_files` — it holds people's, customers' and emails' files too);
  rows, policies, triggers and keys followed the rename, and
  `sc_files_claim`, `sc_plan_sheet_same_job`, `sc_search` and `sc_undo_bid`
  were re-pointed. **`job_files` remains as a view of `files`**
  (security_invoker, anon none) only for the deployed `email-intake` and
  `bid-intake`, whose repo source still names it (`read-files` moved to
  `files` with v16, 2026-10-10); each
  moves to `files` at its next redeploy, then the view goes. The storage
  bucket keeps its id `job-files` (bucket ids can't be renamed; nobody sees
  it). The page is **Files** at `/dashboard/files` (`/dashboard/documents`
  redirects, `?file=` and all), the menu key is `files` (a saved menu's
  `documents` maps over), its search is `/api/files/search` (was
  `/api/documents`), and every label says Files. The customer page's
  Documents tab was folded into its Files tab the same day. Not renamed:
  `documents` / `document_events` (sent proposals and their openings since
  migration 075 — not files) and
  `doc_templates` (the proposal's warranty and contract text). Rehearsed
  (rolled back): 18 files before and after, through the view too; the
  service role inserts and updates through the view; a member sees all, a
  stranger none, anon nothing; search still finds files.
  **The filters (2026-10-03, Coy: "start with the search items you
  mentioned, including just whatever name I type in").** The search box finds
  the words in a file (name, note, Dusty's read, its text) **and any name
  typed** — a person, vendor, customer (with its people and jobs) or job
  whose name holds it brings every file filed on them, each row saying
  "Matched: filed on Angelle Concrete Group". Then **Type** (photos · PDFs ·
  other), **Way in** (uploaded · email · texted; reads **Input type** since 2026-10-10 — Coy), **Job**, **Customer**,
  **Vendor**, **Person** (each listing only those with files, with counts),
  **When** (last 7 / 30 days, this year, or between two dates), and the
  category chips as before. Every filter in use is a chip with ×, plus Clear
  all; the whole filter lives in the page's address (`src/lib/files-filter.ts`,
  tested), so a filtered view can be linked. `/api/files/search` takes
  `q kind source job customer contact vendor since from to` and `facets=1`
  for the lists — session client, a typed name stripped of wildcards before
  it reaches a pattern.
  **Laid out like OneDrive (Coy, 2026-10-05: "toggle between photos and files … a left column as a type of file menu for folders … a search bar inside the file page … a filter bar … show the amount of remaining storage").** **Photos | Files** at the top: Photos is the filter's `kind=image`, drawn as a grid of thumbnails by month (Central time); Files is everything, photos included, as a list (a table — Name · Filed on · Size · Added — when there is room). The **folder column** on the left (≥ 900px measured; a drawer behind ☰ on a phone): All files, Recent (last 30 days), each kind (Plans, Specs, Bid invites, Vendor quotes, Receipts, Contracts, Photos, Proposals, Emails, Other, Unfiled), then Jobs · Customers · Vendors · People, each opening to every one with files and its count. Folders aren't stored: each sets the one filter's where to look (category, job, customer, vendor, person or since) and clears the other places, keeping the words, type, way in and dates (`folderPatch()` / `activeFolder()`, `src/lib/files-view.ts`, tested), so the address still carries the view. The search bar sits at the top of the content; the filters are one bar of drop-downs with the ones in use as chips (×, Clear all). **Since 2026-10-06 (Coy, on a phone: "the filter bar here in files is completely useless. It needs to be an icon that opens a column list that opens sub options to the right"):** the title row is ☰ (phone) · FILES with an ⓘ at its corner (the three-line description behind it) · the filter icon · the search icon; the filter icon (a count on it when any is in use) opens a panel — Type, Way in, Job, Customer, Vendor, Person, When down the left, each with what is picked, and the one tapped lists its choices on the right with ✓ (`FilterMenu`, `files-page.tsx`; Between dates… shows From / To there). The chips under it stay. **On a phone since 2026-10-09 (Coy)** the folders drawer slides in from the left, where its ☰ is (`Drawer left`) — **since 2026-10-22 (Coy) it drops down under the Files header instead, the screen's full width, over the talk bar, with × at its top right (`Drawer under`, the subheader's bottom measured as it opens), and its People group reads Contacts**, and the filter is a drawer from the right that fits the screen (`FilterMenu sheet`); the desktop keeps the drop-down. **Preview** is an icon beside the filter in the header, and the open folder's name ("All files") sits on the left with Photos | Files on the right, smaller on a phone so it fits. **Storage** under the folders: "37.4 MB used of 1 GB · 987 MB left" with a bar, amber from 80%, red when full. `GET /api/files/storage` (session client) adds up `files.byte_size` for files not deleted and `message_media.byte_size` for texted files once stored, against `STORAGE_LIMIT_GB` (server side, default 1, Supabase's free plan). Call recordings, logos and proposal pictures aren't counted: nothing records their size. On a customer's Files tab the column stays, its customer stays pinned, and the folder lists are that customer's alone.
- **Documents (2026-09-30)** — `/dashboard/documents`
  (`src/components/v34/documents.tsx`, `/api/documents`): every uploaded file,
  newest first, searched through the same index, filtered by category (or
  Unfiled), job, person and date; tap for the files viewer, with links to its
  job and person. `?file=` opens one (search results link here). **Final
  proposals** are listed above the files (chip Proposals; number, job,
  customer, total — `job_proposals` with `status='final'`, read from their
  snapshots), each opening that version. **Emails** (migration 061, §2h):
  the mirrored inbox, unfiled first, each opening a sheet to file it.
  Texted photos stay on the person's page.
- Legend collapses to a chip.
- **Desktop (lg+, 2026-09-27)** takes its LAYOUT from the Claude Design desktop
  screens and its LOOK from v34 (Barlow Condensed / IBM Plex, hi-viz orange) —
  one design system, not two. The footer becomes a left rail. **The desktop
  header (2026-09-30, Coy):** logo │ Dusty's activity line on the same row,
  then 🔍 · pipeline · + · ☰ — no phone number, no "unnamed", no drift
  counter (drift is on Logs). `--sc-head` is 54px on a desktop (the line
  lives in the header) and 92px on a phone (the line is the row beneath).
  Threads
  is list · conversation · contact rail. **No dead margins (2026-09-29):** the
  customer / vendor page fills its pane — conversation on the left at full
  height, Next steps · Jobs · About in a 340px column on the right (the
  design's 1d) — whenever the pane is at least 880px wide, measured
  (`src/lib/v34/use-wide.ts`), not by breakpoint; narrower (a phone, or Dusty
  docked) it is the single column with tabs. The plain conversation's contact
  rail follows the same rule at 920px and carries Dusty's status line. The desktop design calls the list
  "Triage"; that word is not used — it is **Threads**, where every channel for
  a contact merges into one conversation.
- **Colour themes (2026-09-30, Claude Design handoff,
  `design/customer-and-themes/themes`).** Settings → **Appearance**:
  **Site Office** (default), **Blueprint**, **Kraft Yard**, **Night Shift**
  (dark), and since 2026-10-02 (themes v2, Claude Design) **Steel**,
  **Signal**, **Plex Office** and **Kraft (clean)**. Since 2026-10-10 (Claude Design "Studio", Coy: "add this new theme to appearances") **Studio**, second in the list: cool slate ground, white header with no grid paper and one hairline (`paperEdge` / `paperRule` → `--sc-paper-edge` / `--sc-paper-rule`, which every other theme leaves at the pencil line, so their doubled rule is unchanged), white rail, one steel-blue accent. **Floodlight** (Claude Design, same day), after Night Shift: a second dark theme, easier to read — blue-slate ground, near-white text, an amber work-light accent, the header one hairline like Studio, its text faces Inter where Night Shift has Kalam. Site Office stays the default. **A theme carries its
  typefaces too** (`Theme.font`: display, body, strong, light, hand →
  `--sc-font-*`; the `disp` / `body` / … tokens read those variables, and the
  dashboard's base text is the theme's body face — IBM Plex Sans in Site
  Office, where it was Outfit; Barlow, Archivo, Atkinson Hyperlegible in the
  new ones). The wordmark keeps its own fixed letters, and chat bubbles keep
  Coy's muted colours (`DA` in `conversation.tsx`) rather than the handoff's
  `outBg`. `src/lib/v34/themes.ts` holds them; `v34-shell.css` carries each as
  `--sc-*` variables under `html[data-theme]` (generated from `themes.ts`);
  an inline script in `app/layout.tsx` sets `data-theme` from
  `localStorage` before first paint, and `useTheme()`
  (`theme-picker.tsx`) saves the choice to the account (`usePref("theme")`,
  migration 053). **Every `T.x` is a CSS variable**, so every screen follows
  with no branching; the *Dim fills the themes don't name are mixed from
  their colour and the card; `T.dark` stays dark (job money panels, white
  text) in every theme. Rules that keep dark themes readable: no hard-coded
  white backgrounds (`T.card`), text on the accent is `T.onHiviz` (dark in
  Night Shift), SVG icons draw in `currentColor`. The rail, paper header,
  wordmark (`--sc-hatch`, `--sc-guide`, `--sc-crete`) and chat bubbles
  (`T.sunk` / `T.outBg`) follow the theme.
- **A contact's page is a contact card (Coy, 2026-10-06, from his phone: "just a simple contact page with all the contact information and no threads or nothing else and that's where we'll change contact information").** `?person=1` (Contact › on a thread) draws `contact-card.tsx`, laid out like a phone's contacts app: ‹ Back to the thread, Edit; the coloured initials (`initialsHue()`), the name, who they are (bucket · subtype · company, Customer page › when they have one); **Call · Text · Email** (Text opens their thread, where Send goes through Quo); then **Contact info** — each number with its label (Mobile by default) and a text icon, each email, the address (opens Maps). **Edit** turns the same card into the form: the name, each number's label, **+ Add a number** (the old + Number dialog, with its "join another thread of theirs"), an email box, and the address with the Mapbox street finisher and Remove — through `/api/contacts/[id]/name`, `/phones`, `/emails`, `/address`. `/api/people/[id]` adds `phone_list` and `email_list` (labels, main first). **Since 2026-10-10 (Coy: "When I click edit contact from the thread it should open the contacts page already with editor open. And I need to be able to edit the phone number")** the thread's ⋯ Edit contact opens the card already in Edit (`?person=1&edit=1`; the breadcrumb's Contact still opens it to read), and in Edit each number is a box of its own above its label: a changed number goes through `PATCH /api/contacts/[id]/phones {phone, new_phone, label}`, which rewrites that row of `contact_phones` and `contacts.phone` when it is the main number. Refused, by name, when the new number is someone else's thread (409), already theirs, or one of the org's lines. The calls and texts already on the old number keep it (`messages.counterparty`), so the thread still shows them under the old number's tab; a call from the old number after the change would start a new Unsorted thread. Removing a number or email is not built. A vendor's `?person=1` is their contact card too, with Vendor page › beside who they are (Coy, 2026-10-09: the breadcrumb's contact and vendor links both opened the vendor page); Edit contact and the breadcrumb's Contact go there. **Since 2026-10-09 (Coy, from his phone):** its ‹ Back goes back to the page
  it was opened from (the browser's history; the thread when opened by a link);
  the line under the name gives their customer's own name ("Customer · Art
  Apartments") when it isn't the person's; a **Customer** row in Contact info
  names it, opens it, and **Change** lists every customer to search and pick —
  the pick puts them under it through + Add contact's own `sc_attach_contact`
  (their jobs follow; no Undo line since 2026-10-10 — Coy: picking again is the undo); and a
  **Notes** box under it is the thread's Internal notes, typed or dictated with
  the mic. **Scan a business card** heads the New contact form (Contacts → +
  Contact, Dusty's New contact, the pickers): the camera's photo is shrunk to
  1,600px JPEG in the browser and `POST /api/contacts/scan` sends it to the
  model API in one tool-less call (`modelJson()` now takes images; needs
  `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`); `readCard()`
  (`src/lib/business-card.ts`, tested) keeps a number only with 10 or 11
  digits (no fax), an email only with its shape, a state of two letters, a
  5-digit zip; nothing is stored — the form is filled for Coy to check and
  Add contact, which keeps its duplicate check.
  **Nothing widens a page sideways on a phone (2026-10-09):** Coy's contact card showed the dashboard
  header ending two-thirds across — something sticking out past the right edge (the toolbar's comment
  pins at least) let the layout grow wider than the screen, and the fixed card grew with it to its 560px.
  `html { overflow-x: hidden }` below lg (on html only, so it moves to the viewport and sticky headers
  still stick).
  **A vendor on a job opens (2026-10-09):** Luis's card failed with "more than one relationship was
  found for 'jobs' and 'contacts'" — `jobs` has two keys to `contacts` (`contact_id`,
  `referred_by_contact_id`), and the jobs a vendor is on were read with a bare `contacts(...)` embed,
  run only for someone in `job_vendors` (Luis alone that day). It names
  `contacts!jobs_contact_id_fkey` now; any embed between those two tables must name its key. **The same for `jobs` and `customers` since migration 109** (`customer_id`, `owner_customer_id`): the Scope Builder's `customers(name)` failed on master with "more than one relationship was found" (2026-10-13) and names `customers!jobs_customer_id_fkey` now. A card
  that can't load says why on its page instead of falling back to the thread.
  **Since 2026-10-10 (Coy):** a **Map** button beside Call · Text · Email (off with no address) opens the
  address on the Map page (`/dashboard/map?addr=…`, looked up by `/api/aerial/suggest?find=` and pinned
  at zoom 18 instead of the office's ring); in Edit an address on file shows with **Change** and Remove,
  the street fields only after Change.
  The record-page contact layout below (Overview · Next steps · Jobs …) is no longer drawn for a contact (`PersonView` kept, unused).
  **On the thread (same day):** the **+** after the channel tabs is gone (numbers are added on the contact card); **Notes** is no longer a tab but the first item in the ⋯ menu on the name's row ("Notes (3)"), opening the same Internal notes; and "(225) … was merged into this thread · Undo" left the conversation for the top of those notes (MERGED, with Undo).
  **Later that day (Coy, on a desktop):** a call card no longer shows next steps — not under the card, not in its details (*"it clutters the information card … The next steps can be in the dusty chat window"*; Quo's next steps stay in `call_artifacts` and in Dusty's context). **Ask Dusty is the ScopeCrete emblem** (*"to represent the scopecrete AI. It will open up the AI chat panel with dusty, and will pull the latest brief summary from this thread and give the next steps"*): the emblem beside Customer page › (since 2026-10-09 its own button beside ⋯ on every size — Coy: "Remove the where it stands button from mobile. Put the emblem in its own button next to the hamburger menu") opens Dusty on the person and asks, on that tap, for a brief summary of where things stand and the next steps (`askDustyBrief()`, `src/lib/v34/dusty-brief.ts`; `sc:dusty-draft` with `send: true`, sent once their chat has loaded). The customer page's Timeline call cards dropped next steps too.
- **A thread's header, and Threads on the customer page (Coy, 2026-10-07, desktop toolbar notes).**
  The thread's header is the name, the emblem (Dusty's brief), a **Notes** icon with its count (it

  holds **Edit contact** (the contact card, `?person=1`), **Customer page ›** / **Vendor page ›**
  (no longer a button on the row), **Move thread** (was File as… / Change bucket), New scope while

  opens from the customer page). **Merge** is a button on the contact card beside Edit: another
  contact's calls, texts and numbers fold into this one's thread (the same `sc_merge_contacts`,
  undoable, §2c); the "Also Amber — same person?" suggestion still opens it from the thread.
  The thread view itself is `src/components/v34/thread-view.tsx` (`ThreadView`), so the
  **customer page's Threads tab** is that view for the customer's own people: their contacts down
  the left (initials, Primary or role, last heard; chips across the top when narrow) with the
  threads they're in, and the picked one's whole thread — every number, email, notes, the
  composer — beside it (`embedded`: not pinned, no link back to the list or to the customer page).
  It replaced the per-person summary cards of 2026-10-05; Dusty's lines stay on the Overview.
  **Since 2026-10-07 (Coy)** the column lists only the contacts ("Threads they're in" is gone from
  it; the Overview keeps its chips), and the thread is measured to fill the screen from where it
  starts, so its text box sits at the bottom without scrolling.
  Files (the page) is full width like the other lists, its subheader too.
  **Files' preview (Coy, 2026-10-07: "open inside the files page with the actual files much like desktop explorer does").** **A sent proposal opens there too (Coy, 2026-10-10)** — a final in the Proposals list opens in the preview (the phone's full screen) as the document was sent, read from its snapshot (`GET /api/proposals/final/[id]`, `final-preview.tsx`; area maps and photos left to the proposal page), with **Open the proposal page ›**.
  A file or photo opens in a **preview panel** inside the page, not a sheet over the screen: on a desktop a column right of the
  files, under the subheader and no taller than the folder column, its width dragged by its left edge (300–1000px,
  double-click resets, kept on the device `sc:files-preview-w`); **Preview** (an icon beside the filter since 2026-10-09) shows or hides it
  (`sc:files-preview`). A file's links read **Open job: Wilson Driveway ›** and **Customer page:
  Icon Technologies ›** (its own customer, else its person's; a person with no
  customer reads "Contact: …"), the names from `/api/files/search` (2026-10-09).
  On a phone a round button at the bottom right of the preview shows the
  document **full screen**, with a small × in the corner to come back. On a phone it is the whole screen with "‹ Files › folder › name" across the top, each part back to
  the list. **Every file is seen in the app (Coy, 2026-10-08: "I need a way to actually view the file
  inside the APP … PDF's, word documents, .md files")** — `FilePreview` (`file-preview.tsx`), in the
  panel and in the sheet: a PDF's pages drawn with pdf.js as they scroll near (60 at most; the
  browser's own viewer downloaded instead on a phone), a Word .docx as its paragraphs, headings, lists
  and tables, Markdown rendered, other text (.txt, .csv, .json) as it is (`src/lib/doc-text.ts`,
  tested; an old .doc, a spreadsheet or a video says it can't be shown). "Open the file" is gone:
  a small **Download** sits beside Delete file under the details; "What it is" reads **File type**. `FileDetails` / `fileSub()` in `files-panel.tsx`; other pages keep the sheet
  (`FileViewer`). **The preview pane is the file, not a form (Coy, 2026-10-08):** the name at its
  top is where it is renamed (tap, type, Enter or leave — `patchFile()`); the pane has no Name,
  File type or Note fields, and its line under the name is the size and pages only. The kind is
  in the **file list**: a Type column on a desktop, a small picker on each row on a phone, changed
  there (`PATCH /api/files/[id] {category}`). The sheet on other pages keeps all three fields.
- **Contact, customer and vendor pages look the same (Coy, 2026-10-05: "This 'contact' page is a mess … I would rather have consistent formatting across contact, customer and vendor … Really a contact needs to become a customer or vendor or spam anyway").** The customer page's parts moved into one module, `record-page.tsx`: ‹ back · name and role tag · the button row (folded into ⋯ on a phone) · the facts line · one row of tabs, then the Overview with a 400px right column (Location, the facts, Recent), contact cards with a line per number and email and Open in Threads ›, thread chips, panel titles and empty states. The customer page, a contact's own page (`?person=1`, `person-view.tsx`) and the vendor page (`/dashboard/vendors/[id]`) all use it. **A contact:** ‹ Back to the thread, the name with its pencil, the role tag (tap to change the bucket), Customer page › when they have one, Edit (name and address), New job, Ask Dusty; under ⋯ Call notes, File as… / Change bucket, Same person as…, Archive. Tabs Overview · Next steps · Jobs · Threads · Timeline · Files. While they are unsorted or filed by Dusty, the four buckets sit right under the header (Dusty's guess dashed, or Keep / Not X), since filing them is what the page is for. The Overview has Dusty's read, their card, next steps, jobs and the threads they're in. Files is the Files page pinned to them, with + Add files. **A vendor:** the same page, with Prices (★ = the price budgets use; their cards under it) and Receipts & quotes after Overview, + Receipt or quote as the main button, Edit as the vendor form, and Delete vendor under ⋯; its Overview has no Where it stands or Next steps (Coy, 2026-10-18: "Let's remove these next steps block"). `?person=1` for a vendor goes to their vendor page, which is their one page now; the vendor page's back link returns to the thread when opened from one. No API or data change.
  **The customer page's Threads tab (Coy, 2026-10-05: "supposed to be more useful … pull information from the thread into here. maybe a summary").** A card per person on the customer: Dusty's line on where it stands and on the latest conversations, their last three calls and texts (summary or words), last heard, Open in Threads › and "Every call and text in Timeline ›"; then the threads they're in. The "Not in any thread you made…" line is gone.
- **The record page's tabs on a phone, and the Location box (Coy, 2026-10-06).** *"the option to see the list of these tabs … as a hamburger menu that slides open from the left on mobile … right to the left of the customer name."* On a phone `RecordHeader` puts the ☰ (`MenuButton`) left of the name; it opens the tabs as a list in a `SlideDrawer` (`TabDrawer`, `record-page.tsx`) — customer, contact and vendor pages alike. Later that day (*"this 8 tabs row can be removed from mobile"*, *"this information below the name should be on the overview page"*): on a phone the row of tabs is gone (the current tab's name sits right of ‹ Back, ▾ opening the same drawer) and the facts line ("Residential · since … · 2 contacts …") is the first line of the Overview (`OverviewGrid`'s `meta`); a desktop keeps both in the header. The ☰ panel opens **under the page's own header** (Coy: "let this slide out panel sit below the … page header"), measured when it opens, or under the dashboard header once the page's has scrolled away. The customer's **Location** box has no title or explanation line: the address as a Google Maps link (no Copy — Coy), then a satellite picture of the address from the Plan Room's own tiles (`site-views.tsx`; `/api/aerial/tile`, Mapbox only since 2026-10-07 — Esri's tiles came back grey; the point is the customer's stored lat/lng, else looked up by `GET /api/aerial/suggest?find=`, not stored) that opens full size on a tap (zoom 16–20, Esc or × closes); "check it" shows only when the address came from their words or a call. The picture carries no "Satellite" label (Coy, 2026-10-10). **Street view is gone** until a Google Maps key is set up (Coy: "a feature coming soon on the roadmap") — a Planned card on the Roadmap. **Street view is back (Coy, 2026-10-13: "google street view on the customer and job pages"):** under the satellite picture on the customer's Location, and under the address on a job's Summary, `StreetView` (`site-views.tsx`) — with `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_EMBED_KEY` (Maps Embed API, free, restricted to the app's domains) Google's Street View in the page at the address's point (the customer's stored one, else looked up by Mapbox) with Open in Google Maps ↗; without the key, a **Street view ↗** link to Google Maps (no key, no cost). Never the keyless embed. The old Google map embed is gone. **One box with a toggle (Coy, same day: "street maps should be a toggle"):** the customer's Location and the job Summary show **Satellite | Street view** over one picture (`SiteViews`), the pick kept on the device (`sc:site-view`); without the embed key Street view is a link to Google Maps. With `GOOGLE_MAPS_GEOCODE_KEY` (server-side, Geocoding API only) an address is looked up by Google first, else Mapbox.

  Back to the thread" when opened from Threads (else "‹ Customers"); name
  and role tag; **Edit · New job · Ask Dusty** (Call notes and Delete under

  Invoiced/Paid/Open from the budget and payments, the newest four across
  channels) · **Threads** (every contact's calls and texts in one timeline,

  counts; the green phone button by each contact is the only Call control;
  call cards: Dusty's summary → (next steps gone 2026-10-06, below) → recording → transcript on tap →
  **notes on that call** (`POST /api/messages/[id]/notes`); the message box

  Open in Maps; the map and street view only with

  customer: its facts, every contact's block and their latest messages.

  `#6A55B0` · Spam `#B5412C` · Unsorted = accent. All of it is `T` in

  and numbers, Inter panel titles) wait for Coy's feedback.
- **Since 2026-10-10 Dusty is drawn as the ScopeCrete emblem (§2g); what follows is the Lander he was.** **Dusty is the Scopecrete Lander (2026-10-04, Claude Design package,
  `design/dusty-lander/`; was the pixel foreman of 2026-09-30).** A hatched
  concrete block on steel legs, green-light eyes, a dish antenna and an
  orange ring. He lives in two places only: the **centre of the phone
  footer** (the header button on a desktop) and the **chat**.
  `src/components/v34/dusty-avatar.tsx` draws him inline from `dustySvg()`
  (`src/lib/v34/dusty-svg.ts`, a typed port of the package's `dusty.js`;
  ids from `useId()` so server and browser agree; its reduced-motion rule
  scoped to `svg[data-dusty]`); the drawings are also in
  `public/dusty/lander/` (240px, `-24` with heavier strokes, a 512px PNG).
  States come from real events only: **idle** (still at 24px and under),
  **loading** → listen (a chat loading, a voice note), **calculating** →
  sort (a reply streaming, the reader reading), **thinking** → scan,
  **done** → celebrate once then idle, **error** → alert (amber eyes) for a
  moment then idle; the package's own land and sleep exist, unused yet.
  `prefers-reduced-motion` stills him. In the chat: 96px when empty, 32px in
  the message bar once a chat is going. **The favicon and app icon are the
  ScopeCrete emblem** (Coy, 2026-10-05: "the emblem used as the favicon for
  the landing page, login page, and all the dashboards") — still, from his
  emblem pack: `public/favicon.ico` / `favicon.svg` / `apple-touch-icon.png`
  / `icon-192`·`512.png` and `site.webmanifest`, set once in
  `app/layout.tsx`. The pixel sprites stay in `public/dusty/` unused.

  footer's centre Dusty is 40px in a 54px disc that rises above the 58px bar
  (`.sc-tab-dusty`), larger than the page icons beside him. On a phone the
  chat covers the whole screen, the header included (`.sc-dusty-sheet` top 0,

  header has no "What's new?" button (the catch-up code is gone; saved
  catch-ups still open from history); "Looking at:" is 👀, and a chat's label
  drops its "scope:" prefix. **Text size:** "Aa" in the chat header (Small ·
  Normal · Large · Extra large, 0.88–1.3× via CSS `zoom` on the conversation),

  bar's row, at its left (68px, twice the old 36 — Coy: "offset to the
  left … twice as big"), always there while the chat is open, in his
  live state — out of the bar since 2026-10-09 (Coy), and since 2026-10-10 inside the chat just above the bar, at its left, the conversation leaving room under its last line ("live inside the actual chat window, right above where it is"), so the text box is wider
  (up to 900px) and grows with what is typed, up to ~8 lines; the 96px

  … Hey Coy … something subtle to greet and to maybe make me smile").** An
  empty chat no longer says "What are we working on?", what he can and can't
