- **Hide the thought line (Coy, 2026-10-05: "it can be distracting").**
  Settings → **AI** (was Dusty (AI)) → **Show Dusty's thought line**, **On a phone**
  and **On a desktop** apart (Coy, same day: "off on mobile and on on
  desktop") — `activity_line_phone` and `activity_line`, default on; the
  phone's follows the desktop's until it is set. Off on a phone,
  `html[data-activity-phone="off"]` makes `--sc-head` 54px, set before first
  paint by `app/layout.tsx` from the `sc:prefs` copy. Reminders, "is back", tags,
  proposal openings, bid notices and the reader-down warning only show on
  that line, so they go with it.
- **Dusty draws (Coy, 2026-10-21: "How do we give dusty the ability to create artifacts like simple
  drawings or renderings?"; no migration).** Asked for a drawing, sketch, layout, plan view, section or
  detail, Dusty writes one SVG inside a ```` ```drawing ```` block (his prompt's Drawings rule: a viewBox
  about 800 wide, a title, to scale from the CONTEXT's sizes with the scale noted, every dimension in feet
  and inches, a size nobody gave "not recorded" or "est.", black on white). The chat never puts his text
  on the page: `parseDrawing()` (`src/lib/dusty/drawing.ts`, tested in `tests/dusty-drawing.test.mjs`)
  reads it into a tree of svg · g · rect · line · polyline · polygon · path · circle · ellipse · text ·
  tspan with a fixed list of attributes, each value checked (numbers, path letters, a hex or plain colour,
  translate / rotate / scale) — no script, style, class, link, image, foreignObject, defs, markers or
  url(), a handler or anything else left out and counted — and `DustyDrawing` (`dusty-drawing.tsx`, from
  `markdown.tsx`'s new `drawing` block) draws it with React elements on white paper in every theme, with
  its title and **SVG ↓** / **PNG ↓** (1,600 px, drawn in the browser from the cleaned SVG). While the
  block is still streaming it says "Drawing it…". Share leaves the code out. The Artifacts column lists
  them under Drawings. **Save to Files (Coy, 2026-10-22: "create the artifact and save it as a file in my
  Org")** saves the PNG through `POST /api/files` onto what the chat is about — the job, the person, the
  customer — else unfiled (`from_dusty`), the same target as an attachment (`setDustyFileTarget()` in
  `dusty-live.ts`), then "Saved ✓ Open" in Files. **A page to print** — a cover page, a flyer, a sign —
  is a drawing on a letter page (viewBox 0 0 816 1056) with the business's name, bands of colour and the
  job, customer and date; it can't carry a photo or the logo (no images in a drawing), which is the
  proposal's own cover's job (the proposal builder redesign, after Roof Squad's SquadQuote cover). Not
  built: putting a saved drawing on a proposal, and photo-like renderings — Dusty's model makes no images;
  that needs an image model from another provider, Coy's call.
- **A long question folds (Coy, 2026-10-22: "only show 1 or 2 lines in the chat panel with an expand arrow down … similar to how Claude does it").** A question over ~180 characters or three lines (the prompts the emblems write for a proposal section or a scope of work) shows its first two lines as the chat's heading with ▾ **Show the whole message** under it (`AskHeading`, `dusty-panel.tsx`); what is sent to Dusty is unchanged.
- **Dusty answers in Markdown (2026-09-30).** Coy: *"showing the
  calculations in .md format in the chat panel."* His prompt asks for
  lists, bold and, for a job, a table per area (area · qty · cost · O.H. ·
  margin · price) from the budget; `src/lib/markdown.ts` (tested) turns it
  into a tree and `markdown.tsx` renders it with React elements only — no
  HTML from a reply ever reaches the page.
- **The job page, 2026-09-30:** "Why this job" is an **ⓘ** in the header
  that opens it in a sheet. Each section has **Photos** under its
  dimensions — a camera button (several shots on a phone) filing each photo
  on that section (`job_files.section_id`, `section-photos.tsx`). Since
  2026-10-05 (Coy's toolbar notes) its buttons are **Copy** (photos on the job
  or texted, ticked onto the area; since 2026-10-10 the picker drops down under the page header at the page's width, `Drawer drop`, and the ticks are a choice until **Add N photos** at its foot — Coy: "there is no button to add the photo"), **Upload** (the device's files) and
  **Camera** — the camera in the page (`getUserMedia`, back camera): shoot
  several, × drops one, **Add N photos** uploads them together; no camera or
  refused → the phone's own camera. The section's plan sheets row sits under
  its photos; its Notes mic is the icon alone (a spinner while it writes it
  down); the budget's sales tax is "Sales tax 10.5% ⓘ", where it came from
  behind the ⓘ; and the proposal's Tax line chip reads "Tax · off in the
  budget" when the budget's tax is off (there is nothing to show or fold),
  a tap turning it on. While
  Dusty is docked on a desktop, the job's totals move **into his panel**
  (`#sc-dusty-slot`, `use-dusty-docked.ts`), folded to one line, and the
  page drops its right column.
- **The customer page header, 2026-09-30:** one row — "CUSTOMER | Maurice
  Watkins" (Lexend 18) with "1 contact · 1 job" beside it (contact → Details
  at Contacts; job → Jobs); Dusty's summary in Inter 300. **On a desktop the
  seven tabs are a second icon rail** beside the main one (`sub-rail.tsx`,
  72px, Details first) — the main rail picks the page, this one the
  sub-page; the phone keeps its row of tabs.
- Dusty has two entry points: COLD from the footer (no job context) and HOT
  from a page (that page preloaded). The chat's header is one line (Coy,
  2026-10-02): **"Looking at: <the job>"** when HOT, else the chat's title
  (since 2026-10-07, Coy: "say the person's actual name, or the phone number if
  there is no name. Or … the job name" — before a chat starts the header reads
  the page's name from `/api/dusty/label`, session client, never "this
  person"; a saved chat's label drops "'s thread" / "(customer page)")
  ("New chat").
- **Voice messages and attachments (2026-10-01).** The **mic** is always
  beside the box, and Send is an arrow icon, off until there is something to
  send (Coy, 2026-10-09; it was Telegram's way, the mic only while the box was
  empty, and an Ask button): it records
  at once with a breathing red dot, the time and a live level meter
  (`voice-recorder.tsx`), × throws it away, ➤ sends the audio to
  `/api/dusty/transcribe` (Groq Whisper, `GROQ_API_KEY`; nothing kept) and
  the words go to Dusty as the question. Three minutes at most. **+** left
  of the box attaches photos and files: uploaded through `/api/files` onto
  what the chat is about (the job, the person, the customer —
  `job_files.customer_id`), else unfiled in Documents (`from_dusty`); the
  question carries their ids and Dusty is given each one's read, or told it
  isn't read yet (the background reader reads it within minutes).
  **A photo he looks at himself (Coy, 2026-10-04: "I shared a screenshot …
  he said it will take a couple of minutes to read the screenshot and never
  responded").** An attached JPEG, PNG, WebP or GIF of 5 MB or less that the
  reader hasn't read yet is sent to the model as an image with that question
  (up to four; `seeableMime()`, `src/lib/dusty/see.ts`, tested), so he
  answers about it in the same reply — a screenshot of ScopeCrete is read as
  the screen. HEIC and anything larger still wait for the reader.
  **A PDF he reads himself (Coy, 2026-10-07: icon-budget.pdf, "See attached
  preliminary budget" — Dusty said it was "not read yet" and asked him to
  wait; the reader read it 34 s later).** An attached PDF the reader hasn't
  read yet has its text pulled out in the same request (unpdf, as the
  reader does; `pdfTextNow()` in `see.ts`, tested) — at most two a question,
  12 MB at most (the reader's own cap, §2e), 8 s at most, the first 30,000
  characters — and he answers from it. A scan with no text, a bigger file, or
  one that won't open in time still waits for the reader. Nothing is written
  to the file; the reader still reads it and files its summary and facts.
  **The chat keeps its files (migration 079, applied 2026-10-04 on Coy's
  "apply 079"; checked after: the column, its ≤ 8 check, RLS unchanged — Coy 2026-10-04: "the chat
  history should at least keep the summary of the image that he read. We'll
  need to have a place for the photos uploaded to dusty to be saved in the
  database").** The uploads were already `files` rows (on what the chat is
  about, else unfiled `from_dusty`); `dusty_messages.file_ids` (≤ 8, nullable)
  now says which question carried them. Every later turn gives Dusty FILES
  ATTACHED EARLIER IN THIS CHAT — each by name and `file <id>` with the
  background reader's summary, facts and text once it has read it (within
  minutes), and the photo itself again while it hasn't (the four-image cap,
  this question's first) — so a follow-up question still knows what was in
  the picture. A reopened chat shows each question's photos as thumbnails
  (`/api/dusty/conversations/[id]` signs them), each opening in Files. Before
  079 is applied the route reads and writes without the column. **Dusty files
  them where Coy says** — plan steps `new_customer` (key `cust1`; name,
  type, person, phone, email, address only as said or written; through `POST
  /api/customers`, which now returns its person), `new_job` (now with a key
  `job1`, and `contact` may be `cust1`) and `file` {files, kind customer |
  contact | job, a full id or a key} → `PATCH /api/files/[id] {file_to}`, a
  move (the file leaves the job or person it was on; an old job's area and
  takeoff go; a file on no job leaves the Plan Room). The card lists the
  files' real names and where they go; Coy's tap applies it.
- **Dusty v1 (2026-09-27)** is wired: `src/components/v34/dusty-panel.tsx`
  rises from the footer on a phone and slides in from the right on desktop.
  It is HOT on whatever screen is open — a thread, a scope, a customer, or
  cost settings — and COLD anywhere else; the header says which. The browser
  sends only `{kind, id}` from its route; `/api/dusty` fetches the facts itself
  under the session (`src/lib/dusty/context.ts`), so RLS decides what Dusty can
  see and nothing typed in the browser becomes "context". Scope answers use
  the engine's own output, so Dusty quotes exactly the builder's numbers or
  names what blocks them. **Read-only toward the business:** no tools, no
  record changed, nothing sent — drafts come back as text to copy; on a job,
  a budget plan comes back as steps Coy applies with a tap (§3).
  **Docked on the right on a desktop (2026-09-29, Coy: "integrated into the
  main dashboard neatly so that I can see the rest of the dashboard").** At
  lg+ Dusty is a 400px column (`--sc-dusty`) under the header, no backdrop;
  `<main>` gains `.sc-with-dusty` (padding-right) so the page beside him stays
  whole and usable. **His column widens by its left edge (Coy, 2026-10-15: "widen the dusty chat window by clicking and dragging with my mouse")**: drag it (320px up to what leaves the page 480px), double-click to put it back to 400; kept on the device (`sc:dusty-w`) and set as `--sc-dusty`, so the page's padding and the drawers beside him follow. He stays HOT on the page that is open; a continued chat
  about something else offers "Ask about this page instead". History is behind
  ☰ in his header on every size. On a phone he is still the full page below.
  **Beyond the open page (Coy, 2026-10-04: "Dusty has trouble seeing other
  contexts outside of what page is open … He needs to be aware of all phone
  calls, texts, emails, ect").** Every chat, whatever page it started on, now
  also carries BEYOND THE PAGE (`reachBlocks()` / `renderReach()` in
  `src/lib/dusty/context.ts`, ~45k characters, oldest lines trimmed first):
  everyone, every customer (with its people) and every job **named in the
  question** or Coy's last three messages (`mentioned()`,
  `src/lib/dusty/mentions.ts`, tested: a whole name, or a surname /
  distinctive first name of 4+ letters that isn't an everyday or trade word;
  a shared first name brings all who have it) — who they are, their last 40
  calls (Quo's summaries and next steps) and texts, Coy's notes, emails filed
  on them, their files' reads; **a search of everything** for the question's
  words (`sc_search` over people, texts, call summaries and transcripts,
  notes, jobs and files, OR'd, plus email subjects and text); and the last
  five days of calls and texts with the intake inbox (a cold chat already
  had three days of them). Under the session, like the rest; the prompt
  tells him never to say he can only see the open page.
  **Vendors from the web, prices from their call (Coy, 2026-10-05: "I need
  dusty to be able to search the web and find vendors for steel bollards. And
  then when I make a call have that recording for the vendor thread. And then
  I'll have dusty create the items").** Dusty still has no tools: the APP
  searches. When Coy's message asks for it ("find vendors for …", "search the
  web for …", "who sells …" — `webAsk()`, `src/lib/dusty/web.ts`, tested in
  `tests/dusty-web.test.mjs`), `/api/dusty` builds one or two queries from the
  thing asked for and the job's city (never its street, never a customer's
  name — `webQueries()`, `cityOf()`), runs them through Tavily
  (`TAVILY_API_KEY`) or else Brave (`BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY`) on the server
  (`src/lib/web-search-server.ts`), and puts up to 12 results under **WEB
  SEARCH**, marked data-not-instructions, with Coy's vendors already on file.
  No key → the block says so and Dusty tells Coy which key to add; he never
  lists vendors from memory. He suggests the best few with their links and
  ends with `new_vendor` steps {name, kind, phone / email / address only as a
  result's text shows them, website, source = the result's link}; the card
  links the page and says to check the number. Coy's Apply goes through
  + Vendor's own call (`POST /api/vendors`, a number already on someone is
  409 and not added again) and leaves "Found by Dusty on the web" with the
  links as a note on them. This is the one exception to "never look a number
  up": for a vendor Coy asked to find, from a page he can open, on his tap — a
  customer's or person's number is still never looked up.
  **Reading a vendor's site (Coy, 2026-10-06, decision card: "Tavily
  Extract" — over Crawl4AI on the VPS or Crawlbase).** When Coy pastes a link
  (up to three), or asks to read the site of the vendor he has open (the
  "Website:" / "Found on:" lines on Dusty's new_vendor note), `/api/dusty`
  reads the page with Tavily Extract (`readPages()`, same `TAVILY_API_KEY`;
  only the link leaves the app) and puts its text under **WEBSITE TEXT**,
  marked data-not-instructions, ~7k characters a page, 16k in all
  (`pageAsk()`, `publicUrl()` — never a local, private, tailnet or our own
  address — `renderPages()`, tested). Dusty answers from it; a phone or price
  from a site goes on a plan step only as printed, a price marked "from their
  website — confirm". Scraping stays off the VPS, where Hermes's agent lives.
  **A vendor from a document, a thread or Coy's words (Coy, 2026-10-12: "Vendors need website fields, and
  Dusty wasn't able to add a vendor from this document … he should be able to add or create a vendor with all the
  information in the document").** Dusty read Teague Rental Equipment's rate sheet and offered the vendor, and the
  card said "1 step didn't make sense": `new_vendor` was kept only with a web result's link as its `source`. Now
  `source` is that link, `file <id>` (a file in the CONTEXT) or Coy's words / their thread (`from`: web · file ·
  words); a phone that isn't a US number is left off instead of dropping the step; and the step takes `website`
  (as typed — "teaguerental.com" — kept as a full address, `cleanWebsite()`, `src/lib/vendor-website.ts`,
  tested), `trades`, a `note` on what they sell or rent, `contact` (someone in the CONTEXT — the thread with that
  number — filed as the vendor instead of a second person) and a `key` (vend1) that a later `new_item` /
  `vendor_price` names as its vendor, so a rate sheet's rates come in as Price book items with that vendor's
  price in the same plan. On Apply the vendors are made first (`applyVendors()`, `apply-plan.ts`): `POST
  /api/vendors`; a number or email already on a contact (409), or the contact Dusty named, is filed vendor
  (`/api/contacts/[id]/bucket`) and filled in only where blank — never written over, and someone filed customer
  or personal is left alone and named on the card. A note on them says where it came from. The prompt
  (`RECORD_RULES`) tells him to do it with no web search, copying details exactly as written.
  **A vendor's website (migration 105, applied 2026-10-13 on Coy's "apply 105" — checked after: the column nullable, `contacts_website_shape` validated, RLS and the 4 policies on contacts unchanged, 221 contacts untouched; no "Website: …" note was waiting to carry over).**
  `contacts.website` (nullable, http(s), ≤ 300). + Vendor and Edit have **Website**; the vendor page shows it in
  its facts with **Website ↗**; `GET /api/vendors[/id]` return it, `POST` and `PATCH` take it. Before 105 the
  reads leave it out and a website given is kept as an internal note "Website: …" on them
  (`vendor-website-server.ts`); Edit sends it only when it changed, so no note is repeated.
  **On the contact card too (Coy, same day: "Where is the website field?").** Contact info shows **Website**
  (a link) when there is one, and its Edit has a Website box with Save → `PATCH /api/contacts/[id]/website`
  (blank clears; session client, org from the session); `/api/people/[id]` returns `website`. Before 105 the
  card, the vendor page and Dusty's reads take the newest "Website: …" note on them as the website
  (`readWebsites()`), so what was saved shows; clearing one waits for 105.
  **Store prices (Coy, 2026-10-09: "Why can't Dusty look up pricing for
  2x4x16 number two pine on Home Depot and Lowe's and add it to the
  budget?").** The search above only ran for "find vendors …", so a price
  question searched nothing and Dusty said so. Now a message with a price
  word and a store named (Home Depot, Lowe's, Menards, Ace, Walmart, Amazon,
  Grainger, White Cap, 84 Lumber, Fastenal) or a look-up word (`priceAsk()`,
  `src/lib/dusty/web.ts`, tested; "the price of this job" is not one) runs a
  search per store kept to its site (Tavily `include_domains`, Brave
  `site:`), else Home Depot, Lowe's and anywhere (`priceQueries()`); only the
  item's words leave the app. The results go under **WEB PRICES**, marked as
  data, with the date. Dusty quotes a price only as a result prints it, with
  its link, and on Coy's ask offers a `new_item` (or `vendor_price`) whose
  `note` — "Home Depot $9.98 on Oct 9, 2026 — <link> — confirm" — is kept as
  the item's description; Coy's Apply makes it and puts it on the area. No
  price in the results → he says so and asks for the product's link (WEBSITE
  TEXT reads it).
  **Estimates, labelled, and going prices (Coy, 2026-10-09: "There's a rule that says that
  he can't guess or make any assumptions without hard facts. But I want him to estimate based
  on best guesses in some cases. And I want him to do research on the internet").** Facts stay
  strict — a name, a number someone said, a site dimension, a date is never passed off as
  recorded — but for budgets and prices Dusty may **estimate**, saying it is one and what it
  rests on: Coy's own Price book and budgets first, then the web, then a rule of thumb for
  concrete work. A site size is guessed only when Coy asks. In a plan, an estimated number
  carries `estimate` (its basis) on `add`, `new_item`, `update_item`, `vendor_price` or
  `new_area` (`ESTIMATE_STEPS`, `cleanAction()`); the card shows ESTIMATE. On Apply the line's
  (or item's) description gets "Estimate — <basis>" after any note (`markEstimate()`,
  `src/lib/estimate-flag.ts`, tested in `tests/estimate-flag.test.mjs`; no migration), a new
  area's Notes say its size is an estimate, a vendor price's note says so. The budget shows
  **ESTIMATE · <basis> · Confirm** on the line (Confirm takes the mark off, keeping the note);
  the draft's Approve lists unconfirmed estimates as a block; the proposal never shows the mark
  (`unmark()` in `priceDraft()`). The job's own price is still only the engine's; Dusty's
  estimate of a blocked job is a separate, labelled figure. **Going prices:** on a job, any
  budget or pricing question (`budgetAsk()`) that isn't a store lookup or a vendor search runs
  up to four searches (`budgetQueries()`): the job's kinds of work per square foot near its
  city, and the names of budget lines with no price yet — nothing of the customer, the street or
  a number — under **GOING PRICES**, marked as data, which he cites. Store prices from WEB PRICES
  go on as estimates too, until Coy confirms. "Never look a customer's number up" is unchanged.
  **Any other question for the web (Coy, 2026-10-10: "dusty still can't search the web for vendors
  and pricing on materials and labor").** `TAVILY_API_KEY` was set in production all along; the
  searches above each needed their own words, so "can you search for concrete contractors in baton
  rouge", "look up a vendor called Syncor" and "the going rate for removing curbs in a parking lot"
  (a job, but not about its own work types) searched nothing, and Dusty, with no results in front of
  him, said search wasn't switched on. Now `webAsk()` also hears "search for …" and "look up …" and
  counts contractors and "a vendor called X"; and any other question with a search or research word,
  or a going-rate, per-unit, hourly or what-does-it-cost ask (`researchAsk()`), on any page, is
  searched once with Coy's own sentence cleaned (`researchQuery()`): niceties and "and add it to the
  budget" cut, no phone, email, house number and street, nor any word of a contact's or customer's
  name on file (trade words like pump or steel kept), the job's city (else Baton Rouge) and the year
  added to a price question. The results go under **WEB RESEARCH**, marked as data; on a job a
  budget question puts that query first among its GOING PRICES. A question about Coy's own records
  ("search my texts for …", "look up the Icon job") is not sent to the web. The prompt tells Dusty
  never to say search is off or lives in Settings — to give a failed search's reason word for word,
  or ask Coy to say "search the web for …". Tested in `tests/dusty-web.test.mjs`. Hermes still runs
  Dusty with its own memory and tools on the VPS (§6, Hermes ran him as an agent), which shows as
  "I found a relevant past session" in his answers; that is fixed on the VPS or by moving Dusty to
  the model API, not here.
  **A suggested search is a button (Coy, 2026-10-15: "make this search phrase clickable instead of making me type a specific phrase").**
  When an answer says "search the web for …", the phrase is drawn as a 🔍 link (`MdAsk` in `markdown.tsx`); a tap
  asks it as Coy's next question in the same chat, so the search runs with that conversation's context.
  **Share (Coy, 2026-10-14: "a share button right next to the text size adjuster button on the header in
  Dusty").** Beside Aa: **The last answer** or **The whole chat** as plain text (`shareText()`,
  `src/lib/dusty/share.ts`, tested — the plan block left out and counted, ids hidden), through the
  phone's share sheet (`navigator.share`), else copied. Quoted lines (">" or "&gt;") in an answer are
  drawn as a quote (`parseMarkdown()`), and entities read back.
  **Room for a whole budget (Coy, 2026-10-12: "it keeps saying cut off, ask for rest").** A plan for every section of the Donaldson job ran past the 4,000-token answer cap; the unfinished plan block is never drawn, so all Coy saw was the cut-off line, and "give me the rest" started the same plan over. The model API call now allows 16,000 tokens (Hermes 8,000), the route 300 s and the upstream 280 s; the prompt asks for few words and compact JSON before a plan; a cut-off inside a plan block says the plan ran out of room and to ask one section at a time.
  **Dusty on Anthropic's API (Coy, 2026-10-10: "move dusty to the api").** `/api/dusty` calls
  Anthropic's Messages API straight from the Vercel route whenever `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set (the
  key the draft reader, card scan and section-cut reads already use; `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE_ID` sent
  when set): streamed, no tools, no memory, the same system prompt and context, model
  `claude-sonnet-5-5` (`DUSTY_MODEL` overrides), photos as base64 image blocks on the latest
  question, turns merged so roles alternate (`anthropicTurns()`). Hermes on the VPS is the
  fallback only — used when the key is missing or `DUSTY_PROVIDER=hermes`. This ends Hermes's own
  memory and tools leaking into his answers ("I found a relevant past session") and the open
  prompt-injection worry about an agent with a terminal reading customer texts. Usage is on the
  Anthropic bill.
  **The call.** Nothing new was needed: a call from Coy's Quo line to a number
  on a contact projects onto that contact's thread (§2a), and 8 of the 12
  outbound calls in the fortnight to 2026-10-05 came with Quo's recording and
  transcript; the vendor page's Timeline and their thread play the recording.
  The vendor has to be added (with that number) before or after the call —
  a call to an unknown number makes an Unsorted thread, filed vendor by hand.
  **The items.** On a vendor's page Dusty's context now carries the Price book
  and the budget-plan rules, beside their last calls' transcripts. Asked to
  make the items from what they quoted, he proposes `vendor_price` {item,
  vendor, unit_cost, unit, note} — their price on an existing item, through
  `POST /api/cost-items/[id]/sources` — or `new_item` with `vendor`, made with
  them as its first (default) vendor price (`price_source='quote'`). A price
  only as the transcript or Quo's summary has it, the line quoted for Coy to
  check (a machine heard it). Then, on the job, he adds those items to the
  budget as before.
  **People on customers, and names (Coy, 2026-10-05: "The actual customer
  name is Icon Technologies … John … is just a contact that's inside this
  customer. Can you fix it?").** Dusty had no step for it: his plan made a
  NEW customer with John's phone (which `POST /api/customers` refuses as a
  duplicate) and a `move_contact` step that doesn't exist. Now `new_customer`
  takes `contact` — someone already in the CONTEXT becomes its person (filed
  customer, primary; their own one-person customer renamed; 409 if they are
  on a customer with other people) — `attach_contact` {contact, customer
  full id or cust1} goes through the customer page's + Add contact
  (`sc_attach_contact`), and `set_name` {contact | customer} through the
  pencil and the customer's Edit. A customer has no phone column, so a
  company's office number is a contact on it: `new_contact` {customer, name,
  phone or email, label} — only one Coy gave or the context shows (he is told
  he can't look numbers up or search the web, and to ask) — through `POST
  /api/contacts` (filed customer) then + Add contact. Threads and the person
  page now show a business customer's name beside its person ("Icon
  Technologies" on John Freeling's row; `company` on `/api/threads` and
  `/api/people/[id]`). **A contact needs no customer (Coy, 2026-10-10: "It's a new contact and i can move it to a customer later if I want; or not").** Asked to add Elena Sacco, Dusty refused three times — `new_contact` required a customer, so he offered to make a "Personal" customer to hold her. Now `customer` may be null: the step goes through + Contact's own `POST /api/contacts` and nothing else, Unsorted unless Coy names personal, vendor or customer (`bucket`); the card says "Add … to Contacts". A customer named that isn't a full id or cust1 still drops the step. The prompt tells him not to ask which customer and never to make one just to hold a person. Tested in `tests/dusty-plan.test.mjs`.
  The prompt now says he is Dusty only, has no tools, files or sessions of
  his own, never narrates looking things up or names his setup, and takes
  Coy's word and spelling instead of asking him to confirm it.
  **Who spoke on a call (2026-10-09).** Quo labels each transcript line with
  the speaker's phone number, and Dusty's chat was handed them raw: on the
  Patio caller's call he took the org's own Quo line for the customer, because
  the caller asked "This is Baton Rouge Concreeter, right?" and Coy's "This is
  Coy" was heard as "Corey". The background reader had relabelled for a long
  time (`relabelTranscript()`, enrich-contacts); now Dusty's transcripts (the
  thread's, a job's calls, search snippets) read "Coy (our line):" for any of
  the org's lines, switched-off ones too, and "Customer:" / "Vendor:" for the
  other side, with "Corey" on our side read as Coy (`labelSpeakers()`,
  `src/lib/dusty/transcript.ts`, tested). Stored transcripts are unchanged.
  **No ids in his words (2026-10-05).** Asked whether a text from (727)
  628-9657 came in, he answered with "contact dd08251b-…". Ids are for plans
  only: the prompt says to name people (or give the number when there is no
  name), and the chat takes any full id, or an 8-character ref with "…",
  out of what is shown along with its "contact" / "(customer …)" label
  (`hideIds()` in `plan.ts`, tested); the plan block keeps its ids.
  **Hermes ran him as an agent with tools (seen 2026-10-05).** His answer
  read Hermes's own `state.db`, hit Hermes's command guard ("an error from my
  own security layer"), narrated each step, and said "I'm Coy, not Dusty" —
  Hermes's own persona and memory talking. That is the VPS's Hermes serving
  `/v1/chat/completions` with its toolsets, memory and persona on, which this
  section already forbids: the context carries customer texts, and a text
  can carry instructions to an agent with a terminal. Fix on the VPS: serve
  Dusty from a Hermes with no tools, no memory and no persona files (its own
  profile and port), or a plain pass-through to the model. Nothing in the
  app can turn those off.
  **The catch-up (2026-09-29; since 2026-09-30 the chat's "What's new?"
  button, the header emblem being gone)** asks "What's new since I last looked?" with context kind
  `activity` (migration 044): `activityContext()` reads, under RLS, who got in
  touch since this device's last catch-up (`localStorage`, clamped to 7 days,
  default 24 h) with Dusty's status lines, spam senders left out; the
  reader's runs; next steps due today or overdue (Central); and the unsorted
  count. Saved as "Catch-up · <date>". It does not make the background reader
  run early (that needs the worker token, which no browser request may reach).
  The logo goes to the Dashboard; the footer Dusty just opens the chat.
  **Full screen, and kept (migration 043, Coy 2026-09-28: "reference older
  conversations in a side project panel to select and continue").** Dusty is a
  page under the header, over the footer (v34 DustyScreen): header with
  hamburger, title, "Looking at …", + and ×; the history rail slides in from
  the LEFT on a phone and stays open on a desktop, grouped *About this
  person/job* · People · Jobs · Everything else; answers read like a page —
  the question as a heading, the answer full width, Copy under it.
  `dusty_conversations` / `dusty_messages` are private to the person (RLS:
  org member AND `user_id = auth.uid()`; verified a second user sees none).
  `/api/dusty` takes `{question, conversation_id?}`, reads the history from
  the database — never from the browser — and a continued chat keeps the
  context it started with. Opening Dusty on a person or job picks up the
  latest chat about them. **The model runs in the Hermes container on the VPS**
  (§8), reached over Tailscale Funnel at `HERMES_URL` with `HERMES_API_KEY`
  (OpenAI-compatible chat completions; Hermes routes via OpenRouter, model
  `HERMES_MODEL`, default `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` since 2026-09-28 — 3.5-haiku began returning empty 200s). No
  model-provider key lives in Vercel — except `GROQ_API_KEY`, for speech to
  text only (section dictation, above). The Vercel route stays the only thing
  that touches customer data — Hermes sees only the context the route sends.
  Hermes must serve Dusty with its tools OFF: the context carries customer
  texts, and a text can carry instructions. The old `/api/chat/dusty` — an
  unauthenticated relay to a private-IP Ollama box — is deleted.
- **Dusty's activity line (2026-09-27)** sits under the header on every
  screen (`src/components/v34/activity-line.tsx`, `/api/activity`). v34's
  StreamBar, fed by hardcoded DREAM_LINES, was never ported; this replaces it
  with **real rows only** — each line is a `contact_enrich_runs` row (reading,
  named, suggested, couldn't read), the waiting count, or the heartbeat. **Each
  new thing shows once per device, then leaves the line** (Coy, 2026-09-28:
  "don't be redundant"; reminders once a day); with nothing new it shows
  Dusty's unseen **musings** (above), then "All caught up · last read …". Tap
  to drop down the last ~20 with detail, each linking to its thread. A reader that isn't working
  (not configured, Hermes unreachable, error, silent >15 min) **pins** the line
  in amber and stops the cycling. The dot pulses only while a read is running
  or a Dusty reply is streaming. Idle says when something last happened; it
  never invents something happening now. **No bubble (2026-09-28):** the
  words sit on the grey glass row and type in letter by letter; a streaming
  Dusty reply shows its own tokens. **The dot says what kind of work
  (2026-09-30):** blue, slow glow — reading someone; amber, quick — Dusty
  answering; green, one blink — just named or filed; red, steady — the
  reader isn't working; grey — idle. Reduced motion stills it. **Since 2026-10-02 it
  is a diamond** that always glows (idle a slow grey breath), with a small
  arrow under it — the expand button; the chevron on the right is gone so
  musings get the whole width (Coy). **Since 2026-10-05 it is six small
  square dots** — green, orange, blue, twice — lighting one after another
  (Coy: "6 little square dots 3 different colors blinking in this order"),
  sitting low, level with the logo's ".ai"; quick while Dusty answers, slower
  while he reads, a slow pass at idle, all lit and steady when the reader
  isn't working (`Dots` in `activity-line.tsx`, `.sc-dots`); the whole line
  is the expand button. **Its words glow green like the wordmark's ".ai"
  (Coy, 2026-10-10)** — #1FA971, breathing on the ".ai"'s rhythm (`scGlowLine`,
  still under reduced motion), a warning in its own colour — and sit low,
  their baseline on the ".ai"'s (`TEXT_DROP`). On a desktop
  the line sits in the header beside the logo and its drop-down is ~560px
  wide under it; on a phone it is the row under the header. The emblem and
  the line from it are gone; while a read runs the footer Dusty shows the
  mixer. **On a desktop it takes all the header's room up to the buttons**
  (2026-10-02, Coy: "there is enough room … don't cut it off"; it was capped
  at 720px with an empty spacer beside it). The words aren't cut before they
  reach it either: `/api/activity` clipped file names to 40 characters
  ("Reading 2635 Walker High Dugouts Specs 2026 08……"); its one-line
  summaries now carry up to ~140–220 characters and the line trims to the
  room it has.
  **Dusty's type is Figtree (Coy, 2026-10-05: "change the font for
  everything dusty write to Figtree … even the thoughts in the header";
  JetBrains Mono since 2026-10-02 before that)** — the activity line, its
  drop-down and thoughts, and the chat (questions, answers at 14px, his
  plans, the message box), in every theme (`agent` in `tokens.ts` →
  `--sc-font-agent`, next/font's `--font-agent`). Machine text on Lines and
  Logs (event types, webhook URLs, payloads) stays JetBrains Mono
  (`--sc-font-code`). Buttons and
  headers keep the theme's faces. **The drop-down comes down over
  everything** — the plan sheets panel, Dusty, any sheet: while it is open
  the header's layer is lifted (`html[data-head-drop] .sc-head-wrap`).
  **Thoughts can be read, traced and talked about (Coy, 2026-10-02: "this
  thought can be found nowhere in the dropdown and it doesn't scroll … it
  would be more interesting if I knew what the thought was being processed
  from … and maybe even comment on it in the dusty chat").** A line too long
  for its row, once typed, slides to its end, rests and slides back
  (`.sc-typed[data-marq]`, 40 px/s, measured on the DOM — no React state;
  still under reduced motion), and stays up long enough to slide through
  twice (`lingerMs()`, at least 20 s for a thought). The drop-down opens
  with **Dusty's thoughts**: each musing and dream in full, newest first,
  with where it came from — "From reading Dylan Ordoyne's calls and texts",
  or "A dream about Mon, Sep 28's calls and texts — Amber, Luis"
  (`/api/activity`). Tapping one goes to that person's page and opens Dusty
  there with `About your thought — "…" —` in his message box; his context on
  a person carries his own recent thoughts and dreams about them, so he can
  say what in the conversation it came from. **On a phone Dusty's chat
  covers the activity row** (it starts under the logo row, `.sc-dusty-sheet`
  at 54px) — his sprite in the message bar already says what he is doing.
- **Loading faster (Coy, 2026-10-05: "How can we get the app to load
  faster").** Measured that day: the database answered in IAD beside the
  functions, but every RLS read ran `get_user_org_ids()` once per row — it was
  plpgsql with no volatility, so VOLATILE, and no org_id index could be used
  (four list reads as Coy: 49.8 ms; STABLE: 10.9 ms). **Migration 083** marks
  it STABLE (applied 2026-10-05 on Coy's "Apply 083"; checked after: STABLE, the same reads 13.5 ms, Coy still sees 208 contacts and 42 jobs). Every API call checked the session
  with `auth.getUser()`, a round trip to Supabase Auth (p50 25 ms, p90 ~180
  ms), then `sc_my_orgs`, then `org_members`, one after another;
  `getApiSession()` now verifies the token locally with `getClaims()` (the
  project signs with published keys; the database checks the same signature on
  every query) and, on an org's address, takes the membership from
  `sc_my_orgs` alone — one round trip. `/api/session/me`, which the header
  waits on, runs its five reads side by side, and `/api/activity` (every 20 s)
  its six. The Threads badge and drift dot are read at most every 30 s, not on
  every page change. supabase-js and the search panel load only when needed:
  the dashboard's first JavaScript went 1,086 → 843 KB (320 → 257 KB gzipped).
  Not done: a service worker (the PWA has none, §8), so every launch fetches
  the page and its data afresh.
- **Taps stay quick (2026-09-30, Coy's interaction timing: INP 1,056 ms,
  976 ms of it input delay).** The activity line typed by setting React
  state every 18 ms and re-laid-out the page per letter; it now types with
  one stepped CSS clip (`.sc-typed`) on its own layer, the button is
  `contain: strict`. Dusty's streamed reply reaches the line through a small
  store (`src/lib/v34/dusty-live.ts`), not layout state, and tokens are
  drawn once per frame; earlier answers' Markdown is memoised. The Price
  book draws its rows 60 at a time (`ROW_CHUNK`). Measured in the harness at
  4× CPU slowdown: idle long tasks on the Price book 11 → 1 in 5 s, its
  biggest load task 775 → ~230 ms, every tap under 200 ms. Don't put
  per-frame or per-token state in `dashboard/layout.tsx`.
- **Chat bubbles are muted (2026-09-29, Coy: "easy on the eyes"):** theirs
  warm paper `#F7F6F2`, ours soft slate `#D8E1E8`, ink text on both
  (`DA.bubbleIn` / `DA.bubbleOut` in `conversation.tsx`). Hi-viz orange is
  for actions, not for our own words. **Matte since 2026-10-15 (Coy: "make the colors more matt and muted"):** no border; theirs `color-mix(sunk 70%, card)`, ours `color-mix(outBg 38%, sunk)`, ink text on both in every theme (`DA.bubbleIn` / `bubbleOut` / `bubbleInk`), the customer page's Timeline the same. **The date and time sit under the bubble, on its side** (`BubbleTime`), texts and calls alike — not inside it.
- Dusty conversations persist and are resumable (above), grouped by the
  person or job they are about.
- **Files panel (2026-09-29)** — `src/components/v34/files-panel.tsx` on the
  job page (under the sections) and the customer / vendor page (right column
  when wide, a Files tab when narrow): Add photos / files (camera or library,
  many at once; drag and drop on a desktop) with progress and retry; a grid
  filtered All · Photos · Documents · From texts; tap for the viewer — name,
  what it is, area, note, Show on proposal, delete, and Dusty's read.
- **Price book (2026-09-29)** — `src/components/v34/price-book.tsx`. On the
  desktop rail after Customers, and in the Toolbox on a phone; Cost settings
  links to it (one editor), and a vendor's page keeps its Prices door. No line of explanation under the title (Coy, 2026-10-05).
  Find, **By group · By vendor** (remembered per device), Show retired.
  **Wide (≥900px measured): a spreadsheet** — Item · Vendor (or Group) ·
  Item # · Type · Unit · Unit cost · Markup · Taxed · Counted on a job ·
  Price date · From; cost, markup and taxed edited in the cell (a cost typed
  there is stamped today, "typed"); a price older than 90 days says **old**.
  Narrow: cards. The card sheet shows **Price history** (date, price, ▲▼ %,
  from where). **Import ▾** opens the import (`price-import.tsx`) — since
  2026-10-10 a panel that slides down from under the Price book's subheader,
  the page's own width beside Dusty when he is docked (Drawer `drop`; Coy:
  "slide DOWN from the price book header and be the same width as the Price
  Book page"), scrolling on its own, × or Esc to close; it was a wide sheet
  over everything: a spreadsheet's file → columns and vendors → preview → Import N, a UFGS
  section's file → preview (§3);
  imports are listed with **Undo** on **Logs → Price book** (Coy, 2026-10-07: the line under the title is gone; that tab also lists every price change the database recorded, `/api/price-book/changes`, `price-book-log.tsx`).
  **Columns (2026-09-30):** drag a header divider to size a column
  (double-click resets it); **Columns ▾** shows or hides any but Item.
  Saved to the account (`pricebook_cols`). The phone view is unchanged.
  **Several at once (Coy, 2026-10-10: "select multiple cost items to delete at the same time or select
  an entire cost group to edit/delete").** On the desktop grid each card has a tick, a group's row ticks
  all its cards, and the header ticks everything; with any ticked, a bar offers **Move to a group…** and
  **Retire N** (the same retire as one card's, Undo for 10 s). **✎ Group** on a group's row opens it to
  rename, change its cost type (`PATCH /api/cost-groups/[id] {cost_type}`) or **Delete group** — its
  cards retired and the group deleted (kept, saying so, when budget lines are filed under it); Undo makes
  the group again and puts its cards back in it.
  **Today's prices (Coy, 2026-10-10: "When I select items … pull updated pricing from vendors that are
  connected to this product").** **↻ Today's prices** on the ticked bar, and on a card's Vendors, asks
  Dusty for those items' current prices from their vendors (`refreshQuestion()`,
  `src/lib/dusty/price-refresh.ts`, tested): the question names the items and their vendors, so his
  web price search runs (WEB PRICES, kept to a store's site when a vendor is one); he gives each price
  with its link and offers `vendor_price` steps marked estimate. Nothing changes until Coy applies them.
  A vendor with no web prices (a ready-mix plant) still needs their quote or invoice.
  **Show retired is gone (Coy, 2026-10-10)**: retired cards stay out of the list; the swipe's Undo is the way back.
  **Coy's notes of 2026-10-22:** the Price book's By group / By vendor is one **Sort by: Group ▾** button with a menu; picked from a budget on a desktop, its title and area share the Cards · Assemblies row, and its search is the magnifier, the bubble dropping full width under the row. An assembly opens its editor under its own row (▸ / ▾), not as a sheet from the bottom. On Takeoffs the "N of M scanned sheets read" chip sits on the drawing's toolbar row, not a row of its own.
  **Coy's budget notes of 2026-10-22 (no migration):** a cost item's settings open as a panel dropping down from its own row, the budget's full width (the grid's row or the card's line), not a side sheet — × or Esc closes it. Cost items move within their group by a ⋮⋮ grip at the row's left on a desktop, saved as each line's `sort_order` (`PATCH …/budget/lines/[id] {sort_order}`). A file opened from the budget's section Photos or a Files panel opens front and centre over the page, and every photo there and in the Files page's preview has **✎ Draw & comment** (`photo-markup.tsx`): a pen in four colours, notes placed with a tap, Undo / Clear and a comment, on a phone or a desktop; **Save a marked-up copy** adds the drawing as a new photo beside the original (same job, section or person, the comment as its note), the original untouched. A Price book item's card has **Images** (`/api/cost-items/[id]/images`): pictures kept as `files` rows on no job or person with `source_url = cost_item:<id>`, in job-files under `{org}/cost-item/`, a tap showing one large, × removing it.
  **Swipe a card left on a phone to retire it (Coy, 2026-10-09)** — the
  card slides to a red Retire (`SwipeRetire`), the same retire as the card
  sheet's (`DELETE /api/cost-items/[id]`, `active=false`), with Undo for 6 s;
  Show retired brings it back.
- **Scope rail with no budget (step 3b).** No lines → just the days box
  (since 2026-10-02, Coy: the orange **No price yet** box and its Assemblies
  button are gone — assemblies load inside the budget's areas — and so is
  the rail's "Read this on the phone" title). With lines → each option's total, cost, O.H.,
  margin, $/sf, and the job minimum (day minimum × days). The section's
  Reinforcement picker lists reinforcement cards and says "default for …"
  when an assembly chose it.
- **Price book → Assemblies (step 3b)** — `src/components/v34/assemblies.tsx`,
  the second tab (`?tab=assemblies`). A row: name, what it applies to
  ("Residential · Driveway", "Any job · Patio", "Loaded by hand"), card
  count, reinforcement, **Auto**. The sheet: name, job type, kind of area,
  "Fill new areas on its own", its cards (no reinforcement dropdown since
  2026-10-01 — Coy; mesh or rebar is a card like any other, and one set
  before shows with × to take it off) (rule and
  cost, a quantity box for typed ones, ×), + Add cards (search), delete.
  The budget's card picker has **Load an assembly** (pick the area first)
  and shows groups folded until you search; lines say "from X" and
  "reinforcement".
- **Vendors (migration 051, Coy 2026-09-29)** — its own page,
  `/dashboard/vendors`, on the desktop rail after Price book and in the Toolbox
  on a phone (`src/components/v34/vendors.tsx`). The list: find, All ·
  Suppliers · Subs · Equipment, each vendor's kind, where they deliver,
  phone, prices, how many items they are default on, newest price,
  receipts; **+ Vendor** (no phone needed) and **+ Add a receipt or quote**
  (the main button). **Delete** on a vendor's page (Coy, 2026-10-04: "Why
  can't I delete a vendor?") — the same restorable delete as their Threads
  page (§6 Archive and delete), with the Why? box. **Delete vendor asks about the contact too (Coy, 2026-10-22: "it should also ask me if I want to archive the contact, because usually it's still an unknown contact"; no migration):** the dialog has **Also archive the contact**, ticked to start. Either way the vendor filing comes off first (`/api/contacts/[id]/bucket` {bucket: null}, back to Unsorted; their Price book prices stay). Ticked, the contact is then archived as before (Undo puts the contact back, in Unsorted). Unticked, they stay in Contacts and Threads as Unsorted, and a reason given is kept as the internal note "Taken off Vendors — …" (`vendorRemovedNote()`, `src/lib/archive.ts`, tested); there is no Undo for that one — file them vendor again. **Dusty is HOT on a
  vendor's page** (it was cold): `/dashboard/vendors/<id>` is that person's
  context, plus a **Vendor** block — their kind, where they deliver, every
  price of theirs in the Price book and the jobs they're on. A vendor's page (`/dashboard/vendors/[id]`) is
  business first: name, kind, area, phone, email, address (Edit), Call,
  Conversation (if they have messages); **Prices** — each item they price
  with their price, date, source, the cheaper/next vendor, and a **★**
  (theirs is the price budgets use; tap to make it so); **Receipts &
  quotes**; **Jobs** they're on. Their Threads page keeps its Prices tab,
  now at THEIR price with ★, linking here to change it.
- **Vendors v2 — trades and bid requests (migration 091, applied
  2026-10-09 on Coy's "apply 091"; checked after: both tables with RLS and 5
  policies, the guard triggers, `trades` and `crew_size` on contacts, the
  `shared_bid_assets` storage policy, anon holds no grant on either table and
  may run only the three `sc_*bid*` functions, a bad token returns nothing;
  Claude Design, Vendors v2, 2026-10-08: "Find subs by
  trade. Pick several. One bid request goes to all of them.").** A vendor's
  **trades** (concrete finishing, forming, rebar, pump, ready mix, demo &
  haul-off, excavation, base & gravel, saw cutting, equipment rental, other —
  `TRADES`, `src/lib/bids.ts`, tested) and **crew size** are on their contact
  (`contacts.trades` text[] ≤ 12, `crew_size` 1–500 — there is no vendors
  table), picked on + Vendor and Edit. **The list:** a trade column on the
  left on a desktop (chips on a phone, Kind a drop-down), sort by name,
  fastest answers or most prices; each row opens the vendor's page (Coy, 2026-10-19 — only the tick at its left picks them, and the Open button is gone) and has a tick, trades, how to reach
  them (Text / Email, "No email"), bids answered and how fast ("2 of 3
  answered · median 3h", `bidStats()`), prices on file. **On a phone (Coy,
  2026-10-09)** the header carries no count and there is no "22 vendors" line;
  Sort sits at the end of the trade chips' row, after Kind. Ticking shows a
  black bar: Group text (Coy's Messages app) or **Send a bid request →**.
  **The composer** (`bid-composer.tsx`, also from a vendor's page with them
  picked and from the job's Summary → Bids → **Get bids**): the job (open
  ones), its scopes — each area and its lines with quantities from the
  engine's `budget()`, "Whole job" lines too, a tick per line and
  **Sacco supplies / They price it** on materials — then the packet (address
  & access, dates, quantities, the aerial, site photos, each plan PDF), how
  they price (one total · per line · **their rates**, which fills in their
  past prices by line), price-by and pour dates, an optional note, and a
  follow-up next step for Coy ("Get Kim's price for …", `contact_tasks`,
  due the price-by date) — never a bot follow-up (§10). **Send** texts each
  one through Quo on that tap (§6 Texts send through Quo); email opens Coy's
  own mail app; Copy link. **Sent is not delivered (2026-10-09):** Coy's
  test to himself said "Texted ✓" and never came — his vendor contact's
  number was (225) 508-3358, one digit off his 3258, and Quo takes a text to
  any number. Each row now says "Sent to (225) 508-3358 · waiting for Quo to
  deliver…", then "Delivered ✓" once Quo's webhook brings it back (`GET
  /api/texts?quo_id=`, the message by Quo's id under RLS), or after 40 s that
  Quo hasn't delivered it — check the number. **A sent request opens from
  the job's Summary (Coy, 2026-10-09: "resend a bid request or check on the
  status by clicking into a bid request").** A tap on a request in Bids opens
  it: per vendor, when and how it was sent, whether a text carrying their
  link came back from Quo delivered (and to which number), opened how often,
  answered with the price and note, or link off — and **Resend text** (the
  same words and link through Quo, Coy's tap, then waiting for delivery),
  Email ↗, Copy link, See their page, Vendor ›. `GET
  /api/bid-requests/recipients/[id]` gives the resend's words (bidText from the
  stored request) and the delivered text (their outbound texts with
  `/b/<token>` in them). **What a vendor sees** is a frozen copy built by
  `shownScopes()` field by field — names, sizes, quantities, units, who
  supplies — never a cost, price, margin, O.H. or Coy's notes.
  `bid_requests` (job, mode, dates, `scopes`, `packet`, `assets.paths`) and
  `bid_request_recipients` (one per vendor, its own 32-character random
  `token`, sent / opened / views, their answer, `revoked_at`), RLS by
  `get_user_org_ids()`, members insert and read and may update only
  sent / follow-up / revoked (column grants); a guard keeps job, request and
  vendor on one org; anon nothing. **The vendor's page `/b/<token>`**
  (`shared-bid.tsx`, public, no sign-in, noindex; at its foot "Sent with" the
  ScopeCrete emblem and wordmark and **Click here to find out more**, one
  link to https://scopecrete.ai — Coy, 2026-10-11; the proposal's `/p` page
  has no such footer; `/api/b/[token]` with the
  request's own client, no service role) goes through two SECURITY DEFINER
  doors, the only things anon may run: `sc_shared_bid()` (their copy of a
  live link, the opening counted unless the reader is in the org) and
  `sc_answer_bid()` (a total and/or a price per line and a note, checked
  again there, 20 answers at most, never by the org itself) — written on
  their row, as an internal note on the vendor with the job ("Bid answered —
  Kim priced … "), and Coy's follow-up marked done. Storage policy
  `shared_bid_assets` lets anon read only the photos and plans a live link
  lists (`sc_shared_bid_asset()`), signed for an hour. **Where the answers
  show:** the job's Summary (**Bids**, each vendor answered / opened / sent,
  cheapest first) and the vendor's page (first tab **Bid requests** — stats,
  each request, **Their rates**). **The vendor page's header has no Send a bid request (Coy, 2026-10-24,
  toolbar note):** the Bid requests tab lists every request sent to them, each opening it on the job's Summary
  (`?bid=`), with **+ New bid request** on the list's header, which opens the composer with them picked. Links point at scopecrete.ai
  (`publicOrigin()`); the code is on master, so they open.
  Rehearsed (rolled back) on the live data: a request and a vendor's link
  made; another org's or a made-up vendor refused; anon read the scopes by
  the token, a bad token got nothing, the listed file was readable and an
  unlisted one not, the tables refused; an empty answer refused, an answer
  with one bad line kept the good one, the note written on Kim's thread, the
  view counted once; a revoked link gone and its file with it. Not built:
  insurance papers on a vendor, and reading a price they text back
  (Dusty, later).
  **Kinds and trades — buckets inside buckets (Coy, 2026-10-24: "I need vendor groups or types; like
  buckets inside of buckets. Figure out the hiarchy" and "a place to quickly and easily modify, edit,
  rearrange this lists of groups and kinds … so that dusty can find the correct vendor easily").** One
  hierarchy, two levels under the bucket: **Vendor › Kind › Trade.** A vendor has ONE kind
  (`contacts.contact_subtype` → the org's `org_contact_subtypes` rows, bucket vendor: Subcontractor,
  Supplier, Equipment Rental, Services, Looking for work, Lead Generation …) and ANY trades
  (`contacts.trades`); each trade sits under one kind, or none (`org_vendor_trades`, **migration 129**:
  name, kind, sort_order, one name per org case aside, member RLS, anon nothing; seeded with the app's
  list under its kinds — Flatwork, Finishing, Labor … under Subcontractor, Ready-mix, Rebar & steel,
  Lumber & forms under Supplier — and every trade typed on a vendor, under no kind). Groups and types
  are not two more levels: a kind is the "type" (who they are to you — someone you hire, someone you buy
  from), a trade the "group" (what they do), and two levels are what a person scans and what Dusty
  matches ("who pumps?" → the Pumping trade, else a Subcontractor). **Settings → Vendors**
  (`/dashboard/settings/vendors`, `vendor-settings.tsx`): every kind with its vendor count — rename,
  ↑↓, × (refused while vendors are filed under it, kept switched off), + Add a kind — and its trades
  nested under it — rename, move to another kind, ↑↓, ×, + Add a trade; "Other trades" for those under
  none. Saved together (`GET/PUT /api/vendors/taxonomy`, `src/lib/vendor-taxonomy.ts` +
  `-server.ts`, tested in `tests/vendor-taxonomy.test.mjs`); a trade renamed or removed is renamed on,
  or taken off, every vendor carrying it. The **Vendors list's** column (desktop) and the **Spaces
  drawer** list All vendors, each kind, and its trades indented under it with counts, with Edit kinds &
  trades →; the Kind and Trades pickers on + Vendor and Edit offer the org's kinds and the trades
  grouped by kind, the vendor's own kind first. **Dusty**, asked about a vendor, sub, supplier or crew
  (or searching for one), is given YOUR VENDORS BY KIND AND TRADE — the kinds and trades, then each
  vendor under their kind with trades, area and 1099 (`vendorDirectory()`); a vendor's page context
  carries their trades and 1099. Before 129 kinds edit as now (no migration — they were the org's rows
  already) and trades read from the app's list, the editor saying trades wait for it.
  **A vendor's page (Coy, 2026-10-24, toolbar notes on Luis):** no Ask Dusty button; **App access** in
  ⋯ (`RecordExtras.menu`); **+ Receipt or quote** out of the header — **Receipts & quotes** is the
  page's width, lists every receipt and quote with what Dusty read and **↑ Upload a receipt or quote**
  in its header; the **Cost book** tab is the page's width with **Add or edit items** a button in its
  header, opening their cards under it. **1099 & W-9** tab: **Send them a 1099**, a switch
  (`contacts.is_1099`, 129; "1099" on the facts line and in Dusty's context); **W-9** — their W-9
  files (`files.category = 'w9'`, 129 widens the check; **Files → W-9** folder), **↑ Upload their
  W-9**, and **Ask for a W-9 by text / by email**, which put the words and the IRS form's link
  (irs.gov fw9.pdf) in their thread's text box or a new email for Coy to send (§10), and keep the day
  (`contacts.w9_requested_at`). Before 129 the tab says so and the switch and upload are off.
  **The bid request as a document (Coy, 2026-10-15: "I need the bid request page to look more like the
  proposal page with the sections showing the takeoffs … And when sending the bid request to a sub like
  Luis … it should default to all the Labor cost items … see the sq/ft or sqyd per section and the total
  sqft of the job … same for linear ft and counts. And I don't want to show the estimated amount of days";
  no migration).** One `BidDocument` (`bid-document.tsx`) draws both pages, white paper like the proposal: a
  dark band (the org · BID REQUEST and the job), For · Job site · Price by · Pour, the note, **Job totals**
  (total SF and SY — pours only when the job has any, so base under a slab isn't counted twice — LF, and
  counts by unit, `jobTotals()`), then each section numbered with its size from the engine's `quantities()`
  ("2,583.81 SF · 287.09 SY · 7" thick", "430 LF", "4 EA" — `measureLabel()`, kept on the frozen copy as
  `measure`), its takeoffs on its sheet and its photos, and its lines. **No days:** a line in days, man-days,
  hours or weeks shows no quantity (`isTimeUnit()`; `shownScopes()` writes it as null and the page hides it
  on older links too) and is priced as a total. **The composer** is two columns on a desktop — job, who,
  packet, how they price on the left, "What they see" on the right, the document itself with ticks on
  sections and lines and the supplies / they-price-it switch. **A labor sub** (trade **Labor**, first in
  `TRADES`, or filed subcontractor / looking for work — `laborVendor()`) starts with every labor line ticked
  and materials left out (`defaultPick()`), with a "Labor items only" chip to turn it off. **Maps:** each
  shown section's takeoffs are drawn in the browser (`mapPicture()`), uploaded to
  `job-files/{org}/bid/{request}/map-<key>.jpg` (`POST /api/bid-requests` returns their signed uploads) and
  listed in `assets.paths`, so the vendor's page shows them through `sc_shared_bid_asset()` as it does photos.
  **Section Notes are never sent** — they are Coy's internal notes; the packet's access line is null, and
  `/api/b/[token]` drops it from older requests. Luis was filed under Labor (trades, subtype subcontractor)
  the same day. A request sent before this keeps its frozen copy: no section sizes or maps, but its days are
  hidden.
  **Laid out for a phone (Coy, 2026-10-22: "need to be improved dramatically to be clear and simple to
  understand in a page or 2 on a phone"; no migration).** `BidDocument` reads top to bottom: "Bid request ·
  from <org>", the job, the address (a Google Maps link), **Price by Sat, Oct 24 · in 2 days** as a chip (red
  when 2 days or less or past, `dueLabel()`, Central) and the pour day, "Hi <name>, <org> would like your price
  on the work below", the note; then **The work at a glance** — one row a section, its size on the right
  (`sizeParts()`: SF with SY, thickness and the yards worked out for a pour with area and thickness,
  `sectionYards()` = SF × in ÷ 324 — never for a curb in LF, whose width isn't on it; LF; counts), a tap
  jumping to it, and a **Total** row; then **What to price**, each line with its quantity on the right ("one
  price" for a line with none), "<org> supplies — don't price" as a tag. The takeoff map (`mapUrl`, frozen when
  sent) and photos are 64px thumbnails that open full screen (‹ ›, Esc). On the vendor's link a line's price box
  sits under it full width on a phone, with "= $X" once priced, and **Your total** and **Send my price** are
  pinned to the bottom of the screen. The composer previews the same page (its live map unchanged). Tested in
  `tests/bid-document.test.mjs`.
  **Groups, what to send, and the bids side by side (Claude Design "Bid requests" handoff, 2026-10-23 — Coy
  dropped it as a zip, kept in /mnt/project-files/bid-requests/; no migration).** A section's lines on the bid
  are grouped **Labor · Materials · Equipment · Other** by cost type, as on the Budget (`groupOf()` /
  `groupLines()`, the frozen copy's lines carrying `group`; a request sent before reads as one list); in the
  composer each group has a tick that takes or leaves all its lines (tri-state, `groupState()`), and on the
  vendor's link a group shows its subtotal as they price. **With your bid, send** — chips in the composer
  (Insurance certificate, W-9, 1-year workmanship warranty, Price good for 30 days, + your own; `REQUIREMENTS`,
  `cleanRequirements()`: 8 at most) kept as `packet.requirements` (jsonb, no column) and listed at the foot of
  the vendor's page. **Compare bids** (the design's 1f) inside a sent request on the job's Summary once anyone
  has answered: one column per vendor, a row per line with their price per unit and what it comes to, a line
  a by-line vendor left out red, a price under 60% of the median of the others' on that line marked LOW, each
  section's total per vendor (a tap picks that vendor for the section; the cheapest complete one is picked to
  start), the whole bid with the cheapest complete one green (`compareBids()`). **Fill the Proposed budget**
  (asked first) writes each picked vendor's price onto that section's budget lines' unit cost through the
  line's own PATCH — a line sent with no quantity (days, one price) spread over the budget line's quantity
  (`budgetUnitCost()`) — and says how many it changed; nothing is written before that tap, and a vendor who
  sent one total has no lines to fill. Tested in `tests/bid-compare.test.mjs`. Not built from the handoff yet:
  per-line notes and a schedule answer from the vendor, a decline, the send dialog's channels per vendor
  (these need a migration), Cost book price requests, the vendor portal and the customer's code sign-in.
  **Editing a sent request (migration 124, applied 2026-10-23 on Coy's "apply 124" — checked after: RLS on, the update policy beside insert and read, authenticated may update only the seven columns and anon none, 5 requests unchanged; Coy: "I need to be
  able to go in and edit the bid request that I sent").** 091 let members insert and read a request, never change
  it. 124 grants members update on `price_mode`, `price_by`, `pour_date`, `scopes`, `packet`, `assets` and `note`
  (not `org_id`, `job_id` or who made it) under the same org check, and adds the update policy; nothing else.
  **✎ Edit bid request** inside a sent request on the job's Summary opens the composer on it — its sections,
  lines, who supplies, plans, dates, note and what to send, read back off the vendor's copy (`pickFromShown()`) —
  the job fixed. **Save changes** (`PATCH /api/bid-requests/[id]`, the POST's own build in
  `src/lib/bid-save-server.ts`) rebuilds the copy from today's budget, replaces it in place and stamps
  `packet.edited_at`, so every vendor's `/b/<token>` shows the new version with no new link; the maps are drawn
  again. Vendors added get their own link and the request's text; the ones already on it (link not off) are
  texted "updated the bid request … Same link" through Quo on that tap, or not, by a tick (`bidUpdateText()`, and
  "check your price … send it again" to anyone who answered). Answers stay on their rows; the Summary says
  "Answered before your last edit" and the vendor's page "Updated Oct 23 — you priced the earlier version" until
  they send again (`answeredBeforeEdit()`). Before 124 Save said it waits for it. **Photos one by one (Coy, 2026-10-23: two screenshots Jeremy texted, pulled onto the HPC job with his texted photos, went out with the request "and i'm not sure how to even remove them"; no migration).** Under the composer's **Site photos** chip, **Pick photos ▾** lists each photo on the job; a tap leaves one out of this request (it stays on the job), and the chip reads "Site photos · 12 of 14". Editing a sent request starts with only the photos it sent. **Photos from your files or uploaded (Coy, same day: "select photos by pull from the job or organization files or uploading my own photos to the bid request"; no migration).** Under step 3, **More photos: From your files ▾** searches every photo in the company's Files (`/api/files/search?kind=image`, a word, a job or a customer) as thumbnails, a tap adding or taking one off; **↑ Upload photos** files new ones on the job (`POST /api/files`, category photo) and adds them. Each shows as a chip with ×. The save (`bid-save-server.ts`) takes a picked id that isn't the job's only if it is an image of the org's own under RLS, and lists its path in `assets.paths` like the job's, so the vendor's link signs it; 30 files at most. Editing brings them back as chips. **When they open it (Coy, 2026-10-23: "a notification of when they open the document … on the document itself from my login"; no migration).** Each vendor's row keeps its last opening (`last_viewed_at`, 091), so: the **bell** lists "Luis opened your bid request · HPC Ascension Campus · opened 4 times · no price yet" with a pop-up as it lands (`bidOpenAlert()`, `src/lib/alerts.ts`, tested; id per opening, so a later one alerts again), opening the job's Summary with that request open (`?bid=`); **Dusty's banner** shows it for two days under its own label, Opened; and the vendor's link opened from a signed-in member's login (`own`) shows above the document "👁 Luis opened this 4 times · last opened 12 min ago · first Oct 23 8:12 AM" and whether they priced it — read under the session's RLS in `/api/b/[token]`, never sent to the vendor. Rehearsed (rolled back) as
  Sacco's owner: refused before 124; with it a member's edit saved, a change of job or org refused, a signed-in
  stranger changed nothing, anon refused. Tested in `tests/bid-edit.test.mjs`.
- **Bid requests from the budget's cost items, or by Dusty (Coy, 2026-10-24: "I need to be able to request bid requests
  from cost items in the budget page. Or have dusty do it for me. or do it from the expanded Cost Item panel that comes
  down from the cost item to edit it"; no migration).** One composer for the whole dashboard (`BidRequestHost`,
  `bid-request-host.tsx`, in the dashboard layout, not for an onsite estimator), opened by `sc:bid-request` {job, seed,
  vendors} (`requestBids()`). A cost item's drop-down panel on the Budget has **Request bids for this item ›** (its
  Vendor box), and each cost group's row (Labor · Materials · Equipment in a section, and the whole-job costs) a
  **Bids** chip for that group's lines. The composer opens on that job with only those lines and their sections ticked
  (`pickFromSeed()`, `src/lib/bids.ts`, tested in `tests/bid-from-budget.test.mjs`): budget line ids pick exactly
  those; Dusty's Price book item refs pick every line of the item; otherwise every line of the sections and cost types
  named. "Started from N cost items on the budget — only they are ticked" sits over the document with **Show
  everything**, which goes back to the usual pick (every line, or a labor sub's labor lines); another job, or Labor
  items only, does the same. The compose read now carries each line's Price book item id (`item_id`, Coy's side only —
  `shownScopes()` builds what a vendor sees field by field, so it never reaches them). **Dusty:** plan step
  `bid_request` {job — a full id, areas (8-character refs or "job"), groups (labor, materials, equipment, other),
  items (Price book item refs), vendors (full contact ids only when Coy names them)}, a record step in every context
  (`RECORD_RULES`); Apply opens the composer with them ticked and the vendors picked (closing his chat on a phone).
  Nothing is sent from either door until Coy picks who and taps Send, as from the job's Get bids, and the job
  Summary's Bids list refreshes when one is sent (`sc:bids-changed`). Not built: ticking single lines across groups on
  the budget for one request (a group's chip or one item's panel today).
- **A vendor's portal, and won / lost (migration 126, written 2026-10-23, waiting on Coy's word; Coy: "from the bid
  request page … I would like the vendor to access their portal to see the other bid request documents / projects we

  has **All your bid requests from <org> ›**, which opens `/b/<token>/all` (`vendor-portal.tsx`, `/api/b/[token]/all`,
  the request's own client, no service role). It lists every bid request that org sent that vendor (the link's
  contact), newest first, in **Open · Won · Not won** — the job's name and address, sent, price-by and pour days,
  their own price and when, a line on where it stands (`vendorStage()` / `groupPortalBids()`, `src/lib/bids.ts`,
  tested in `tests/vendor-portal.test.mjs`): Coy's mark first (You got it · Not chosen this time), else the job lost
  or archived ("The job didn't go ahead") or their link turned off (Closed) is not won; else open — "Waiting for your
  price", "Price-by day passed — you can still send it", or "Priced — waiting on the job" ("we won the job, choosing
  subs" once the job is won). Each open one opens its own link; a link turned off is listed without one. Never a cost,
  Coy's notes or another vendor's price; nothing is counted. 126 adds `bid_request_recipients.outcome` (awarded ·
  not_chosen, checked), `outcome_at`, `outcome_by`, a column grant so members may set them, and
  `sc_vendor_portal(token)` (SECURITY DEFINER, anon may run it; a live token only). Coy marks it inside a sent
  request on the job's Summary: **Award** / **Not chosen** on each vendor (a second tap clears it; `PATCH
  /api/bid-requests/recipients/[id] {outcome}`), the list row reading "✓ awarded" or "not chosen". Before 126 the
  page says it isn't switched on, the marks say they wait for it, and the bid requests read as before. Rehearsed
  (rolled back) on the live data as anon on Luis's Wiggins link: his 4 requests, one marked awarded, no other
  vendor's, a bad token nothing, the table refused. Not built: one portal across a vendor account's several
  people (116), and telling the vendor when a mark is made.
- **A customer's portal (migration 123, applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "apply 123" — checked after: RLS on both tables, 4 member policies and the portal_uploads storage policy, anon no grant on either table and may run only the three SECURITY DEFINER doors, a bad token returns nothing, 0 portals; Coy: "a
  vendor portal page. somewhere customers like Gulfline paving can go to see all the proposals that i've sent
  and bid requests they can send and upload"; decision card: "Both: link first, login after").** One private
  link per customer, `/c/<token>` on scopecrete.ai, no login (`portal-page.tsx`, `/api/c/[token]`, request's own
  client, no service role): the proposals sent to them (a live or signed link on one of their jobs, newest

  progress · Done · Not going ahead, from the job it made — `requestStage()`, `src/lib/portal.ts`, tested), and
  **Send a bid request**: job name, address, price-by day, what to price, their plans / specs / photos (each
  with a picker) and who they are (kept on their phone). Files go from the phone straight to storage as anon
  (the anon key as bearer, so a signed-in session never stands in) into `job-files/{org}/portal/{portal}/…` —
  storage policy `portal_uploads` lets anon insert only there, under a live link, 60 a day
  (`sc_portal_upload_ok()`). Then `sc_portal_request()` (SECURITY DEFINER, anon may run it; refuses the org's
  own members, 20 a day) makes the job in **New lead / Bid invite** (`status='invited'`, `origin='machine'`,
  `lead_source='gc_invite'`, counted, `bid_due_at` 5 pm Central on the day), its person the sender matched by
  email then phone among the customer's people, else the primary; each listed file (under the link's folder,

  reads it all for a live token and counts the opening unless the reader is in the org (`own` — the page then
  shows no form). Tables `customer_portals` (one live link per customer; members read, make, and may set only
  `revoked_at`; a guard keeps the customer in the org) and `portal_requests` (members read; only the door
  writes); anon has no grant on either. **On the customer page** (not for an onsite estimator) the Overview's
  **Customer portal** card: Make the link · Copy link · Text it (their person's thread with the words in the
  box) · Email it (a new email in Threads) · Open ↗ · Turn off, with how often it was opened and how many
  requests came (`/api/customers/[id]/portal` GET · POST · PATCH {revoke}, session client). Before 123 the card
  says it waits for it. Rehearsed (rolled back) on Gulfline: anon saw its three proposals, uploaded into the
  link's folder and was refused elsewhere, could not read the tables; the request made "Gulfline Paving …"
  invited with Jeremy as its person, due 5 pm Central, its plan PDF in Takeoffs, a path outside the folder
  skipped, the note written; a bad token refused. **Not built yet:** the same page inside a GC's own
  ScopeCrete login, and a GC's takeoffs and budget flowing into a bid request to a sub across companies.
- **A receipt → the vendor and their prices (2026-09-29)** —
  `src/components/v34/receipt-import.tsx`, from the Vendors list, a
  vendor's page, or Price book → Import → "A receipt, invoice or quote".
  Receipt · Invoice · Quote; **Take a photo** (camera) or **Choose a file**.
  With no vendor chosen it is saved unfiled and Dusty reads who it's from
  (§2e); `matchVendor()` (`src/lib/vendor-match.ts`, tested) matches the
  printed phone, then email (or its domain spelling the vendor's name —
  billing@angellematerials.com is Angelle Materials), then the name, then a
  distinctive first word only one vendor has — never a guess between two.
  Match → "From X", changeable, with "add the phone, email and address from
  it where their page has none". No match → **New vendor** with the details
  as printed and **Create vendor** — nothing is made without the tap; a
  number already on a contact offers to file that contact as vendor
  instead. Then the prices: repeated ticket lines folded to one row per
  item (`collapseLines()`: 10 + 10 + 3 CY → 23 CY on 3 tickets); each row
  matched by `matchItem()` (their item #, the name, a close name with every
  number the same — 3000 is never 4000) and saying **Price change** (old →
  new), **Add their price** (to an item priced by someone else or no one),
  **New item** (type / counted / group, guesses labelled) or **Same**, with
  an item picker to point it elsewhere and **Make default** (ticked for a
  new item or one with no default). Tax is shown as printed, or as
  "$399.74 on $3,807.00 — works out to 10.50% (worked out; no rate
  printed)"; it is not a price and does not change Cost settings. From a
  photo the screen says the prices could not be checked against text.
  **After Coy's first real invoice (2026-09-29):** the drawer's title is the
  document's name (✎ renames it) and its subtitle the vendor's NAME — never
  a phone number. A vendor matched by the number on the invoice but saved
  with no name (Angelle's line was one of his unnamed contacts) gets a Name
  box filled with the invoice's name, saved as Coy's on Next; the match says
  how it matched. The explanation sits behind **ⓘ**. Each row's item is
  found with a **search box** over the Price book (every word in the name,
  group or item #, grouped, 30 at most; + New item first) — never a
  427-item dropdown — and once Coy points Angelle's 8006 at his item, their
  item # is saved on their price, so the next invoice matches by # on its
  own. A new item's name is editable (Dusty's text, title-cased).
- **Price book with vendors (2026-09-29)** — the grid's Vendor column shows
  the default vendor and "+2" when others price the item; **By vendor**
  lists an item under every vendor that prices it, at that vendor's price
  (edited in the cell), ★ on the default. The card sheet: "Unit cost ·
  default vendor's", **Default vendor**, a **Vendors** section (each
  vendor's price, area, item #, date; ★ to switch; × to remove; + another
  vendor's price), **Same item as…** (merge, with Undo), and price history
  naming each price's vendor.
- **Budget (2026-09-29)** — `src/components/v34/job-budget.tsx` under the
  sections on the job page. (Its option bar is gone since 2026-10-05 — one
  price per job, §3.) **Wide (≥900px
  measured): a spreadsheet** — Item · Vendor · Qty · Unit · Unit cost · Ext
  cost · Markup · Unit price · Ext price · Margin · Tax, rows area ▸ cost
  group ▸ line with subtotals, qty / unit cost / markup edited in the cell,
  ƒ = from the rule, ✎ = typed. **Narrow: cost cards** — one per group with
  its total, lines inside, tap a line for its sheet. Footer: tax switch,
  discount, each option's cost / price / total / margin or what blocks it.
  **+ Add from cost cards**: pick the area, search, add a card or a whole
  group, or a one-off line. **The grid only when it fits (2026-10-02):** a table wider than
  its room hid its last columns behind Dusty's docked panel (Coy); when it
  would overflow, the budget shows the cards until it has more room than
  that.
