  **Each section holds its own cost items (Coy, 2026-10-04: "the sections
  that are in the budget [should] have the budget line items in the sections
  themselves … The budget below can be an additional costs panel that
  includes the OH and shows a spreadsheet of how the labor, equipment and
  materials add up for all the sections and how the Over Head and profit all
  math out").** A section's card shows its lines — its Price book groups as
  sub-groups, then the items, then the section's total and $/SF — with **+ Add
  items from the Price book** (drawn there by `JobBudget` through a portal
  into the card's slot; no data change, one set of numbers). The panel under
  the sections is **Job totals**: options, **Additional costs · whole job**
  (lines on no section — mobilization, a permit, a pump day; **+ Whole-job
  cost**), tax / O.H. / margin / discount, each option's total, then **How it
  adds up** — a row per cost type (Materials, Labor, Equipment, Subcontract,
  Other) with each section's cost, then cost, O.H., profit and price — and the
  steps Cost → + O.H. → + Profit → Price → + tax − discount → Total (`Rollup`;
  for the option picked, else the first). The profit split and job minimum stay
  under it. **A section's Budget folds (Coy, 2026-10-05: "let the budget panel
  be colapsable")** — ▸ / ▾ Budget on its card hides the lines and keeps the
  section's total and + Add; folded sections are remembered on the device
  (`sc:budget-folded`).
  **Cost analysis and folded sections (Coy, 2026-10-08: "redesign the budget so that it's easier to
  read and more effective for … building a profitable and realistic budget"; brief
  `job-page/claude-design-brief.md`).** "How it adds up" is replaced by **Cost analysis**
  (`cost-analysis.tsx`, maths in `src/lib/cost-analysis.ts`, tested): rows Materials · Labor ·
  Equipment · Miscellaneous (cost type *other*) · Subcontract (only when a job has one) · Overhead,
  then Net profit and Price, each with its share of the price, tapped open to its lines by section;
  **$ · /SF · /SY · /LF** (LF from sections measured in linear feet, never mixed into SF) for the
  whole job or one section (a section carries no tax or discount); **gross profit** = price −
  discount − direct cost, **net profit** = gross − O.H. (the tier's profit), in all, a day and a
  man-day; **Crew · Days · Hours a day** (8 unless set) → man-days, man-hours, SF a crew day,
  labor per man-hour, and the job minimum cleared or short. Unpriced lines are left out and named.
  Crew size and hours are `jobs.crew_size` / `crew_hours` (**migration 093**, additive,
  applied 2026-10-09 on Coy's "Apply 093"; checked after: both columns nullable, both range checks
  validated, RLS on jobs and its 5 policies unchanged, 49 jobs untouched; without it
  `GET /api/jobs/[id]` returns `crew: null` and the boxes say so).
  Days stay `duration_days`. Sections **fold to one line** — name, size and thickness, price and
  $/SF or $/LF, an orange dot when something is missing — remembered per job on the device
  (`sc:open-sections:<job>`; a job with one section opens it); Remove section moved to the card's
  foot and asks first; the Job totals header lost its explanation line; the desktop rail adds net
  and gross a day.
  **A section's groups are its cost types (Coy, 2026-10-21: "Any time a budget section is created it needs to default with cost groups: Labor, materials, equipment … a place to set the default groups … this same grouping transposes directly into the job totals"; no migration).** A section's lines are grouped by cost type, no longer by Price book group (the Price book group stays on each line as its cost code). Labor · Materials · Equipment show on every section even empty, each with + Add, then any other type it has lines of; whole-job costs show only the types they have. Which groups every section shows is **Settings → Cost book → Section cost groups**, for the whole company since **migration 118** (applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "apply 118" — checked after: the column, its check, RLS and the 2 policies unchanged): `org_money_settings.section_groups` (text[], known cost types only, null = the defaults) through `/api/settings/money`; a person's own saved list (`user_preferences.prefs.section_groups`) is read only while the company has none (`src/lib/section-groups.ts`, tested). Cost analysis already totals the job by the same cost types.
  **Remove a cost item (Coy, 2026-10-15: "A delete icon on desktop. Swipe left on mobile to review a delete button").** Every budget line, in a section or under Additional costs · whole job, has a trash icon on a desktop (cards and grid) and swipes left on a touch screen to **Delete**; both ask first and go through the line's DELETE, as the line sheet's Take off the budget does.
  **A group's kind and one vendor for it (Coy, 2026-10-15: "an identifier for the group as labor or materials …" and "select entire cost Groups … and choose the vendor for all of those items … ask dusty to find and select the vendors"; no migration).** A budget group's header shows its kind from its lines' cost types (Labor, Materials …, or Sub · mixed when several), a **Vendor for all** pick that pins every Price book line in it to that vendor's price (giving the vendor the line's price first when they don't price the item yet), and the emblem asking Dusty to find vendors for the group as vendor_price / new_vendor steps for Coy to apply.
  **A line's cost code and type (Coy, 2026-10-15).** Under each budget line's name in the grid: its cost code, read off the Price book group's leading number ("2800 Concrete" → 2800; JobTread's codes became groups in 050), then its cost type. No migration.
  **Your price against the range, in the totals box (Coy, 2026-10-15: "merge them … so I can see where I sit in the range and understand how that range was calculated").** Market rate's price card (`PriceVsRange`, `market-rates.tsx`) now says the total and the concrete SF it divides, marks the typical price on the bar and says how the range was worked out (the middle of N installed prices per SF near the city, when, from which sites); the job's dark totals box (and Dusty's docked panel) carries the same card under the budget (`RailMarket`). No migration.
  **The dark totals box on every budget tab (Coy, 2026-10-15, eight desktop notes).** The job's rail (and Dusty's docked panel) shows the price-vs-range card on Market rate, Proposed budget and Actual costs alike, priced or not, refetched as the budget changes; the Market rate tab no longer draws its own copy. The total area and the price are 28px, the area's unit and sections line 13px, and cost (red), O.H. (yellow) and profit with margin (green) are one coloured line at 14px. Under the price: crew days (typed, else worked out from the labor lines, `days_auto`), crew size and the labor budget (labor lines' cost), then the job minimum when it is set. The going-rate explanation wraps. Under the coloured line a **Margin** slider (0–60%) moves the price and the green profit as it is dragged, saved as the job's margin on release (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id]/budget {margin_pct}`), and the price-vs-range card's $/SF marker and Under / Inside / Over move with it while dragging (`sc:margin-preview`), and O.H. shows its % beside its dollars (Coy, 2026-10-15).
  **Compare vendors (Coy, 2026-10-15: "a vendor options list in the Vendor column that opens a vendor intelligence panel in the budget … Dusty can pull the cost data from my phone conversation with the vendor or pull it from the vendor website"; no migration).** A Price book line's Vendor cell is a button (its vendor, and "· N ▾" when several price the item) opening the line's sheet, whose Vendor box adds **Compare vendors** (`VendorCompare`, `job-budget.tsx`): every `cost_item_sources` row for the item, cheapest first, the lowest marked, **Use** pinning that price to this line; a box for other vendors to check (Casco …) and **Research prices**, which sends fixed words to Dusty (`vendorCompareQuestion()`, `src/lib/dusty/vendor-compare.ts`, tested) asking each vendor's current price from Coy's calls, texts, quotes or their website and other local sellers, as `vendor_price` (and `new_vendor`) steps marked estimate for Coy to apply. The grid's old vendor select is gone.
  **Dusty fills a section (Coy, 2026-10-15: "an AI emblem specifically to add budget items into the section based on the dimensions/takeoffs … the psi … ready mix per cubic yard with the fuel-surcharges … the steel requirements … wire mesh or rebar … if it's not in the cost book, it can create it … suggest edits to the cost item").** The emblem on a section's Budget row, beside + Add item, opens Dusty and sends fixed words (`sectionItemsQuestion()`, `src/lib/dusty/section-items.ts`, tested) naming the section and its area ref: find the psi, thickness and steel in the specs and plans, add ready mix by the yard with its fuel surcharge and per-load fees as their own items, mesh or rebar and the rest of the scope; use Price book items first, else `new_item`, and `update_item` for a close item missing information; say where each spec came from and mark estimates. He answers with a plan (§3 Dusty plans budgets) Coy applies. No model call until the tap; no migration.
  **Any section can carry a pour (Coy, 2026-10-15: "Some groups don't have a width and depth and concrete volume").** A section whose work type is demolition or prep (often guessed from a takeoff group's name, and the kind picker left the row on 2026-10-10) had no width, thickness or yards. Its size row now has **+ Concrete** / **✓ Concrete**: on sets the work type to slab and shows Width (linear), Thickness and Concrete; off sets it to demo. Count sections have none. No migration. Several size rows per section adding together (typed and from takeoffs, each its own depth) is the next step if wanted; it needs a table of its own.
  **Size rows (migration 111, applied 2026-10-15 on Coy's "apply 111" — checked after: RLS on, one member policy, the guard trigger, anon no select; Coy: "Build size rows").** A section can hold several sizes that add up — 30 × 40 at 4" and a 600 SF apron at 6", or curb runs of different widths — in `job_section_sizes` (label, mode dims · area · linear · count, the sizes, a linear row's width in inches, its depth; RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`, anon nothing, a guard keeping the section on the row's job and org). **+ Add a size** under the section's Measured by row: the first tap turns the section's own size into the first row ("Main") and adds a blank one; while it has rows the single size cells hide and the rows show, each with its SF / LF / count and yards, then the total. The server writes their total onto the section's own fields (`sizeTotals()` / `sizePatch()`, `src/lib/section-sizes.ts`, tested in `tests/section-sizes.test.mjs`): SF summed, thickness the one that gives the summed yards (yards × 324 ÷ SF); LF summed with the one width and depth that give the same plan area and yards; counts in one unit. So the engine, budget, Dusty and proposal read the section unchanged. A section that follows its takeoffs adds them as one more row (`syncTakeoffSections()` and `syncSectionSizes()`, `takeoff-sections-server.ts`). A row of another kind is kept but not added, and the yards wait until every row has its thickness. API `/api/jobs/[id]/sections/[sid]/sizes` (GET, POST, PATCH `{row}`, DELETE `?row=`), session client. Before 111 the button says it waits for it.
  **Product sections, notes and quantities (Coy, 2026-10-15, toolbar notes on HPC).** An LF section counts as a pour only when its work type is curb, footing, slab, sidewalk, driveway or patio (`isLinearPour()`, engine); a takeoff group named for a joint, sealant, saw cut, dowel or mesh, or any linear group otherwise, starts as "other", so it asks no width, depth or yards (+ Concrete turns it into a curb pour). A section's Notes show **Save** while they hold unsaved words (leaving the box still saves). A cost item's note folds to one line with ▸ and opens in full with ▾, each on its own; (the Expand / Collapse notes button was taken off 2026-10-18, Coy: "not fully built … can be removed"). A section line with nothing typed takes the section's count or LF, else its SF (not for Each or lump sum), shown ƒ; a typed number wins (`ruleQty()`).
  **Days from the sections, crew under Labor (Coy, 2026-10-15: "Days on the job should be an automatic calculation from the sections. The crew size and days should be shown on the 'Job Totals' section under the labor portion").** `daysFromLines()` (`src/lib/cost-analysis.ts`, tested): each labor line counted in days adds its days (sections are worked one after another), one in hours or man-hours adds hours ÷ (crew × hours a day, 8 unless set; crew 1 when not set), rounded up to the half day. There is no production rate per SF, so a section with no labor line in time adds nothing. Cost analysis uses it (ƒ) while `duration_days` is blank; a typed number wins, with ↺ to go back. Crew · Days · Hours a day sit under the Labor row of the breakdown, not in the right column; the scope rail no longer has a Days box beside a budget (it says where they are). The job minimum on the rail and Summary still reads the stored days. No migration.
  **The takeoff group on the section's row (same day: "This takeoff detail should be included on the same line as the section header").** The heading above a group's sections is gone; each section's header row shows its group's colour and name (with the group's section count and total when it holds several).
  **Since later that night (Coy, from his phone: "I want the icon with its unique color of the type of measurement … to the left of the section name and the green function symbol"):** a section tied to a takeoff group shows, before its name, the group's kind (a square for an area, a line, a dot for a count) in the group's colour and a green ƒ; the group's name and total show on the header only while the section is open.
  **Drag to reorder (Coy, 2026-10-15; reworked the same night: "I thought I would be able to click and drag it to an area and the 2 other sections separate and it snaps in when the click is released"):** every section, folded or open, has a grip at its left. Dragging it lifts the card under the pointer, the cards it passes slide aside to open a gap, the page scrolls near the screen's edge, and on release it snaps into the gap and each section whose place changed is saved with its `sort_order` (`PATCH …/sections/[id]`, no migration). Sections are drawn in their own order now, not bunched by takeoff group (the group is named on each row), so a section measured in Takeoffs moves like any other. Pointer events and transforms set on the cards, no library.
  down moves it in the list, and on release each section whose place changed is saved with its
  `sort_order` (`PATCH …/sections/[id]`, no migration).
  **Budget v3 — Market rate · Proposed budget · Actual costs (Coy, 2026-10-09; Claude Design
  "Budget v3").** Three tabs under the Budget, in the address (`?tab=market` / `?tab=actuals`, none =
  the proposed budget): on a phone they hang from the Budget button like Takeoffs' Files ·
  Measurements; on a desktop they open a tools row under the name, the emblem on the job's tabs row above (the tools row's own left 2026-10-10, Coy: "just one in the header"), not at
  its right (`budget-tabs.tsx`). They look like Takeoffs' Files · Measurements — words with the accent underline, not buttons — and a hanging row is centred on the screen, its branch reaching across from its button (Coy, 2026-10-09: "looks more symmetrical"; every row since later that day, Takeoffs' too). **Proposed budget** is the budget above, unchanged, kept mounted
  under the other tabs. **Market rate** (`market-rates.tsx`, `GET/POST /api/jobs/[id]/budget/market`,
  maths in `src/lib/budget-v3.ts`, tested): the job's price per SF against the installed going-rate
  range (the median of the sources' middles as typical), past jobs of the same type that have actual
  costs, and each recipe step's going rate beside its lines — under / inside / over (2% either way),
  unpriced, no data. An *installed* rate is compared with the line's price and a *cost* rate with its
  cost; **Use low / Use high** writes the line's unit cost (an installed price turned back into a cost
  at the job's O.H. and margin, price × (1 − margin) ÷ (1 + O.H.)) and marks it Estimate — "going
  rate …" (Undo puts both back); only on a step with one line. Rates are the draft build's
  (`made.rates`) or a lookup from the tab (`searchRates()`, the same web search and model read, kept
  in `market_lookups`). A rate never prices anything by itself. **The installed range (2026-10-09, Coy: "why does it say that it's not looked up, even though I tapped look up?").** The whole-driveway search did find Baton Rouge prices, but the model filed "$6 to $12 per square foot … 4-inch broom finish" and "$12 to $22 … 4-inch slab" on the gravel **base** step, and the rest as whole-job dollar totals, so the card's per-SF range was empty. Now the prompt says what each step is, `restep()` (`src/lib/draft/rates.ts`, tested) moves an installed per-SF or per-SY price for a driveway, slab or patio whose words don't fit its step onto the job (applied to old lookups as they are read too), and `jobRange()` reads per SY ÷ 9 and a whole-job total over the one size its own quote states ("$4,556 to $6,165 for a 500 sq ft driveway"), leaving out material-only or labor-only prices. The card says "none per SF in the <time> lookup" when a lookup found none, and its range with the typical price and how many sources. The top **Look up** also looks up up to four sections (unlooked first) side by side. **Actual costs**
  (`actual-costs.tsx`, `/api/jobs/[id]/actuals`): each cost type and line, budget against actual —
  a line with nothing filed is held at its budget in every sum, so a half-filed job doesn't read as
  under — with filters All · Over budget · Not filed and a section; costs filed on a line (a receipt
  from the job's Files, a bill, crew time as man-hours × rate, or typed), Mark paid, Unfile;
  receipts with Dusty's read and a suggested line (`suggestLine()`: vendor and words), Not on the
  budget, As a bill; change orders (price and cost, Mark signed — sold price = price − discount +
  change orders); crew and days budgeted against actual; Net actual = sold − actual direct cost −
  the budgeted O.H., a day, and the margin. Filing is on the page, Coy's tap; Dusty does not read
  actuals yet. **Migration 094 (applied 2026-10-09 on Coy's "Apply 094"; checked after: the three tables with RLS, their policies and guard triggers, anon no select, the three `jobs.actual_*` columns)**: `job_costs`,
  `change_orders`, `market_lookups` (select and insert only), `jobs.actual_days` /
  `actual_crew_size` / `actual_hours`, RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`, anon nothing, a guard keeping
  each line and file on its job. Until it is applied Actual costs shows a banner and saves nothing,
  and Market rate looks rates up only through an existing draft build.
  **Market rate by section, and the draft from the plans (Coy, 2026-10-09: "the market rates page
  needs to pull in the sections … created automatically from the plan sets").** Market rate opens
  with **By section**: every section of the job (the plan set's first), its size in its own unit
  (LF when measured so, EA when counted, else SF), its items' price and what they come to per unit,
  its sheets (a tap opens the sheet with its line boxed, below), and the going rate looked up for
  that section — **Look up going rates** searches each section by its own name and unit near the
  job's city, four a tap (`sectionQueries()`, `SECTION_RATES_SYSTEM`, `searchSectionRates()`;
  the model gives each price to a section by key, `sanitizeRates()` keeps only those keys), kept in
  `market_lookups` as `kind: "sections"`, or before 094 on the latest draft build
  (`made.section_rates`), or shown once when there is neither. `sectionRows()`
  (`src/lib/budget-v3.ts`, tested): installed prices against the price per unit, else costs
  against the cost per unit; a per-SY rate reads per SF ÷ 9; No size, No items, No rate say so.
  **The Draft budget box (Coy's notes the same day):** the "They have 3 other open jobs …" line is
  gone; a bid job's sections with no size are a **To measure — from the plans** list, each with its
  sheet buttons and the bid's line; when nothing but the job's description was read (no calls or
  texts about the work) the reading's areas, questions and "said two ways" are left out on a job
  with plan sections; elsewhere a question shows the line it came from and where ("the job's
  description, not a plan sheet"); "said two ways" is a list, not a red paragraph; and what blocks
  Approve is listed under "Before you can approve it:".
- **The Plan Room (migration 059, 2026-09-30; Claude Design,
  `design/plan-room`).** Coy: *"scopecrete.ai will be a tool for me to do
  takeoffs manually."* (His own Hermes agent reads the bid emails he forwards
  to intake-concrete@agentmail.to; ScopeCrete will only mirror that inbox —
  next.) `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/plans`, opened from the job header's
  **Plan room** button; the rail's Jobs stays lit and Dusty is HOT on the job.
  Four steps — three since 2026-10-05, when Files and Sheets became one
  **Files** tab (Coy: "one place where planset pdf, photos, images and any
  other file can live"): a folder column (All files · Photos, then the
  sheets by Concrete scope · All drawings · each discipline · Pages to
  read) beside what the folder holds; `?step=sheets` still opens it on the
  sheets. Plans are added from the Files tab — the header's ↑ Add plans
  button is gone (Coy, 2026-10-09). Since
  2026-10-08 the steps are **Files · Satellite · Takeoff** (Coy: the Aerial
  button "as a tab in between 'files' and 'takeoff'", the page "nest[ed]
  inside … under the page header"); the Photos button and panel are gone
  (texted photos come in from Files → Photos, below), and so is the
  "N of M sheets scaled" chip — it sits at the start of Takeoff's sheet
  strip under the drawing. **Later that day the Satellite tab left the Plan Room for the Map
  page** (Coy: "push the snipits from the map into the takeoffs tool … That
  can replace the current satellite page in the plan room"): the tab opens
  `/dashboard/map?clip=<job>`, framing that job's site on satellite, and an old
  `?step=satellite` link goes there too (`aerial-picker.tsx` deleted). See
  **The Map page** below. **Then the tab went (Coy, same day: "Satellite tab can be removed
  completely since we have the map page"):** Takeoffs is **Files · Takeoff**; an aerial comes from
  the Map's Clip for takeoff, and an old `?step=satellite` link still opens it.
  **Files and Takeoffs are two job views (Coy, 2026-10-10: "take the 'Files' tab out of the
  'Takeoffs' page and make it it's own page between Summary and Takeoffs" and "the 'measurements'
  tab will be merged with the main 'takeoffs' page since the takeoffs page will not have
  sub-tabs").** The job's switch is Summary · **Files** · Takeoffs · Budget · Proposal. Files
  (`/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/files`) is what Takeoffs → Files was — the folders, sheets, photos,
  Pull satellite images — and a sheet's Measure › opens Takeoffs on it; Takeoffs
  (`/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/plans`) is the drawing alone, `?sheet=` picking one. Both are the same
  `PlanRoom` (`view`), so reading a plan set's pages and OCR run from either. An old
  `?step=files` / `?step=sheets` link on Takeoffs goes to Files; `?step=takeoff` still opens the
  drawing. An onsite estimator with Plan room gets both (`redirectFor()`). The Scope Builder's
  Files step opens Files. What follows about Takeoffs' Files · Takeoff steps is history.
  **A drawing opens large first (Coy, 2026-10-08: "view the files large so that I can inspect
  and read whats on the sheets before I do the measurements").** A sheet tapped in Files (its
  drawings by discipline) opens in the page reader full screen — fit to width, − / + up to 6×,
  drag to look around, ‹ › through the sheets of that folder — with **Measure ›**, which opens
  Takeoff on it (`PageReader` with `onMeasure`, `files-sheets.tsx`). Only that sheet is drawn.
  **List or large icons, and delete (Coy, 2026-10-09).** Every folder of Takeoffs → Files shows as
  a list or as large icons (`LookSwitch`, kept on the device `sc:takeoff-files-look`; a PDF's
  icon is its first page, not drawn on a phone), and each file and photo has a trash icon — the
  Files page's own delete, asked first; a plan set with measurements on its sheets is refused by
  name until they are deleted or it is switched out of Takeoffs. **Parameters** sits beside
  ☰ Sheets on a phone and, like Sheets, slides in from the left over the drawing (Coy, 2026-10-09) —
  one drawer open at a time; picking a takeoff closes it on that shape. The desktop keeps its column.
  **The Sheets drawer labels a sheet as Files does** — number and title, then the page and its file —
  **and × takes one out of Takeoffs** (Coy, 2026-10-09): refused while it has measurements;
  `DELETE /api/plan-sheets/[id]` deletes the sheet row only (the plan file stays in Files), Undo puts the
  page back with its scale (its OCR is read again); a file's only sheet takes the file out of Takeoffs
  instead. In the large reader **Clip detail** and **Measure** are icons on a phone.
  The takeoff is measured to end at the footer's top, so the drawer's + Group / Send buttons sit just
  above the main menu and only its list scrolls (Coy, 2026-10-09). **Section cuts clipped onto budget
  sections (Coy, 2026-10-09: "expose the section cuts on the plans and just extract snippets … push them
  into sections … the depth and the shape of the footings, how much rebar, what size rebar, where it goes
  … the wire mesh").** In the large reader a drawing has **Clip detail**: drag a box round a section cut
  or detail (mouse or finger), pick the budget section (or + New section, or **Job files — no section**: Coy, 2026-10-15, the clip on the
  job alone, in its Files and Photos, noted on and given a section later from its Area) and **Save clip** — the box is
  drawn sharp from the PDF (`renderRegion`, ~2,400 px a side at most), saved as a JPEG `files` row on that
  section, `category='plan'`, with the sheet and box in `source_url` as `plan_detail:<sheet>#x,y,w,h`
  (page points; no migration — `src/lib/plan-details.ts`, tested in `tests/plan-details.test.mjs`;
  `POST /api/files {detail}` checks the sheet is on the section's job and the box on its page). A clip is
  not queued for the background reader and never shows on the proposal (area photos leave out
  `category='plan'`). On the job page each section has **Details from the plans** (`section-details.tsx`,
  under its Photos, which leave clips out): thumbnails by sheet; a tap shows it large with **Open the
  sheet ›** and **Read it with AI** — `POST /api/files/[id]/detail` sends the picture in one tool-less
  call straight to the model API (`modelJson()`, `DETAIL_MODEL`, default Claude Opus 5.5 — never Hermes),
  and `cleanDetailRead()` keeps only values in range with the words they were read from: the detail's
  title and kind, footing / beam width and depth, slab, base, psi, each bar (count, size, spacing,
  continuous / ties / each way / dowels, where), mesh, vapor barrier, notes. Saved on the file
  (`facts.detail`, `summary`), so Files and Dusty see it. **Use** buttons put a value on the section on
  Coy's tap — on an LF section the beam's width (in `width_ft`, as §3) and depth, else the slab
  thickness; psi; the bars and mesh as the section's Rebar note. **Worked out for this section**
  (`detailQuantities()`): concrete CY (LF × width × depth; slab SF × thickness), continuous bars in 20'
  sticks with laps of 40 diameters and their weight, ties by spacing (and LF of bar when a tie length is
  read), a slab grid each way, mesh in 5'×10' sheets laid with 6" laps — each saying what it assumes,
  and what it still needs (the section's LF or area). It prices nothing: the counts are for Coy to put
  on the budget from the Price book (budget rules for continuous bars and ties are not built). **Togal-style toolbar and wheel zoom (Coy, 2026-10-10: "zoom in and out of the plans with
  my scroll wheel on desktop … icons for each of the tools … take a lesson from Togal.ai").** Takeoff's
  tools each have an icon (with the word beside it from md up, the icon alone on a phone), sit in
  groups — Select · Area Rect Fill Cutout · Linear · Count · Scale — and each has a key (V, A, R, F,
  X, L, C, S; shown in its tooltip; not while a shape is being drawn). The greyed-out Auto button is
  gone. Zoom is one group: − · % · + · **Fit**. **The scroll wheel zooms where the pointer is**
  (`useWheelZoom()`, `src/lib/v34/use-pinch.ts`; shift + wheel scrolls sideways; ctrl is no longer
  needed). **Takeoffs → Files on a desktop:** the folder column folds to a strip (« / », kept on the
  device `sc:takeoff-folders-folded`), and a sheet folder has a third look, **Sheets to read** —
  each drawing shown whole at half the page's width, 1,400 px sharp, drawn as it scrolls near, its
  number, title, scale and **Measure ›** above it, a tap opening it large (`Thumb` with `width` /
  `aspect`; `pageThumb()` caches by width). A phone keeps List and Large icons (it draws no sheet
  pictures). **Pinch to zoom** (two fingers)
  on the drawing in Takeoff, the large reader and the job's sheet viewer (`usePinchZoom()`,
  `src/lib/v34/use-pinch.ts`): the point between the fingers stays put, the page is resized at once
  and drawn sharp when the zoom settles, and lifting the fingers never adds a point.
  **Files** — every file on the job; PDFs and images carry **In plan room**
  (`job_files.in_plan_room`); drop plan sets to add them.
  **Sheets** — each in-room PDF is opened in the browser with **pdf.js**
  (`pdfjs-dist` legacy build — the modern one needs JavaScript most browsers
  don't have yet — and its worker from our own bundle, no CDN;
  `src/lib/pdf.ts`). Each page's text is read for its title block
  (`src/lib/plan-sheets.ts`, tested): sheet number, title, discipline from
  the prefix, and the printed scale. A first reading, kept apart from Coy's
  own (`number_source`). Several different scales and none in the title
  block → "set it with the Scale tool". Thumbnails are drawn in memory, never
  stored. **Scanned sets render (2026-10-02):** pdf.js is given its image
  decoders (JBIG2, JPEG 2000 — `wasmUrl`), standard fonts, CMaps and ICC
  profiles from `public/pdfjs/`, copied out of `pdfjs-dist` before
  `dev`/`build` (`scripts/copy-pdfjs-assets.mjs`, not committed); without
  them a scanned drawing was a white page. Burgess Ave's `PLANS.pdf` is 74
  pages, each ONE 1-bit CCITT G4 image of ~10,191 × 6,603 px on a 34×22 in
  sheet — and pdf.js v5 decodes CCITT through the same wasm module, so it
  was blank everywhere (reproduced on a copy of that shape: 0 painted
  pixels without, the drawing with). Phones also refused the ~67 MP image:
  pdf.js is told the canvas limit (`canvasMaxAreaInBytes`; ~16.7 MP on a
  touch device, ~33.5 MP on a desktop) so the worker shrinks it first, and a
  zoomed page canvas is capped the same way (sharpness gives, never the
  picture). `pdf-probe` (edge function, worker token, returns encodings and
  sizes only) is how the file was diagnosed. The Plan Room lists only the
  sheets of files that are **In plan room** (and any sheet with a shape on
  it); taking a file out takes its pages with it.
  **A big scanned set without crashing the tab (Coy, 2026-10-07: "The LA
  Contracting plan sets … are crashing the browser when it tries to read them
  all").** pdf.js keeps a page's decoded picture until the page is cleaned
  up, and a scanned sheet is one ~67 MP image. Nothing cleaned up: the Sheets
  grid drew every thumbnail at once, and the OCR reader kept every sheet it
  had read. Burgess Ave had grown to 154 drawings (an addendum email copied
  PLANS.pdf in a second time — the same bytes, its own 74 sheets — plus
  ADD02). Measured on a 30-sheet scan of the same shape in headless
  Chromium: thumbnails all at once peaked at 2.9 GB; the reader held 1.8 GB
  after 30 sheets and kept climbing. Now `releasePage()` (`src/lib/pdf.ts`)
  lets go of each sheet once read; `renderRegion()` and thumbnails clean up
  after themselves; `drawPage()` lets go of the sheet it drew last when
  another is drawn; thumbnails are drawn two at a time (one on a phone) and
  only once they scroll near the screen. Same test: 1.4 GB and 1.1 GB peaks,
  flat however many sheets. **On a phone no thumbnail is drawn at all** (Coy,
  same day: still crashing): pdf.js decodes the whole ~67 MP scan even for a
  thumbnail, ~300 MB for a moment each whatever the canvas limit (measured),
  so the sheet list shows its number and title and only the sheet opened is
  drawn. **An addendum doesn't copy a file again** (`reuseCopy()` in
  `bid-intake`): a Box file another bid of the org already copied (same Box
  file id and size, the copy not deleted) is that copy, its reading carried
  over (deployed as `bid-intake` v14, 2026-10-10, on Coy's word; read back
  equal to the repo). Burgess Ave's second PLANS.pdf and proposal (from the
  addendum) were soft-deleted on Coy's "remove burgess duplicates".
  **Drawings vs pages to read (Coy, 2026-10-02).** A page bigger than legal
  (longest side ≥ 1,150 pt, so 11×17 half-size sets count) is a drawing:
  measured, scaled, in Takeoff. A letter or legal page bound into a plan
  file is a **page to read** — listed under "Pages to read" and opened in a
  plain reader (fit width, zoom, previous / next), never offered for
  takeoff, unless Coy sets a scale on it (`isDrawing()`, `shared.ts`,
  tested). Coy suggested "no scale found" as the test; it can't be — a
  scanned set never has a readable scale, and those ARE the drawings.
  **Reading scanned sheets — OCR in the browser (migration 064, Coy
  2026-10-02: "option A … I just want to see if I can get some dimensions
  off of plans accurately"; the VPS service, option B, later).** A scan has no
  text, and a Supabase function has ~2 s of CPU, so the Plan Room reads it:
  tesseract.js (worker, LSTM cores and the `best_int` English data copied
  into `public/tesseract/` with pdf.js's files by
  `scripts/copy-static-assets.mjs` — our own origin, no CDN), PSM 11 (sparse
  text), 300 dpi. `src/lib/v34/plan-ocr-runner.ts` is one module-level
  reader (outside React, so it keeps going while Coy moves around the
  dashboard and never re-renders the layout; it stops on a reload, and the
  next Plan Room visit carries on): **1 · title blocks** of every scanned
  drawing — the right strip and bottom band flat, and the right edge turned
  a quarter (DOTD runs the title up the side) — then each sheet's title is
  chosen against the whole set (`chooseTitles()`): what is on ~every sheet
  is boilerplate (the firm, the project, the parish, the engineer, read
  loosely — OCR spells a name several ways), the seal and the review /
  initials boxes are never titles, and flat-vs-sideways is decided once for
  the set by which is lettered bigger; **2 · whole sheets** in 2,400 px tiles
  at 3× (~216 dpi — at 2× table totals were lost), as many parts at once as
  there are readers (three on a desktop, one on a phone), a tile under 60 %
  confidence read again at 4×, civil and structural first; a part that
  takes over 45 s is skipped, the reader restarted, and the sheet says which
  parts weren't read (on the dense profile sheet some parts never finish —
  a longer wait only cost minutes — so such a sheet is read in part). Words are kept in page points (`ocr_words`), the
  text as rows with " | " between columns so a table row reads
  "104+55 | RT | DRIVE | 39" (`ocr_text`), mean confidence (`ocr_conf`).
  **A sheet is read once** (stamped `ocr_title_at` / `ocr_at`); a desktop
  starts the rest on its own, a **phone only on a tap** ("Read the rest" —
  it crashed mobile browsers, Coy 2026-10-02), with no 4× re-reads there;
  **Pause** holds it for the tab; the Plan Room shows "36 of 74 scanned
  sheets read".
  Saved per sheet as it finishes (`POST /api/plan-sheets/[id]/ocr`: never
  over a number or title Coy typed; a printed scale is stored as printed,
  applied only by his tap). **Where it shows:** the activity line (header on
  a desktop, the bar on a phone) — "Reading sheet titles · Burgess Ave … ·
  12 of 74", then "Reading the plans · … · sheet 3 of 74 · part 5/12" — and
  a chip in the Plan Room header; the Sheets step refreshes as titles land.
  **Dusty** gets whole OCR'd sheets (the most concrete-heavy first, ~24k
  characters, the rest listed by sheet), told they are readings off a scan
  — a "+" can read as "4" (104+55 → 104455) — to quote the sheet and have
  Coy check a number there before he prices it. Nothing read is a
  measurement; quantities come from the takeoff. **Checked on Burgess Ave's
  real sheets** (three pages rebuilt from the stored scans with
  `pdf-probe`'s `{"image": n}`, processed in scratch only): titles "TITLE
  SHEET", "SUMMARY TABLES", "PROFILE"; the summary sheet's Proposed Concrete
  Drives table read "TOTAL | 450" (Dylan's email: 450 SY), 426 words at 88 %;
  ~6 s a sheet on a desktop with three readers, ~15 s on a phone's one;
  the dense profile sheet read at 54 % with three parts timed out. Sheet
  numbers on DOTD sets are plain digits ("3"), which the reader does not
  take for a sheet number — they show as p.3. `findDimensions()` finds
  12'-6", 5'-0", 6" in what was read (not the 2 of 1/2") for later use. **A CAD set whose lettering is drawn as lines is read the same way (2026-10-10, Holden Pedestrian Bridge — Dusty said he couldn't read the 26 sheets).** Its only text is the plot stamp ("Sep 22, 2026 - 12:04pm") and the drawing file's path, which the title reader took as titles, so the sheets looked digital and OCR never started. `isStampTitle()` (`src/lib/plan-sheets.ts`, tested; copied into bid-intake's `sheets.ts`, waiting on a deploy) keeps them out of titles; a sheet with no number and only such a title is read by OCR, which clears the stamp. Dusty's job context says how many sheets are still waiting and where Coy starts the reading (**Plan sheets not read yet**), instead of telling him to paste notes. **Its title blocks, read right (Coy, same day: four sheets filed Electrical whose title says "Storm Water Pollution Prevention Plan Best Management Practices", and "Miscellaneous Retaining Wall Details" filed Landscape).** A city standard plan's title sits at ~80% across and down, outside the corner the reader looked in, so the biggest thing left was the DESIGNED / DRAWN row's names ("G. CHENG"), and the sheet number was read off the drawing ("SECTION THRU L3" → L3) or a mark on nine sheets ("E965" → electrical). Now: a title lettered over two to four lines is one title (`stacks()`), a candidate just above a DESIGNED / DRAWN / CHECKED / APPROVED row counts (`nearSignOff()`), initials-and-surname, "STANDARD PLAN", "NOT TO SCALE" and a run ending in ":" are no title (`titleLike()`); the number is read only in the title corner, a number read on a fifth of the set (3+) is taken back off, and a sheet with no number of its own takes its discipline from its title's words (`disciplineFromTitle()`: retaining wall, foundation, bridge → structural; storm water, drainage, sidewalk → civil). Holden's readings were cleared to be read again. Discipline filter, "Concrete scope" = civil + structural.
  **The sheets slide in on a phone (Claude Design master package
  2026-10-03, phone drawers §1).** In Takeoff on a phone the drawing gets the
  screen: a row with **☰ Sheets · N** and the current sheet (scale set /
  not), and the sheet list is a drawer that slides over the drawing from the
  left (`SlideDrawer` in `primitives.tsx`: scrim, Esc, a pull tab, swipe to
  open or close, still under reduced motion) — thumbnail with a CONCRETE chip
  for civil and structural, number · title, scale line; a tap picks the sheet
  and closes it. Open on the first visit to a job's Plan Room, closed after
  (`sc:plan-sheets-open:<job>` on the device). The desktop keeps its strip.
  **Takeoff** — the page on a canvas with an SVG layer in PDF points; tools
  Area, Rect, Linear, Count, Cutout, Scale (Auto is shown off: Dusty doesn't
  read drawings). **Fill (Coy, 2026-10-09: "build out step one" — click-to-fill on the
  architects' vector PDFs he mostly gets):** click inside an area and its outline is traced off
  the drawing. The sheet round the click is drawn by pdf.js at 3 px a point (a 600 pt window, then
  1,300 pt at 1.5, then the whole sheet when the flood runs off the window); what is clearly darker
  than, or another colour from, the paper under the click is ink (a grey-shaded area is paper);
  the paper is flooded from the click, only the outer edge is kept (text, hatch dots and labels
  inside don't matter) and straightened into corners (`traceFill()`, `src/lib/plan-fill.ts`,
  tested; `traceAt()`, `src/lib/v34/plan-trace.ts`). The outline is a dashed **draft** with its
  SF when the sheet has a scale — **Keep it** saves it as an area shape (method `area`, no
  migration) to drag and fix like any other, **Drop it** throws it away; a new click traces
  again. **Bridge gaps** (tight · small · big) closes breaks in a dashed or broken outline, and
  **Skip thin lines** leaves out lines drawn under 0.4 pt — dimensions and hatching across a slab
  — by the width pdf.js draws each line at (at 4 px a point, so only up to ~13 in of sheet round
  the click). No closed outline, a click on a line, or a sliver says so in words. Checked on a
  generated vector sheet: an L-shaped slab with a dot hatch, a label and a hairline dimension
  across it traced to its six corners within 0.3 pt (139,550 of 140,000 sq pt) with Skip thin
  lines, and split at the dimension without. **Hatch dots are paper (Coy, 2026-10-10: on C1.0 the
  outline "creates a lot of extra edges instead of following the border of the sidewalk and
  curb").** The stipple in a concrete hatch was ink: Bridge gaps grew each dot until the ones near
  the edge welded onto the edge line, and the flood went round them, so the outline jogged in and
  out at every dot and the straightening (0.4 pt) kept the jogs. Now a piece of ink that fits in
  ~1.7 pt square (a dot, a letter, a speck) is paper before anything is bridged (`dropSpecks()`);
  the flooded area is closed by 1 pt, filling the notches a dot stuck to the line leaves; and a jog
  under 0.7 pt off the straight line goes. A line, a dash longer than ~1.7 pt, or a dot touching a
  line stays; a boundary drawn as a dotted line no longer stops a fill. **Cut out (Coy, 2026-10-10: "areas where I want to click inside of curbs
  to remove the fill").** Fill · Cut out on Fill's strip: in Cut out a click inside an island, a
  median or anything inside a kept fill traces that region the same way (same Bridge gaps and
  Skip thin lines), drawn red with its SF; **Cut it out** adds it as a hole on the fill it sits in
  (the selected shape if the click is in it, else the smallest area shape on the sheet holding it
  and not already a hole — `areaShapeAt()`, `src/lib/takeoff.ts`, tested), through the Cutout
  tool's own PATCH, so the SF, the perimeter and the sections follow. A traced hole that runs
  outside the fill (`holeFits()`: 90% of its corners inside) is refused by name. Checked on a
  generated vector sheet: a 100 × 100 pt curbed box inside an L-shaped slab traced to its four
  corners and fit. No model reads the drawing — Fill and Cut out are geometry off the PDF.
  **Recalibrate, rotate and a ruler (Coy, 2026-10-15: "recalibrate the scale as shown on jobtread … rotate and a ruler").** The sheet's scale is a button on Takeoff's toolbar ("1\" = 20' ▾") opening Manual scale → **Select points on a known dimension** (the Scale tool), the printed scale, **Architectural** presets (1/16\" … 3\" = 1'-0\") and **Engineering** ones (1\" = 10' … 200'), and Not to scale. A preset is feet per inch ÷ 72 (a PDF point is 1/72\"), so it is right only for a sheet at its printed size; a half-size set is set by points. Any pick PATCHes the same `plan_sheets.scale_ft_per_pt` / `scale_method` (`manual`), so the takeoffs and their sections follow as for any scale change. **Rotate** turns the view a quarter turn, kept on the device per sheet (`sc:sheet-rot`); shapes stay in the page's own points (a click is turned back about the sheet's centre). **Ruler** (key M) reads the distance between two clicks in feet and inches at the sheet's scale and saves nothing.
  **Finish a line on its last point (Coy, 2026-10-15: "click on my last point to finish a linear measurement").** With Linear, a click back on the last point placed (two points or more) saves the line, as Finish does; a double-click lands the same way.
  **The phone's tools in one drop-down (Coy, 2026-10-15: "a drop down tool bar to the right side of the arrow … so I don't have to scroll").** On a phone Takeoffs' tool row is Pan, Select and one button showing the measuring tool in use (else the last one picked, Linear to start) with ▾, opening Area, Rect, Fill, Cutout, Linear, Count, Scale and Ruler. A desktop keeps its column.
  **Sheets in the Parameters panel (Coy, 2026-10-15: "two buttons in the parameters panel header; one for parameters and one for sheets").** On a desktop the panel's header is **Parameters | Sheets · N**; Sheets lists every sheet the panel's width — number, title, page, file and scale — a tap opening it, and × taking it out of Takeoffs as the phone's drawer does (refused while it has measurements, with Undo).
  **A whole group to the budget (Coy, 2026-10-15: "send a parameter group with all the measurements to the budget, not just individual measurements").** Each group row in Parameters has **$ Group**: the Send sheet opens with every takeoff of the group ticked and All in one section picked. Once every takeoff of a group is in one section, a takeoff drawn in that group, or moved into it, goes into that section too (`groupSection()`, `takeoff-sections-server.ts`), and the section's size follows. No migration.
  **Hide takeoffs, fold all, search (Coy, 2026-10-15: "hide measurements on the plans so i can read the plans clearly again. Like jobtread … collapse all/ expand all and search parameters").** Each group row in Parameters has an eye that hides its takeoffs on the sheet (a picked one still shows), and the row over the list has one for every group, **Collapse all / Expand all**, and **Search parameters** (a group by its name, or a takeoff by its name, its group opened). Hidden groups are kept on the device per job (`sc:takeoff-hidden:<job>`); totals and the budget are unchanged. No migration.
  **Sheets side by side (Coy, 2026-10-21, Claude Design "Sheet Compare": "the core ideas of the sheet comparison can be applied"; no migration).** **Compare** on Takeoffs' toolbar, or ticks on the Sheets list and **Compare N sheets**, puts 2–4 sheets side by side on a desktop (2 on a phone, stacked). One pane is **active** — its header tinted, a hi-viz border — and it is the page's sheet (`?sheet=`): every tool, the scale button, Back / Forward and Parameters act on it, exactly as with one sheet. The others are read-only (pan and zoom only); a click or tap on one, or 1–4 on the keyboard, makes it the active one, and picking a takeoff or a sheet that is in another pane moves there. The bar: Side by side · Stacked · One large + two · Four (desktop; a phone has a 1 / 2 toggle), **Link** (the other panes follow the active one's zoom — in feet when every sheet has a scale, "by page" otherwise — and its spot as it scrolls; while linked the active pane's header reads **Leads** and the others **Follows N**, and turning Link on says for a few seconds to zoom in and drag on the leading pane — Coy, 2026-10-22: "i'm just not understanding how linked pages work", since at Fit two sheets of one scale look the same linked or not), ⇄ Swap, + Sheet, "Tools on pane N", **One sheet ×**. Each pane's header: its number, a sheet picker, "same as N", the scale chip, maximise (Esc puts it back) and ×; two panes have a divider to drag. A drawing area under 760px wide shows two panes and the rest as tabs. Kept on the device per job (`sc:takeoff-compare:<job>`), brought back on the next visit ("Your compare is back"); a one-time hint says how to pick the pane. pdf.js keeps a decoded page per pane (`drawPage(…, slot)`), and on a phone a second scanned sheet waits behind **Draw it here**, so the phone never holds two decoded scans. `src/lib/sheet-compare.ts` (tested in `tests/sheet-compare.test.mjs`), `plan-room/sheet-compare.tsx`. Not built from the design: overlaying two revisions (wipe / blend) and Dusty's "what moved", lining sheets up by two matching points, dragging a sheet onto the drawing, opening a detail callout beside its plan, linked rotate, a swipe between panes on a phone, plan-set chips, and per-pane loading / Retry states.
  **Pan, moving a takeoff, and Back / Forward (Coy, 2026-10-15: "when the pan button is selected, the cursor becomes a hand … the select button is able to move the measurements … a back / forward button to be able to undo/redo actions on the plans").** **Pan** (key H, a hand) drags the sheet with the left button; the wheel button drags it with any tool. With **Select**, pressing on a takeoff and dragging moves it whole, cutouts too, saved as one PATCH of its points (sections follow); a press that doesn't move picks it. **Back** and **Forward** on Takeoff's toolbar (Ctrl + Z, Ctrl + Shift + Z or Ctrl + Y; while drawing, Ctrl + Z drops the last point) undo and redo, in this visit only (up to 60 steps): drawing a takeoff, moving it, dragging its corners, + Add / − Subtract, cutouts, and deleting one from Parameters. A takeoff put back after a delete is a new row with its points, cutouts, color and name, not its budget section (Send it again). Scale, groups, colors and moves between groups are not in the history. No migration.
  **Add to or take from a takeoff (Coy, 2026-10-15: "add/subtract from linear ft, areas and counts
  that are already measured in groups").** A picked takeoff shows **+ Add** and **− Subtract** on the
  toolbar; draw, then Finish. A line: Add carries it on from its last point (the points join its
  geometry); Subtract draws a run that is taken back off its length, kept in its `cutouts` (now allowed
  on linear, two points or more; `measurementQty()` subtracts their length, tested in
  `tests/takeoff-adjust.test.mjs`; no migration). A count: Add clicks more points onto it; Subtract takes
  off the counted point nearest each click (never the last one — delete it instead). An area: Add draws
  a new area in the same group (the group's row is their total), Subtract is the Cutout tool on it.
  Sections follow as for any edit.
  **Fills add up in one group (Coy, 2026-10-10: "add to existing measurements so i can capture all
  the sidewalks together").** Fill's strip has **Add to: <group>** (the job's area groups, and
  + New group…): every fill kept goes into that group as its own takeoff, and the group's row is
  their total — what Send to budget's "all in one section" carries as one size. Takeoffs already
  in one group are ticked and **Move to group** (Coy, 2026-10-10; was "Move N to…") another group (or a new one, with the old group's
  depth) beside **Send to budget** (was "Send N to budget") (`PATCH …/measurements/[mid] {parameter_id}`, sections follow).
  One takeoff holding several separate pieces would need a column (a migration) — not built.
  Not built: tracing a scanned sheet (it may work on
  a clean scan, untested), turning a PDF's CAD layers on and off, finding the concrete hatch by
  itself, and Dusty labelling what a fill is. **Find zones (Coy, 2026-10-15: "dusty is smart enough to know
  what sheet i need to find to measure the different thicknesses. But not smart enough to measure himself?").**
  Takeoffs' toolbar has **AI Takeoff** (the emblem and ▾; Coy, 2026-10-15: "Consolidate these find zones/find joints buttons into 1 drop down button"), a list of **Find zones** and **Find joints** — an open one ticked, picked again to close it (`ai-takeoff-menu.tsx`). **Find zones**: the whole sheet is drawn to a ~2,400 px JPEG in the browser
  and sent with its printed words to `POST /api/plan-sheets/[id]/zones` (session client, one tool-less call to
  the model API, Claude Opus unless `ZONES_MODEL`; nothing stored), which matches the legend's hatches to the
  drawing and returns each legend entry (label, thickness as printed) and each zone as a point inside it and a
  rough outline, in thousandths of the sheet (`ZONES_SYSTEM`, `cleanZones()`, `src/lib/plan-zones.ts`, tested in
  `tests/plan-zones.test.mjs`). **Dusty points, Fill traces**: each point goes through Fill (`traceAt()`, the
  point and four spots round it, then once skipping thin lines), and a trace is the zone only when its area is
  within ~0.35–2.8× the rough outline (`judgeTrace()`); otherwise the rough outline is shown, marked rough; two
  points that traced one patch are one zone (`sameZone()`). The zones are drawn dashed in a colour per legend
  entry with their SF, in a panel headed **Dusty's estimate** — drop the wrong ones, then Keep (per entry or
  all): each becomes an area takeoff in a group per entry (`5" Light duty concrete pavement`, the thickness as
  its depth; an area group already named so, or with that depth and a shared word, is used —
  `groupNameFor()` / `matchGroup()`). Nothing is saved before Keep. `dusty-zones.tsx` (`useDustyZones`).
  Not built: learning from what Coy keeps and changes, reading a scanned sheet's hatching (the model sees it;
  Fill on a scan is untested), and Dusty finding which sheet to run it on himself. **Find joints (Coy, 2026-10-15: "any way to enable dusty to measure control joint cutting on a parking lot?"; no migration).** Under AI Takeoff beside Find zones, **Find joints** reads the control joints off the PDF's own lines in the browser, no model: the page's drawing instructions (`pageStrokes()`, `src/lib/pdf.ts`) with the CAD layer each line is on; a layer named for control joints (C-PRKG-JNTS-CONTROL on HPC's C3.0 — never an expansion, isolation or construction joint layer, `controlLayer()`) is used outright, else every line drawn like the legend's CONTROL JOINT sample (its width and colour; dashes at least the sample's, since a plan may draw them at a bigger linetype scale). Dashes on one straight line with gaps of 14 pt or less are one run, gaps included (`joinRuns()`); the legend's sample is left out (`legendBox()`); each run is cut where it passes from one thickness takeoff on the sheet (an area group with a depth or a thickness in its name) into another (`splitByZone()`). Drawn dashed over the sheet in a colour per zone with each zone's LF, as Dusty's estimate; × drops a zone's, **Keep** saves them as linear takeoffs, one per run named "Control joint · 5"", in the job's control joint group (one named control joint or saw cut, else a new "Control Joints - Concrete Cutting"), through `POST /api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/measurements/batch` (up to 500 lines, linear groups only). `src/lib/plan-joints.ts`, tested in `tests/plan-joints.test.mjs`; checked on HPC's C3.0: 154 runs, about 6,700 LF at 1" = 20' (the layer and the legend read agree within 1.5%). Not built: scanned sheets (no lines to read), curved joints (kept as their chords), and saw-cut pricing per thickness. **A sheet's scale is only ever one Coy set** — "Use 1/8" =
  1'-0"" (the printed one, his tap), or two clicks on a known dimension and
  its length; until then nothing drawn there is counted. **Add plans (Coy,
  2026-10-14: "take a lesson from how jobtread handles plan uploads and
  autofills the names of the sheets and autoscales if there is a scale"; no
  migration).** A PDF dropped or picked in Files (the job's) opens **Add
  plans** before anything is uploaded (`add-plans.tsx`; photos and sketches
  still go straight up): the file is opened on the device with pdf.js
  (`openLocalPdf()`), each page read in turn (`readPlanPage()`, the same
  `readSheet()`), a row a page — a thumbnail on a desktop (none on a phone),
  its name as one line, number then title ("C3.0 PAVING AND JOINTING PLAN",
  `sheetName()`), the scale it prints already picked from a list of
  engineering and architectural scales (the printed one marked "printed",
  an odd one added first; `src/lib/plan-upload.ts`, tested), a tick to keep
  it, and Pending · Reading · Read · Scan (no words — the scan reader names it
  after Save). **Autofill** (on by default, kept on the device) off leaves
  the names blank ("Plans.pdf (4)" greyed) and no scale. **Save is Coy's
  tap, so it sets the scale left picked:** the printed one as
  `title_block`, another he picked as `manual`, No scale as `none`, on new
  pages only; a name he typed is `number_source='coy'` (split back into
  number and title, `splitName()`), so the scan reader never writes over it.
  Unticked pages get no sheet (a file with none ticked is kept in Files, out
  of Takeoffs). The bytes are put in the device's plan cache under the new
  file id, so Takeoffs doesn't download them again. A PDF that won't open
  here is uploaded and read in Takeoffs as before. **One scale on
  many sheets (Coy, 2026-10-08):** when a dimension is measured, "This
  sheet · Every sheet · Pick sheets…" beside Set scale, and **Scale → other
  sheets** on the toolbar of a sheet with a scale, open a list of the other
  sheets with ticks (every one but an aerial ticked for Every sheet, none for
  Pick) — each gets the same feet per point through its own PATCH, replacing
  its own scale; nothing is set without the tap. The sheets drawer has no
  pull tab sticking out of its edge any more (Coy: "remove this … tab on
  desktop and mobile"); ☰ Sheets and the strip open sheets. Groups
  (`takeoff_parameters`: area / linear / count, colour, optional depth) total
  their shapes (`takeoff_measurements`, geometry in points); quantities are
  worked out (`src/lib/takeoff.ts`: shoelace minus cutouts, polyline length,
  count × the sheet's feet per point) and **never stored**. JobTread's tree:
  group row with total (and CY with a depth), a row per shape; the selected
  shape carries a floating label. Full screen; the hint bar floats over the
  drawing so starting a shape never moves the sheet under the next click.
  **Aerial of the site (Coy, 2026-10-03: "take a screenshot of the address
  on Google maps and measure the driveway area" — "mapbox and esri and
  Google if possible"; since 2026-10-08 the **Satellite** tab of Takeoffs,
  in the page, Mapbox only — Google's look-only button and its key line are
  gone, a Planned card on the Roadmap (Coy: "remove the Google button … add it to the roadmap");
  zoom in quarter steps, by the scroll wheel or a pinch: between whole
  levels the next level's tiles are drawn smaller and a dashed box shows the
  frame saved at that level, so the saved sheet's scale is a whole zoom's).** The Plan Room header's **Aerial** (now the tab) finds the job's
  address (else its customer's; Google's geocoder if its key is set, else
  Mapbox's, else Esri's public one — the result is not stored), shows it
  on **Mapbox** or **Google** (Esri's button is gone — Coy, 2026-10-07: "it
  doesn't work"; the server can still draw an aerial saved from it), Coy drags to frame it and zooms
  (16–21; Mapbox 22), and **Put it in the Plan Room** saves the frame as a
  one-page PDF on the job, In plan room, with its sheet (`AERIAL`).
  The PDF is the tiles themselves (JPEG, nothing re-encoded), 1,280 ×
  1,024 points = tile pixels at that zoom, with a 50 ft bar, the provider's
  attribution and "imagery date not known" printed on it
  (`src/lib/aerial.ts`, tested: projection, scale against ground distance,
  tile cover, the PDF opening in pdf.js). **The scale is the map's
  arithmetic** — 156,543.03392 m · cos(lat) ÷ 2^zoom per point, 0.422 ft
  at zoom 20 in Baton Rouge — stored as the sheet's *printed* scale and,
  like any printed scale, used only on Coy's tap ("Use the map's scale",
  with a nudge to check it against the bar or a garage door). Checked in
  the harness: a 20 × 40 ft driveway drawn into fake tiles measures
  800 SF. **Google is a look only** — its terms don't allow keeping or
  tracing its imagery, so it is proxied with no caching and never saved.
  Keys stay on the server (`/api/aerial/tile`, `/api/aerial/google`,
  `/api/jobs/[id]/aerial`, session client): `MAPBOX_TOKEN`, optional
  `ESRI_API_KEY` (without it the public World Imagery tiles), and
  `GOOGLE_MAPS_GEOCODE_KEY` with the Maps Static API on.
  **Esri's grey tiles (2026-10-03):** where Esri's public tiles stop
  (often zoom 19) it answers with a small "Map data not yet available"
  picture. `fetchTile()` asks with `blankTile=false` and treats a tile
  under 6 KB as that placeholder, then draws the tile's box with Esri's map
  export, which uses the sharpest photo it has there — enlarged, not a
  sharper photo. Not testable from the agent container (map hosts are
  refused); check it on the preview.
  **Suggestions while typing (2026-10-03):** the address box offers up to
  six matches a quarter second after the last key (`/api/aerial/suggest`:
  Mapbox autocomplete when `MAPBOX_TOKEN` is set, else Esri's public
  suggest, resolved by its key when picked; nearest the map first; nothing
  stored). The drawer's subtitle names the place the map is on. Not built: the
  photo's date (Esri's metadata service), and Dusty outlining the
  driveway himself — when he does, it is a dashed suggestion Coy accepts.
  **Budget** — moved to the job's **Summary** as **From the plans**
  (Coy, 2026-10-06: "this budget tab shouldn't be here since we already
  have a budget page"; the Plan Room is Files · Takeoff, an old
  `?step=budget` opens it as usual): each group and the line that counts it,
  priced by the engine's `budget()`, each shape's share as a child row, with
  Send to budget (`BudgetStep` / `SendSheet` in `plan-room/budget-step.tsx`),
  shown only when the job has takeoff groups. (Until 069,
  **Send to budget** picked a Price book card and added a `from_takeoff`
  line; that line rule still works and Dusty's plans use it.)
  **Sections follow the Plan Room (migration 069, applied 2026-10-03 on
  Coy's "Apply 069").** Coy: *"Pushing to budget should push the area
  measured under the group. And I should be able to select the individual
  measurements … Section should follow any edit in the plan room … if I
  delete the measurement, it should delete it in the budget without deleting
  the whole section because i wouldn't want to lose all the cost items … I
  should be able to pull measurement into the budget from the budget page
  also."* `takeoff_measurements.section_id` (on delete set null) puts a shape
  in a budget section; `job_sections.takeoff_sync` (default on) says the
  section's size follows its shapes; `job_files.takeoff_measurement_id` (on
  delete set null) files a photo on a shape. Triggers keep all three on one
  job. **The size is the sum of the section's shapes** in the first shape's
  unit — area → `sqft_override` (and the groups' depth when they agree),
  linear → `count_qty` LF, count → `count_qty` EA; a shape of another kind
  is kept but not added, and a shape on a sheet with no scale leaves the size
  blank with the reason (`sectionTakeoff()` / `sectionPatch()`,
  `src/lib/takeoff-sections.ts`, tested). It is **written on the server**
  after every takeoff change — a shape drawn, re-shaped or deleted, a group's
  depth, a sheet's scale (`syncTakeoffSections()`) — so the engine, the
  Dashboard, Dusty and the proposal read the section as before. A shape
  deleted takes its share out; a section left with none has its size
  cleared and **keeps its cost items**. A size typed on the section turns
  the following off (✎ "size typed here" · **↺ follow the takeoff**).
  **Send to budget** now opens the group's shapes with a tick each (those not
  in a section yet ticked) and makes **each its own section**, **all in one
  section**, or adds them **into a section** already there, with a kind of
  area guessed from the group's name (`/api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/send`); the
  group row says "N of M in the budget" and a shape in one shows **$✓**. On
  the job page each section has a **Takeoff** strip — its shapes and total,
  ƒ follows / ✎ typed, and **Pick takeoffs** (every shape on the job by
  group; ticking one in another section moves it here; one kind per section;
  `PUT …/sections/[sid]/takeoff`) — and sections sit under their takeoff
  group's name, as in the Plan Room. Cost items go on afterwards, as before.
  **Editing a shape:** with Select, a picked shape shows its corners — drag
  one to move it, the dot mid-edge adds one, double-click removes one; the
  section follows. **Shape photos** on the toolbar (with a shape picked) ticks
  the job's photos of that area or takes new ones; they show under the
  shape's section and on the proposal. Checked in the harness: Send made one
  section per shape, a dragged corner saved the moved point (800 → 1,148 SF),
  a ticked photo was filed on the shape. **Checked again (2026-10-03):** a
  section made by Send now fills from Coy's auto-loading assembly for that kind
  of area, like one added by hand (it didn't); and a group's depth only fills a
  blank thickness — one typed on the section stays (a re-measure used to write
  the group's over it). Rehearsed (rolled back) before
  applying: a shape or photo on another job's section refused, the following
  on by default, a stranger sees nothing.
  **A colour per takeoff (migration 070, applied 2026-10-03 on Coy's "Apply
  070").** Coy: *"the ability to select different colors for each
  measurement in the parameters panel … The colors should show different on
  the drawing as well. The colors don't need to move over to the budget, it's
  just for the plan room."* `takeoff_measurements.color` (nullable `#rrggbb`,
  checked; null = the group's colour, as before). The colour bar on each
  takeoff's row in Parameters opens its swatches (`SHAPE_COLORS`, "group"
  first) and the drawing — fill, outline, corners — uses it (`shapeColor()`,
  `shared.ts`); a colour alone doesn't re-sync sections. The budget, the
  proposal's area maps and the sheet viewer keep the group's colour.
  Rehearsed (rolled back): a hex kept, anything else refused. Checked in the
  harness: the Apron recoloured blue on its row and on the sheet.
  **Tick to send (Coy, 2026-10-03: "a check box next to the measurement in
  the plan room parameters so that I can select the measurement and push to
  budget").** Each takeoff's row has a tick box; ticking any (one group at a
  time — a section takes one kind) turns the panel's button into **Send N to
  budget**, which opens the Send sheet with just those ticked. Checked in the
  harness: ticking Apron sent only Apron. **On the job page** the section's
  row is **Measurements** with a real button — **+ Pull measurements from the
  Plan Room** (Change measurements once it has some; Measure in the Plan Room
  › when nothing is measured yet) — Coy couldn't find the old "Pick takeoffs"
  link.
  **Perimeter of an area (Coy, 2026-10-04: "calculate the linear footage
  around the perimeter of that area").** Every area shape also gives its
  edge in LF — its outline closed, plus each cutout's outline (a hole is
  edge to form too) × the sheet's scale, never stored, none while the scale
  isn't set (`measurementPerimeter()`, `src/lib/takeoff.ts`, tested). Shown
  as "N LF edge" under the group's SF / CY and each takeoff's SF, and on the
  shape's floating label ("971 SF · 180 LF perimeter"). A budget line counted
  from an area group whose card is in LF takes the group's perimeter
  (`ruleQty()`, `perim_lf` on the takeoff total) — form & finish by the foot
  from the same outline. Dusty's job context carries it per group.
  Checked in the harness on a generated sheet: a 30×40 ft rectangle at
  1/8" = 1'-0" is exactly 1,200 SF (14.8 CY at 4"), $2,467 on a $1.43 card at
  15% O.H. and 20% margin; a 10×20 apron moved the line to 1,400 SF / $2,878;
  taps ~16 ms. Dusty's job context includes each sheet's scale and each
  group's total. APIs: `/api/jobs/[id]/plans` (GET), `…/plans/sheets`
  (POST, the browser's reading), `/api/plan-sheets/[id]` (PATCH scale /
  number / title), `/api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/parameters[/pid]`,
  `…/takeoff/measurements[/mid]` — session client, org from the session,
  every id checked against the job; triggers keep a shape's group and sheet
  on one job, and a sheet's file on its job.
- **Job › Proposal (migration 060, 2026-10-01; Claude Design,
  `design/proposal`).** Coy: *"Go ahead with the proposals."*
  `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/proposal`, from the job header's **Proposal**
  button (`src/components/v34/proposal/`). Left, the job panel (a sheet on a
  phone): project type (switching loads that type's defaults, asking first),
  the option, cost → price by cost type with the **margin** (slider and box —
  it writes the job's `estimates.margin_pct`, so budget and proposal never
  disagree), "Calculated $X → rounded to $Y", the sub split (20/80, 30/70 —
  never on the document), and the warranty and contract pickers. Middle, the
  document — always white paper, whatever the theme: Cover (a photo from the
  job's files), Header, Scope (intro + each area's size and spec), Pricing,
  Payments, Warranty, Terms, Sign — each with an on/off pill; quick toggles
  Item prices · Qty · unit · Tax line; groups and lines shown/hidden, prices
  hidden, lines made optional. **The cover's dark band (Coy, 2026-10-04):**
  the logo on the left, the kind and title on the right, nothing else — the
  "Prepared for …" line was repeated below. **Since 2026-10-05 the band is
  its own strip** between the Cover and the Header (Coy: "The cover should
  not include this dark header. I don't want to lose this when I turn the
  header off"): the Cover switch hides only the photo, the Header switch
  only the quote number and Prepared by / for, and the band always carries
  the logo. In print the cover photo is an `<img>` (3.4 in tall), the band
  keeps its colour (`print-color-adjust: exact`) and nothing forces a page
  break after the cover, so photo, band and header open page 1 together
  with a photo or without one. **Who it goes to, and Dusty beside each part (Coy, 2026-10-10, three desktop notes).** The Preview's
  **Send to** lists the job's person, the customer's other people (`GET /api/jobs/[id]/proposal/recipients`,
  session client) and, under **Your team**, the org's members (`sc_org_people`; a member who is also a
  contact, an onsite estimator, carries that contact's phone), each ticked or not, the job's person
  ticked to start. **Send text** sends one text each through Quo (`sendProposal()` per person, the
  greeting turned into each one's first name with `greetingSlot()`); a member with no contact has no
  phone to text. **Email it** opens one email to every ticked address, its greeting with no name when
  there are several. **Edit with Dusty is the emblem** (`DustyMark`, `document.tsx`) beside the scope
  introduction, beside each area's name (its scope of work, saved to the section's `prep_notes` as the
  budget's emblem does), and on the warranty and terms. Every such question asks Dusty to put the words
  for the page in a ```` ```proposal ```` block (`USE_ASK`, `src/lib/dusty/use-block.ts`, tested); the
  chat draws it as a highlighted box with **Use this …** inside it, and only the box is copied
  (`splitUse()`); an answer with no box is used whole, as before.
  **Prepared by** is the member preparing it — their
  name and phone from Settings → Account → You (`PATCH /api/me {phone}`, kept on their
  own account), then the org; a final freezes them as
  `snapshot.prepared_by`, and the customer's copy carries name, phone and
  email only. **Prepared for** shows the phone as (225) 555-0100 and the
  email (the contact's, else their first listed one) when there is one.
  **Preliminary (Coy, 2026-10-07: "I need to make sure it's clear that this
  is a preliminary estimate").** A **Preliminary** chip beside Item prices
  (`display.preliminary`, no migration; a new version keeps it): the band
  and the quote's name read "Preliminary estimate", and a line above the
  price says the final price is confirmed after a site visit and exact
  measurements. It reaches the customer's link (`publicSnapshot()`), and
  Dusty's proposal context says whether it is on.
  **A full-sheet cover (Coy, 2026-10-22: "I'd like the cover to be built into the proposal itself and
  fill a full sheet of paper when printed out"; no migration).** The Cover is a letter-shaped sheet
  drawn from the job (`cover-page.tsx`), in one of three styles picked on the cover's bar: **Field**
  (the default: white, the logo and a tag at the top, the job photo slanted under it, then the word,
  date, title, number and who prepared it beside the customer and site, an accent stripe and dark
  wedge in the corner) · **Pour** (a dark sheet, the photo cut on a slant, the logo on a tab, the
  number, title, Prepared for / by, and up to three lines along the foot) · **Photo strip** (the old
  cover). Two colours (presets, Navy & orange first, or any picked), the tag and the foot lines are
  Coy's to type. Kept as `display.cover` {style, dark, accent, tag, lines} on the draft (`cleanCover()`
  / `coverOf()`, `src/lib/proposal.ts`, tested), frozen with a final and carried on the customer's
  link (`publicSnapshot()`). Sizes are in container units, so it scales from a phone to the page;
  printed, it fills page 1 inside the 12mm margins and the band opens page 2. **Printing names the
  file after the proposal** ("Wilson Driveway Repair - SE-26-0013-A", `proposalFileName()`; the tab's
  title is swapped on beforeprint and put back after), and a margin box of our own (page N / M, bottom
  right) turns off the browser's date, title and address lines (Chrome 131+; an older browser still
  prints them unless Headers and footers is unticked).
  **A proposal is pages (Claude Design "Proposal Builder v2", Coy's upload 2026-10-23; no migration).**
  The proposal page is a list of pages beside one page open: on a desktop a column of them on the
  left (number, name, where it reads from — "From the job" or "Yours" — an eye to switch one off,
  ⋯ to rename, duplicate, move or delete, drag to reorder), the open page in the middle with its
  name and ‹ › to the next, and **Pull in ›** on the right (the job's photos and PDFs, a tap putting
  one on the open photos page or a new page); on a phone "Page 2 of 9 ▾" folds the list open, and
  Pull in fills the screen. Built pages read the job and appear once each — Title (the cover, band
  and header), **At a glance** (site, start day, the price, payment in a line, valid until), Scope,
  Pricing, **If we find…** (unknowns priced per unit from the Price book — `sellOf()`, the job's O.H.
  and margin — shown, never in the total), Payments, Warranty, Terms, Authorization; the owner's own
  are **Text** ("# " a heading, "- " a bullet, **bold**), **Photos** (job photos with captions, 1–3
  across) and **PDF** (a job PDF, or one uploaded there, drawn page by page with pages left out).
  **+ Add page** puts one after the open page. Kept as `display.pages`, `display.ifind` and
  `display.valid_until` on the draft (`src/lib/proposal-pages.ts`, tested), frozen with a final; a
  proposal saved before reads as the old sections in their old order with their old switches.
  **Before Preview** a send check lists what looks wrong (a page still under its starting name, a text
  page nearly empty, a photos page with no photos, a PDF page with no file, a quantity shown with no
  unit, If we find… with nothing on it, no valid until or one already past) with Go
  to it / Send anyway (`sendCheck()`). Print and Save as PDF print every page that is on, not only
  the open one. **Pages and their sections switch on and off (Coy, 2026-10-24: "toggle on/off pages. And … sections
  in the pages"; no migration).** Each page's row in the list has a switch, and so does the open page's
  head; under it, **On this page:** chips switch its sections off one by one (`PARTS`, `partsOff()` /
  `togglePart()` in `proposal-pages.ts`, tested): Title — Cover, Dark band, Number & names; At a glance —
  Price, Payment, Valid until, Included, Start; Scope — Job site line, Introduction, Areas & scope of work,
  Maps & photos; Pricing — Preliminary note, Section photos; If we find… — Explanation; Warranty and
  Terms — Title; Authorization — Acceptance words, Your signature line; a Text page — Heading. Kept as
  `parts_off` on the page (an empty list overrides the title page's old Cover / Header switches in
  `display.off`, which it reads until then), carried on the customer's link; a section switched off is
  left off for the customer and print, and in the builder folds to a dashed "… · hidden · tap to show". The customer's link carries the pages that are on, the If we find… lines and valid
  until (`publicSnapshot()`), and their photos and PDFs signed like the area photos (`assets.files`,
  `pageFileIds()`). **The cover photo's camera stamp** — when and where it was taken (`stampText()`
  from `files.taken_at`, `lat`, `lng`) — bottom right on every cover style, a switch on the cover's
  bar (`cover.stamp`, on by default). Not built yet (they need a migration): page templates kept for
  the company, the customer ticking choices or leaving comments on a page, a page's own table.
  **Maps & photos per area (2026-10-03, Coy:
  "the customer can easily understand what this section is covering. They
  will have the map view + photos for that map area"):** under the Scope's
  list, each area with takeoff shapes shows the part of their sheet — the
  aerial or a plan page — with the shapes drawn on it (drawn in the browser
  from the plan with pdf.js, `area-visuals.tsx`), beside up to four of its
  photos (filed on the area or on one of its shapes); an eye hides one area's,
  the **Maps & photos** quick toggle hides all (`display.picsOff`,
  `hide_area_pics`). Read live from the Plan Room, like the cover photo — a
  final shows today's shapes for its areas. **Since 2026-10-05 the photos are
  thumbnails** (76px square on a phone, 92 on a desktop), each opening full
  size, and a section's **Photos** row on the job page has a **Proposal**
  switch that is that area's eye (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id]/proposal {pics:
  {section_id, on}}`, read with `GET …/proposal?display=1`, which makes no
  draft; with none, the switch opens one). A link made without the browser
  (sent from Threads or Dusty) now takes photos filed on an area's takeoff
  shapes too, as the Preview does. Warranty and terms are edited in place;
  **Save as template** writes them back (same name updates it, a new name
  makes one). **Edit with Dusty** opens him with a starter question, and
  **Use this** under his answer (`setDustyUse()` in `dusty-live.ts`) puts it
  in the section — Coy's tap; Dusty writes nothing. **Print / PDF** is the
  browser's print (`@media print` shows the pages only); a PDF filed on the
  job needs a server renderer — later. **Preview** (it was Mark final —
  below) freezes the document when it is sent, in `job_proposals.snapshot` (the priced document and the warranty and
  terms as worded), refused while draws don't add up; the database refuses
  any change to a final (`sc_job_proposal_guard`); **New version** starts
  the next draft. One draft per job.
