  **The proposal's number (Coy, 2026-10-10: "SE-26-0001-A (company-year-proposal
  number-project letter)").** SE is the org's initials from `orgs.name` (the first
  letter of each word, letters only, LLC / Inc and the like left out, four at most;
  none → P); 26 the year, Central time, the job's FIRST proposal was made; 0001 the
  job's place among the org's jobs whose first proposal was made that year, in the
  order those were made (same instant: by job id); A the version — v1 A … v26 Z,
  v27 AA (`src/lib/proposal-number.ts`, tested in `tests/proposal-number.test.mjs`;
  `proposalNumbers()` in `proposal-number-server.ts` reads `orgs.name` and every
  `job_proposals` row's job and `created_at` under the session). **No column, no
  migration:** proposals are never deleted (a job is archived, its proposals stay),
  so a job's place never moves. Renaming the org changes the initials on what is
  read, not on what was sent. It replaces `<job_number or P>-<version>` ("P-3")
  everywhere a number is shown: the proposal's own row and version picker, the
  document of a draft, Preview and its link list, Files' Proposals, the Summary's
  proposal and Won cards, Threads' proposal picker and "/" links, Dusty's proposal
  and Proposals to send lines, and the activity line's "Proposal opened / signed:
  SE-26-0004-C · …". **A final freezes its number in `snapshot.number`** (as
  before): the customer's link, its tab title and the document of a final show the
  number it was sent under, so the finals frozen as "P-1" … "P-3" before this keep
  them for the customer; inside the app they read the new number with "sent as
  P-3" beside it (Dusty: "sent to the customer as P-3"), and Files finds either.
  Measured 2026-10-10: Wilson Driveway is SE-26-0004 (its v4 draft SE-26-0004-D),
  Sacco Driveway Repair SE-26-0010-A. `doc_templates` were seeded from the
  design's text (from Coy's won quotes) as **starter** text — "{org}" is the
  org's name, the license number a blank — marked "check it with your
  attorney" until Coy saves his own. Dusty's job context has the proposal
  (total, rounding, what's hidden, draws) and never makes a draft. APIs:
  `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal` (GET, PATCH), `…/final`, `…/new-version`,
  `/api/project-types[/id]`, `/api/doc-templates[/id]` — session client, org
  from the session, every id checked. Rehearsed: one draft per job; a final
  with no snapshot, or an edit to a final, refused; a stranger sees 0 rows
  and can't write; anon refused.
- **Preview and send — the proposal by link (migration 075, Coy
  2026-10-03: "Mark final should be changed to: 'Preview' … the preview of
  the proposal that the customer would see when I send it … send the proposal
  via text and email … track every time the customer views the proposal …
  hosted on the cloud").** The proposal toolbar's **Preview** (Mark final is
  gone, and so is the "Fix the budget first" button — Coy: "doesn't even
  work"; why it can't be sent is said in words on the Preview) opens the
  document exactly as the customer gets it — read-only, hidden sections,
  groups and lines gone, no empty cover, no draw checks — laid out for the room it has
  (the phone layout under 600px, the desktop one above; no Phone / Desktop
  switch since 2026-10-11, Coy: "one proposal that will adapt"), with **Text it · Email it · Copy link** (`preview.tsx`).
  Sending a draft freezes it as a final version first (the same server check
  as before); then its **link** is made: a `documents` row (the old empty
  placeholder for sending quotes) with `proposal_id`, a 32-character random
  `token`, `shown` — the customer's copy, built by `publicSnapshot()`
  (`src/lib/proposal-links.ts`, tested) WITHOUT cost, O.H., profit, margin,
  hidden lines or groups (not even their names), hidden prices (zeroed) or
  the areas' notes — and `assets`, the storage paths of the logo, cover and
  each area's photos and map (the map drawn with its shapes into a JPEG by
  the browser, `mapPicture()`, and uploaded to
  `job-files/{org}/proposal/{doc}/`). Text and Email open **Coy's own**
  Messages or mail app with the words and the link (`sms:` / `mailto:`) —
  ScopeCrete sends nothing (§10); `sent_via` records which. A new version's
  link retires the old ones (the customer sees "replaced — ask for the
  latest"); **Turn this link off** revokes one. **Resending (Coy,
  2026-10-11: "I don't see a way to resend proposals. Also I'm not seeing
  the photos added to the section on the proposal links").** Once sent, the
  Preview's buttons read **Resend text** / **Email it again**, the toolbar's
  **Preview & resend**, and the job's Summary has **Resend** under the
  proposal (`?send=1` opens the Preview). A resend asks the server for the
  live link again, which re-reads each area's photos (filed on the section
  or its takeoff shapes, `areaPhotoPaths()`) into its `assets`, adds a map
  for an area that had none, and keeps the words and prices frozen; a signed
  link (097) is never touched — photos added after signing go on a New
  version. The server's photo read was also two `.or()` filters on one query;
  it is one now, with the area check in code. **An older final too (Coy, 2026-10-11: "I should still be able to copy a link and resend a proposal that was frozen").** An earlier final picked in the Proposal tab's version list keeps Preview & send (Resend) and Copy link, opening the same Preview for that version: the link route takes `{version}` and sends that final's own link (signed or not); one whose link was replaced is made the customer's link again, saying the newer one will show "replaced"; one older than a version the customer signed is refused, with **Open … to copy or resend it** taking Coy to the signed one. New version stays on the newest only. The customer opens
  **`/p/<token>`** (Vercel, public, no sign-in, `noindex`): `/api/p/[token]`
  calls `sc_shared_proposal()` — SECURITY DEFINER, the ONE thing anon may
  execute here — which returns `shown` for a live link only and logs a
  `viewed` event (a hashed IP, the browser) with `view_count`,
  `first_viewed_at` / `last_viewed_at`, unless the reader is in the org (Coy
  checking his own link isn't a view). Pictures are signed by the same
  request's client; storage policy `shared_proposal_assets` lets anon read
  an object only while a live link lists its path (`sc_shared_asset()`). **Sent from Threads (Coy, 2026-10-11: "Sending the proposal should open up the threads for that customer. I can email or text it from there").** The Preview's Send to picks one person (the job's person to start; the customer's other people, Coy's team); its button is **Text it in Threads** / **Email it in Threads** (with Copy link). It makes the link as before (a draft frozen first), fills the words, and opens the person's thread with them in the text box (the composer's own per-device draft, `sc:draft:<contact>`), or a new email to them (`/dashboard/messages/email/new?contact=&to=&subject=&body=`); Coy presses Send there — through Quo, or the two-tap email. The link is marked sent by the server when a text (`/api/texts`) or email (`/api/emails/send`) carrying a `/p/<token>` goes out (`markLinksSent()`, `linkTokensIn()`, tested; a signed link left alone), and the words are kept for learning when the thread opens (`openInThreads()`, `send-proposal.ts`). The Preview no longer texts directly or opens mailto; Dusty's send_proposal step and the "/" link are unchanged.
  Anon has no grant on `documents` or `document_events`; members read and
  write them under `get_user_org_ids()`; a link must point at a FINAL
  proposal on its own job (`documents_guard`). **Tracking:** the toolbar's
  line ("v1 sent · opened 3 times · last 2h ago"), the Preview's list of
  every opening with the device, and the activity line ("Proposal opened:
  Wilson Driveway Replacement"). **Openings, stamped (Coy, 2026-10-23: "on the job summary page and
  on the proposal page a list of time/date stamped proposal openings"; no migration):** the job's
  Summary (under the proposal card) and the proposal page ("opened N times ▾" on its settings row)
  list every opening, signing and customer message across the job's links, newest first, in Central
  time with the version and the device (`openingRows()`, `deviceOf()`, `centralStamp()`,
  `src/lib/proposal-links.ts`, tested; `ProposalOpenings`, `proposal/openings.tsx`). A link is one
  per version, not per person, so who opened it isn't known and the list says so. **The customer
  writes back (migration 127, written 2026-10-23, NOT applied — waits on Coy's "apply 127"; Coy:
  "the customer … can message me back through the app. This will be part of the 'internal'
  communications that will be apart of contact threads").** Under the proposal on `/p/<token>`,
  **Questions? Message <org>**: their name (kept on their device) and words → `POST
  /api/p/[token]/messages` (the reader client, no service role) → `sc_proposal_message()` (SECURITY
  DEFINER, anon may run it; a live or signed link only; refuses anyone signed in to the org; name
  2–80, words 1–2,000, 20 a link a day). It is an internal note (`contact_notes`) on the person on
  the job's customer whose name is the name typed, else the job's person, else the customer's
  primary, with `document_id` (the link) and `from_name` — new columns only the door may set
  (`contact_notes_customer_guard`; a member can't forge or edit one). It shows in that person's
  thread's Notes as **From <name> · on the proposal link**, in Dusty's context like any note, and on
  the openings list with **Answer in their thread ›**; `sc_proposal_messages()` shows the link's
  earlier messages back on its page. Coy answers by text, call or email as usual — nothing is sent
  to the customer from the note. Before 127 the box isn't drawn and every read falls back.
  Rehearsed (rolled back) on Johnette Wilson's link: anon's message landed on her note, a short name,
  empty words and a bad token refused, a member's send, a forged column and an edit refused, a plain
  note still saved. Not built: an alert on the bell or banner for a new message (they show in Notes
  and the list), and the customer seeing Coy's answer on the page. APIs: `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal/link` (GET
  the job's links with their openings; POST the link for the newest final),
  `/api/proposal-links/[id]` (PATCH `{sent}` / `{revoke}`) — session client,
  org from the session, every file id checked against the job; `/api/p/[token]`
  — no service role. Rehearsed (rolled back): a member makes a link; one on a
  draft refused; anon reads the frozen copy and the view is logged once; a
  member's own opening not logged; a bad token null; a listed path readable
  by anon, an unlisted one not; anon can't read either table; a stranger sees
  no links and can't make one; a revoked link is gone and its pictures with
  it. **Applied 2026-10-04 on Coy's "apply 075"** (with anon's leftover
  grants on `contact_phones` revoked; checked after: the columns, the guard
  trigger, the storage policy, anon may execute only the two functions and
  holds no grant on `documents`, `document_events` or `contact_phones`, a bad
  token returns nothing). A preview deployment sits behind Vercel's own login, so a link made
  anywhere but localhost points at `https://scopecrete.ai` (`publicOrigin()`,
  2026-10-07: links made on the feedback preview were *.vercel.app, and Dusty
  hit Vercel's login trying one). Dusty is told never to open a link, and a job
  named in his question carries its budget (none, no items, or items and
  price — `jobValues()`), not "open the job for its budget". The customer
  approves and signs on the page since migration 097 (§6 Won); not built: picking an option there,
  and a PDF filed on the job.
- **The job's Bid strip (2026-10-01, §2i)** — full width like the section
  cards (2026-10-02, Coy: "needs to be the same width as everything else");
  folded by default since
  2026-10-02 (Coy: "doesn't take up a lot of space on my mobile phone") to
  one row, "Bid · due Tue, Oct 13 …", ▶ opens it, remembered per device. On a job a forwarded bid made
  or fed: GC, estimator, bids due (hours left, red under 48), pre-bid, the
  Box folder and what was copied and read, **Scope from the bid** (each
  item with its sheet, spec and line) and **Undo bid**. The Dashboard has a
  **Bids due** tile (open bid invites by due date).
- **Job header (2026-10-02, Coy's notes on the Burgess job):** ‹ Jobs and
  the job name on one row (bold, smaller), the four chips small on one row
  under it, the customer line and description (left out when the Bid strip
  below shows the bid's scope summary — Coy: "redundant"), then the job's
  switch above the money strip. **Delete is at the bottom of the job page**
  (Coy, 2026-10-04: on a phone it pushed Proposal off the screen), and the
  header's sheets button reads **Sheets (n)** on one line. **Since
  2026-10-05 all three views share one top row** (`JobHeaderRow`,
  `job-tabs.tsx`; Coy: "I like the layout of the budget page the best with
  the job name on the top left and the 'plan room, budget, proposal'
  buttons on the top right … Any buttons currently in the top right need to
  be moved down below"): ‹ Jobs, the job's name (and the ⓘ, or the
  proposal's number and draft / final, with its price line under it) on
  the left, the switch alone on the right. Each view's own buttons sit
  below (the Budget's Sheets (n) button is gone since 2026-10-06 — Coy: "accessible from within the plan room"; a section's own sheet links stay, and on a phone the job's name is 14px (`.sc-job-name`) and ‹ Jobs just ‹, so a long name fits the width); the Plan Room's
  reading chips on the row under the name;
  the Proposal's version, Item prices · Qty · unit · Tax · Maps & photos,
  Print / PDF and Preview on the settings row beside Project type,
  Warranty and Contract (on a phone, the buttons on their own row and the
  settings folded).
- **Summary, a fourth view (Coy, 2026-10-05: "a summary page to the left
  of the plan room … including all the chips" and "all of the lines and
  words directly below the job name … we can add to the summary page").**
  Summary · Plan room · Budget · Proposal, at `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/summary`
  (`job-summary.tsx`): the four chips, then one fact a row — the customer, the
  address, "Created Oct 2, 2026", the number of sections (Coy, 2026-10-09) — the
  description (Why this job is gone — Coy, 2026-10-09: "redundant … the information exists right above it"; `GET /api/jobs/[id]` still returns `why`), the Bid strip, the price
  at a glance from the engine's `budget()` over `/api/jobs/[id]/budget`
  (total, cost, O.H., profit, margin, tax, job minimum, or what blocks it),
  the proposal's link (version, sent, opened —
  `/api/jobs/[id]/proposal/link`), and Delete job at the bottom (on the Budget's bottom too since 2026-10-09 — Coy couldn't find it after a New scope) (a deleted
  job opened there shows Restore — `GET /api/jobs/[id]` now returns
  `archived_at`). No new API. Dusty is HOT on the job there. An onsite
  estimator gets no Summary tab, and `redirectFor()` sends /summary to the
  job page. Under them, **From the plans** (since 2026-10-06, the Plan
  Room's old Budget step). The Budget's chip row, customer and description lines, ⓘ and
  Delete moved to Summary (the Bid strip stays on both); the Proposal's
  number, draft / final and price left the name's row for one line above
  its settings. **A job is renamed from inside it (Coy, 2026-10-10: "don't see a way to change the job name
  from inside the job"):** ✎ beside the name on Summary, Takeoffs and Proposal opens it as a box —
  Enter or leaving it saves (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {title}`, `JobHeaderRow`); the Budget's name was
  already typed in place. Not for an onsite estimator. **The Budget has no Bid box (Coy,
  2026-10-10: "it should be in the summary page")** — the Bid strip is on the Summary alone. **A
  section's row (same day):** its name is 13px, the size of the bid's scope line, not the display
  face; Dictate is the mic icon alone; the kind-of-area picker is gone from it (the kind stays on
  the section for assemblies, set when it is made); the fold button sits at the row's right end. **Later that day (Coy):** Measured by is the full width, each way its own colour (Length × width blue,
  Area only green, Linear ft orange, Count violet; `MODES`), with no "Measured by" over it; the
  Measurements words are gone — **Measure in Takeoffs ›** (or Pull / Change measurements) sits on the
  row under it, and a line of the shapes counted shows only when there are some
  (`SectionTakeoffStrip`). The section's **Budget** row (with **+ Add item** on it since 2026-10-10, Coy; it reads only "Budget · N items" since that evening — the cost, price and per-SF left it, Coy: "The other information in between can be removed"; each cost item under a group is set in with a "-" so it reads as part of that group) is bold 13px on a tint, set in with a rule at
  its left so it reads as part of the section, its fold ▸ at the right, + Add items from the Price
  book inside it; its groups and items are 11–13px, the prices no bigger. **On a phone the job page's
  own header slides away with the dashboard's** while the dark numbers strip stays at the top: the
  sticky wrapper (`.sc-job-sticky`) sticks the header's measured height above the screen under
  `html[data-head-hide]`.
  **A job opens on its Summary (Coy, 2026-10-09: "when I open a
  job, let it default to the summary page")** — from the Jobs board and table,
  customer and contact pages, the Map, Files, search, Dusty's job picker, the
  activity line and the Dashboard; a job just made (+ New scope, call notes)
  still opens on its Budget, and an onsite estimator is sent to the Budget.
  **On a phone** (Coy: "the job name in the center of the page … plan room,
  budget, and proposal button … center of the page below the job name") the
  name is centred on its own line (‹ Jobs at its left edge; the Budget's
  editable name centred too) and the switch centred under it; the desktop
  keeps name left, switch right (`JobHeaderRow`).
- **The Summary's parties and job site (Claude Design "Job Summary", Coy 2026-10-13: "we have
  some updates to the job summary page. And be sure to integrate the streetview/satellite view").**
  The Summary is the page's full width (no cap): ‹ Jobs and the name, the chips in the header on a
  desktop (in the body on a phone), the name in Lexend with "Created … · N sections" under it and the
  description; then two columns (one under 1,040px): **Customer · bill to** (green edge) and **Job
  for · owner** (violet) side by side, **Contacts on this job** under them, and the **Job site** map on
  the right; then What's next, Won, Schedule, the Bid strip, price · proposal · bids in a row, Specs and
  From the plans as before (`job-parties.tsx`). **Customer:** the name to its page, its type, "Address
  = job site" when the street matches, the address, "N people · N jobs · customer since …"; Change
  searches the customers; with none, **Make <the job's person> the customer** (their own customer,
  billed at the job site's address) or + Attach an existing customer. **Owner (migration 109, applied
  2026-10-13 on Coy's "apply 109" — checked after: the column nullable with its key to customers, the
  index, the guard SECURITY INVOKER and its trigger on jobs, RLS and the 5 policies on jobs unchanged, 54
  jobs, none with an owner):** who the work is for when the customer is a GC
  — `jobs.owner_customer_id`, a row of `customers` (no new table; null = the same as the customer,
  every residential job), set with + Set owner (search or + New owner, made a commercial customer) and
  cleared with Same as the customer (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {owner_customer_id}`, checked against the
  org; trigger `jobs_owner_guard` keeps it in the org and never the customer itself). Before 109 the
  card says it waits for it. **Contacts:** the job's person, the customer's people and the owner's,
  each tagged with the side they work for (from their `customer_id`), primary first, with call and
  email buttons (the email opens a new email to them in Threads, not the phone's mail app; Coy,
  2026-10-13), a filter by side when there is an owner, and + Add contact onto that side
  (`sc_attach_contact`). **Job site:** Map · Satellite · Street view (the design's two, plus the map;
  remembered on the device `sc:site-view`), drawn by the Map page's own map on the provider Settings
  → Maps picks (Street view is Google's embed), a pin at the site and one at the customer's office when
  it is elsewhere, fitted to both, with "N mi from site"; Copy and ✎ the address (the Mapbox street
  finisher); on a phone the address under the map. **Clip** beside the toggle (was Directions on a phone; Coy, 2026-10-16: "I already have an open in Google Maps button") opens the Map page on the job's address (`/dashboard/map?job=`), searched, ready for Clip for takeoff. The wheel doesn't zoom it
  (the page scrolls); + / − do. `GET /api/jobs/[id]` returns `customer`, `owner`, `owner_supported`,
  `people` and `site` for it.
- **The job page gets the phone's whole screen (Coy, 2026-10-05: "when I
  scroll down let the main dashboard header disappear under the job page
  header").** On a phone, scrolling down the job page slides the dashboard
  header away (`useHideHeadOnScroll()`, `html[data-head-hide]` sets
  `--sc-head` to 0) and the job's own header and money strip stay pinned at
  the top; scrolling up brings it back. Desktop unchanged.
  **Every page since 2026-10-06** (Coy: "this page and any other page that I
  have opened … pushes up and covers over the main header bar"): the hook runs
  in the dashboard layout, reset on each page change, and a list page's own
  title row stays at the top as the list scrolls under it (`.sc-page-head` —
  Customers, Vendors, Contacts; the Jobs board's header; Threads' list header
  was already sticky). Pages that don't scroll the window (an open thread,
  Dusty) are unchanged. **A page with little to scroll keeps its header**
  (under 240px of room — a customer's Files tab jumped back and forth, Coy),
  and a scroll within 350 ms of the header moving is the page settling, not
  Coy (`use-hide-head.ts`). **Every page since 2026-10-10 (Coy: "consistent on every single page"):**
  a page that scrolls inside its own box (the contact card, `.sc-pinned`) slides the header away too —
  the hook hears scrolls in capture on the document, any scroller most of the screen tall, never one
  in a drawer, dialog or Dusty — and a record page no longer keeps it; its own sticky header rides
  up to the top. *Superseded:* **A record page keeps both (Coy, 2026-10-09:
  "the headers here in the customer page should be fixed and not slide
  up")**: the customer, contact and vendor page's header is sticky under the
  dashboard header (`data-head-pin` on `RecordHeader`), and the dashboard
  header doesn't slide away while one is on the page.
- **One ☰, one search (Coy, 2026-10-06: "use the same left slide panel
  hamburger menu button for anywhere there are selections that need to slide
  in … consistent on the left … in line with the page header" and "the same
  search icon and search bubble consistently across every page").**
  `MenuButton`, `SearchToggle` and `SearchBubble` in `primitives.tsx`, the
  look Threads' list had: the ☰ (40px, left of the title, on a phone) opens
  Threads' buckets, Files' folders and the Jobs board's stages; the
  magnifier (26px, in the title row, before + New) opens a full-width pill
  under it, × clears, Esc closes — Threads, Customers, Vendors, Contacts and
  Files. The Price book's Cards keep their search open (the budget's picker
  needs it), drawn as the same pill. Page titles on Threads and Files take
  `.sc-page-title` like the other lists.
- **The Map page (Coy, 2026-10-08, Claude Design 11: "add the map page to
  the main menu on desktop; toolbox on mobile … push the snipits from the map
  into the takeoffs tool … That can replace the current satellite page in the
  plan room").** `/dashboard/map` (`src/components/v34/map/`), after Jobs on
  the rail; in the Toolbox on a phone (`cleanMenus()` now puts a new page after
  its default neighbour, not at the end). **No map library**: a slippy map of
  our own (`map-view.tsx`) drawing tiles from `/api/aerial/tile`, which now
  takes `p=streets` (Mapbox Streets, `fetchStreetTile()`) and any zoom from 2
  — the token stays on the server; drag, wheel and pinch zoom about the
  pointer, double-click in. Web Mercator, scale and clip maths in
  `src/lib/map.ts` (tested, `tests/map.test.mjs`).
  **Pins** (`GET /api/map/pins`, session client, org from the session): every
  job not archived at its own address, else its customer's, coloured by layer
  — Active jobs (won, scheduled, in progress) · New leads · Estimating /
  Follow up · Proposals sent (a sent, live link; "Opened N times") · Bid
  invites (due, red under 48 h) · Complete and Lost (off by default) — and
  vendors at their contact's address as squares (`layerForJob()`). Layer
  switches (behind ☰ on a phone — no Layers button; Map · Sat sits in the
  header beside Clip, Coy 2026-10-09) with counts, job type, city and created filters (kept on the
  device, `sc.map.filters`), the legend, clusters below zoom 11, Map ·
  Satellite. A tap opens a card: Open job, Directions (Google Maps), Call,
  Send to Takeoffs; a vendor's: Vendor page, Call, Directions, Email. The
  search box finds addresses (`/api/aerial/suggest`) and the pins' own names.
  Customers have no pins of their own — they show through their jobs; the
  header's count says how many named customers have no address ("· 23
  unmapped"), with no banner or button about it (Coy, 2026-10-08: "You can
  remove this note"). **Points** are looked up with
  `findAddress()` (the satellite's geocoder), 30 a load, and kept on the row
  by **migration 090** (applied 2026-10-08 on Coy's "apply 090"; checked after: the four columns on both tables, both range checks, RLS unchanged) (`jobs` / `contacts` `lat`, `lng`, `geocoded_at`,
  `geocode_query` — the address looked up; a changed address is looked up
  again; a miss is kept so it isn't asked again). Until 090 is applied
  addresses are looked up on each visit, remembered by the server process, and
  the page says so.
  **Clip for takeoff** (the header's button; the phone's floating one; Send to
  Takeoffs on a card): satellite, a frame (up to 960 × 680 on a desktop, the
  width less 16px a side on a phone — Coy, 2026-10-08: "a little bigger") (dimmed outside, thirds, the scale
  "1 in ≈ 41 ft · zoom 20", a 50 ft bar, "imagery date not known"), **Use this
  frame** — which saves the clip there and then (its centre, zoom and size;
  the map is held still after it, so the job list can't change what was
  framed — Coy, 2026-10-08: on a phone the frame shrank for the list and cut
  off the driveway) — then **Which job?** (on a phone the whole screen under
  the clip bar, the saved frame as a thumbnail with its size in feet and
  ‹ Change the frame) — nearest first, search, or **+ New job here**
  (the street from Mapbox reverse geocoding, `GET /api/aerial/suggest?at=`,
  through `POST /api/jobs`). **Put it in Takeoffs** posts `/api/jobs/[id]/aerial`
  as the old tab did, now with the frame's size (`w`, `h` — 1,150 to 2,560
  pixels at the whole zoom saved, `clipPlan()` / `validClipSize()`, so a sheet
  is always a drawing) — the same one-page PDF with the map's scale for Coy's
  tap. A job's second aerial is "AERIAL 2", and so on (`isAerialSheet()`). Then
  "Aerial added to X · Measure it ›" opens Takeoff on that sheet, and the
  framed box stays on the map. Zoomed out past 16 the frame asks to zoom in.
  **On a phone since 2026-10-10 (Coy):** the floating Clip for takeoff button
  is gone (the header's Clip does it); the legend is an icon at the bottom left,
  closed until tapped; "17 pinned · 25 customers with no address" sits at the
  top of the Layers panel, not in the subheader; the Layers drawer opens over
  the search and every button on the map (the map's box is its own stacking
  layer). A search used to leave the whole map, its pins and the footer shifted
  sideways when the keyboard opened: the page, the map's box and its wrappers
  are `overflow: clip` (hidden boxes can still be scrolled), the window is put
  back to no sideways scroll whenever it moves, and the search box's text is
  16px.
  `?job=<id>` (Takeoffs → Files' **Pull satellite images**, under the drop box; Coy, 2026-10-10) opens on
  satellite with the job's address (its own, else its customer's — `GET /api/jobs/[id]/aerial`) typed in the
  search and found, pinned at zoom 19; Clip for takeoff then has that job picked. No address, or none found,
  says so under the search.
  `?addr=<address>` opens on that address, pinned (the contact card's Map button, 2026-10-10).
  **Street view on the Map (Coy, 2026-10-13: "the option on the map to see the street view of the address
  i'm looking at from satellite"; later that night: "it needs to replace the satellite view. I want to toggle
  between both in the map page header").** The header's toggle is **Map · Satellite · Street** on every size
  (it sat over the map on a desktop). Street fills the map's place with Google's Street View (`StreetFrame`,
  the same Maps Embed as the customer and job pages) of the picked pin, else the address searched, else a pin
  within 40 px of the middle of the map, else the middle itself; a search box above it moves it, and a pin
  card's **Street view** opens it. Map or Satellite brings the map back (Esc too); Clip hides while it shows. **No address card (Coy, 2026-10-13, on a phone: "a tag with the address … Remove that … I already
  have it in the header"):** the embed always draws Google's address card at the top left, so the Map's Street
  view is drawn with the Maps JavaScript API's panorama (`addressControl: false`, `StreetFrame bare`) on the same
  browser key; the key needs **Maps JavaScript API** added to its API restrictions (and enabled in the project),
  else Google refuses (`gm_authFailure`) and the embed is drawn as before. On a phone the line under the search
  ("<address> · Google Street View — to look at, not saved") is gone too. The customer and job pages keep the embed.
  **It is not clipped, by Coy's ask or otherwise:** Google's terms don't allow saving or storing its imagery
  (the same reason Google is a look only for aerials, §6 Takeoffs), and the embed can't be read by the page
  anyway. A photo of the front of a house goes in from the site, or texted (Files → Photos). Clip for takeoff
  stays on the Mapbox satellite.
  **Google Maps by default, Settings → Maps to switch (Coy, 2026-10-13: "set google maps as the default …
  a maps page in the settings where I can choose which one i want to use").** The Map page draws Google's
  roadmap and satellite (Map Tiles API, `p=google-map` / `google-sat` on `/api/aerial/tile`,
  `src/lib/google-tiles-server.ts`: a session per map type kept until it expires, Google's own Cache-Control
  passed on, nothing stored) unless **Settings → Maps** (`/dashboard/settings/maps`, `maps-settings.tsx`) is set
  to Mapbox — saved on the account (`map_provider`, no migration; `src/lib/map-provider.ts`, tested). The key
  is server-side: `GOOGLE_MAPS_TILES_KEY`, else `GOOGLE_MAPS_GEOCODE_KEY` with the Map Tiles API added to its
  allowed APIs. `GET /api/map/provider` says whether Google's tiles work; when they don't (no key, the API not
  enabled — remembered two minutes, Settings asking afresh) the page draws Mapbox and Settings → Maps says why in Google's own words — its status and reason, never the key — with what to change in Google Cloud (`refusalWhy()`: a key limited to websites or IP addresses, which a server call never passes; the API off in the key's project; the API not in the key's API restrictions; billing; tested). The corner carries
  Google's copyright for the view (`?copyright=1`, its viewport call). **Clip for takeoff is always Mapbox
  satellite** — Google's imagery is a look only, never saved or traced. **Compare with Google** beside Use
  this frame (Coy, 2026-10-13: "google maps is much better to clip from") swaps the map under the frame to
  Google's satellite to line it up, the frame saying "to look at only — the clip saves Mapbox's picture";
  Back to Mapbox, Use this frame or leaving the clip puts Mapbox back, so what is saved is always Mapbox's.
  **Not built:** vendors' delivery circles (their area is a line of text), a
  frame resized by its corner, customers' own pins, live directions or
  drive times.
  **The office and its radius (migration 098, applied 2026-10-10 on
  Coy's "apply 098"; checked after: RLS on, 2 policies, anon no grant,
  authenticated select/insert/update/delete, 0 rows; Coy: "set my office location in the settings of my organization …
  When I open the map, it should be centered on the office with a radius of
  … 80 miles").** `org_office` (one row per org: the address as the map
  spelled it, its point, `radius_mi` 0–1,000 or blank; members read, only
  owners write — `get_user_owner_org_ids()`; anon nothing). Settings →
  Organization → **Office and radius**: the street box finishing the address (Mapbox),
  Radius in miles, Save / Remove (`/api/org/office`, a typed address placed
  by Mapbox). The Map opens fitting the ring (else the office alone at zoom
  11, else the pins as before): a dark office marker, a dashed ring at the
  radius with "80 mi from the office" on its north edge, and a ⌂ button
  beside the map's controls that goes back to it (`ringPoints()` /
  `ringPx()` / `cleanRadius()` in `src/lib/map.ts`, tested). Nothing is
  filtered by the radius. Without 098 Settings would say the office
  waits for it and the Map would open on its pins.
- **The Scope Builder (Coy, 2026-10-09: "Inside a job, I've got the Scope
  Builder tool … it flows from files to budgets to takeoffs. To proposal …
  a dedicated page for the scope builder tool … separate from the jobs page").**
  `/dashboard/scope-builder` (`scope-builder.tsx`, `/api/scope-builder`,
  `src/lib/scope-builder.ts`, tested), on the rail before Jobs and in the
  Toolbox. Every scope not archived (Lost left out) with its flow — **Files ›
  Takeoffs › Budget › Proposal › Sent**, done steps ticked and the next one
  marked, each a link to that view — one line on where it stands, and its
  price from `jobValues()`. **Residential · Commercial & government** split
  the markets (job type public or commercial is commercial); a residential
  job measured in the field by section counts as measured without Takeoffs
  or files. Step chips filter by what each needs next; + New scope opens the
  Jobs board's New job form. Counts only, nothing written. A first cut for
  Claude Design to work up; the Jobs board stays the sales stages. Not for
  an onsite estimator.
**Coy's evening notes (2026-10-10).** The subheader's line is the org's name in Manrope 300 (the wordmark's "crete" grey), a small green dot, then the page; the ⓘ description is no longer drawn. The activity line drops its six dots, shows only background work (reads, files, bids — not Dusty's chat), in Manrope 500 at 12px in a softer green (#4DB88C), lower beside the ".ai"; a tap opens **Logs → Dusty** (`?tab=dusty`, `DustyLog`): the reader's warning, his thoughts (tap one to talk about it) and the last week's background work — the drop-down is gone. **Dusty left the dashboard header:** his emblem is a button on each page's subheader (`PageHeader dusty`, `DustyPageButton`, event `sc:dusty-toggle`), beside the job's tabs and on Threads' list header; his history rail opens to the left of his column on a desktop. His greeting's quick selects now cover a thread (New text, New email…), Threads, Scope Builder, Jobs, the Map, Customers, Vendors (Vendor, Receipt, Quote, Bill — each opening its form; `ReceiptImport kind`), the Price book (group, card, assembly) and the Roadmap. **A call is a bubble** on its side like a text (theirs left, ours right), with no Inbound/Outbound word; a call Quo marks `no-answer` (`call.completed` in the raw log, read by `/api/threads/[id]`) reads **Missed call** in red. The customer page's ⋯ has no Call notes; its facts have **Location** — Change / + Add with the Mapbox street finisher (`PATCH /api/customers/[id]`). A job's Summary sets its address the same way (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id]`). The bid request composer drops down under the header (`Drawer drop`, `.sc-drop-panel`) with a search over the jobs. The job's name is 16px with a rule after ‹ Jobs on a desktop. Every "colour" the app shows reads "color".
**A customer's or vendor's page header (Coy, 2026-10-11).** One line like the main pages' subheader: "‹ Customers • Arts Apartments • Overview" (a vendor's "‹ Vendors • … • Prices"), a green dot between each, the tab's word without its count (on a phone a tap on it opens the tabs, as ☰ does). The back link is always the list — never back to the thread — and there is no role tag after the name. A vendor's ☰ has no Call notes, and their Overview shows the address as a link over its satellite picture, like the customer's Location. The Price book picked from a budget has no ⓘ how-to, and its + Group / + Card sit on a row under the title. A vendor's Files tab on a desktop is the desktop Files view (its box had shrunk to its content in a flex column). The job Summary's **From the plans** reads a takeoff group whose shapes are in budget sections (069) as "in <section>", with those sections' items, their price and its price per unit — not "no budget line · no rate", which is left for a group in no section and counted by no line.
**Coy's phone notes (2026-10-11).** **Settings → Cost book** (`/dashboard/settings/cost-book`, `cost-book-settings.tsx`, a section in `settings-sections.ts`, not for an onsite estimator): **Import** — a spreadsheet (CSV, Excel, UFGS .sec) or a receipt, invoice or quote, the same `PriceImport` / `ReceiptImport` the Price book's Import ▾ opened — then the pricing defaults and project types that were the Price book's **Defaults** tab (Coy: "Move the import button to the settings page. Make a new page in settings for the cost book" and "Move the defaults tab to the settings"). The Price book is Cards · Assemblies; `?tab=defaults` goes to Settings → Cost book, and every "Price book → Defaults" the app said (the engine's blocks, tax, the budget, the proposal) reads "Settings → Cost book". The Price book's header is the search magnifier (its bubble under the header, the find row's box gone) and the emblem; **+ Group and + Card left the header** and are Dusty's quick selects **New group · New card**, opening the forms (`create`, `?new=group|card` / `sc:create`); a group's own + Card stays, and picking for a budget keeps both on its title row. **By group · By vendor sit on the Cards · Assemblies row** at its right on a phone too, the card count only on a desktop. **Customers' + New and Vendors' + Vendor and + Receipt or quote are gone** — the emblem's quick selects (New customer; Vendor · Receipt · Quote · Bill) open the same forms. Imports' Undo stays on Logs → Price book.
**Coy's desktop notes (2026-10-11, evening).** **Settings → Templates** (`/dashboard/settings/templates`, `templates-settings.tsx`, a section in `settings-sections.ts`, not for an onsite estimator): every contract and warranty (`doc_templates`, migration 060, `/api/doc-templates`) in a list; a pick opens it to rename, tick the project types it is offered for, change its title and its words (a warranty) or clauses (a contract, add and remove), Save, Discard and Delete (proposals already made keep their copy); + New per kind; **Ask Dusty** with the emblem asks him to rewrite what is in the editor, and Use this puts his boxed words in, unsaved. The proposal's settings row has **Edit templates ›**. The proposal's settings row has no **Project type** picker (the job's type is on its Summary); when the draft's type differs from the job's, a link offers to load the job's type defaults. Its two explanation lines are gone. **Start over** on the proposal's toolbar (`POST …/proposal/new-version {fresh: true}`, no migration — a final can't change and proposal numbers count every version, so nothing is deleted): every customer link on the job not signed is turned off (it reads replaced), and the open draft is reset to the job type's defaults, or a blank new version is made. The Scope Builder's market and step filters sit in a drawer behind ☰ left of the org's name, the ones in use as chips with ×; its + New scope is gone (Dusty's quick select). The job Summary's From the plans header reads "Takeoff parameters → group".
**Coy's desktop notes on the proposal and the job's tabs (2026-10-12).** The proposal's dark band sets the job's title at 20px (17 on a phone; it was 26 / 20). Above Scope of work a **Job information** line reads "Job site: <the job's address>" when the job has one (`vm.job.address`, so the customer's link and a final show it too). On a desktop the job's switch (Summary · Files · Takeoffs · Budget · Proposal) has arrows pointing right for dividers, each the previous tab's tip in its colour, so the row reads as the flow (`.sc-job-tab + .sc-job-tab::before`, `v34-shell.css`); the phone's tabs are unchanged.
**The job Summary's customer row (Coy, 2026-10-13, two toolbar notes).** Under the customer, the job's person with their phone (a call link) and email (`email` on `GET /api/jobs/[id]`'s contact). A job with no customer whose person has a name has **+ Make <name> the customer** beside + Attach a customer: one tap goes through `POST /api/customers {name, contact_id, customer_type}` (residential unless the job is commercial or government; the person becomes its primary, their own one-person customer renamed) and puts the job on it (`PATCH /api/jobs/[id] {customer_id}`); a person already on a customer with other people puts the job on that customer instead. A business with several people is still made on Customers and attached. No migration.
**Deleting a proposal version (Coy, 2026-10-12: "How do I delete proposals?"; migration 104, applied 2026-10-13 on Coy's "apply 104" — checked after: both columns, the guard trigger now before update or delete, RLS on, 11 finals and 7 drafts unchanged, none marked; rehearsed rolled back: editing a final refused, marking an unsigned final allowed, marking the signed one refused).** **Delete** on the proposal's toolbar acts on the version on screen (`DELETE /api/jobs/[id]/proposal/version?version=N`). A proposal's number counts every `job_proposals` row and a job's place comes from its first one, so nothing that would renumber another job is removed: **a draft over an earlier version** is deleted outright (the page goes back to the last final); **a job's only draft** is not (the button isn't drawn; Start over gives a blank one); **a final** is marked `deleted_at` / `deleted_by` (104), left out of the version list and every reader, and its customer link turned off (it reads replaced); **a version the customer signed stays** (no button, the route and the guard refuse). 104 also has `sc_job_proposal_guard()` let a final change only in `deleted_at` / `deleted_by`, refuse marking a signed one, and refuse any DELETE of a final. Every reader of finals leaves out a marked one, and a new version numbers past it.

  (`TourHost`, `src/components/v34/tour.tsx`; steps and placement `src/lib/v34/tour.ts`, tested in

  back, `sc:spaces-open` / `sc:spaces-close`), Settings; on the old menu the rail or footer and
  Dusty. **Job** the first time they open a job: its views, switching jobs, Dusty on a job. Started
  only once the account's prefs are read; seen per account (`tours_seen` in `/api/prefs`, no
  migration); skipping the first skips both. A step whose part isn't on screen is passed over.
  Never while viewing as a teammate. **Takeoffs and the Budget have their own (Coy, 2026-10-23: "Do we also have a tour guide of
  Takeoffs and budgets when he opens that on mobile and desktop?")** — the first time each is opened after the job tour: Takeoffs
  walks Sheets, the scale, the measuring tools (the desktop's column, the phone's drop-down), Parameters and $ Group, AI Takeoff,
  Back / Forward and Ask; the Budget a section, Measured by, Scope of work, the Budget row's + Add item and Dusty's emblem, Job
  totals, the price rail and its margin slider, and Market rate / Actual costs. Their parts are found by the labels the pages
  already carry (`aria-label`, `title`, `data-sec-id`, `.sc-param-panel`), so the pages themselves have no tour hooks; kept in
  `tours_seen` as `takeoffs` / `budget`. **Settings → Account → Take the tour again.** **Sample data
  (migration 119, applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "Apply 119" — checked after: RLS on with one
  policy, anon no grant, authenticated select only, the three functions SECURITY DEFINER and anon
  can't run them; Gulfline seeded the same day, 30 rows, two jobs, six budget lines; Sacco's 60 jobs
  unchanged):** `sample_records`
  (org, kind, row id, removed_at; members read, only the functions write) lists every row put in.
  `sc_seed_sample_data(org)` (SECURITY DEFINER; an owner, a platform admin, or the SQL editor) puts
  in a vendor, a residential customer and a GC with their people, three groups and seven Price book
  items, a priced driveway job (a section with its scope of work, a draft budget, a next step, a
  note, a site visit) and a bid-invite parking lot job — every name saying "(sample)", phones
  555-01xx, emails at example.com; refused when sample data is already in. `sc_remove_sample_data(org)`
  archives the jobs, customers and contacts, retires the items, dismisses the step, cancels the
  visit and stamps removed_at — nothing is deleted. Settings → Account → **Sample data** (owners add
  or remove; `/api/sample-data`, session client); the tour's welcome mentions it and its last card
  opens the sample job. Rehearsed (rolled back) on Gulfline: 30 rows, two jobs, six lines; a second
  seed refused; a stranger refused and saw nothing; removed, no open job or active item left;
  Sacco's 60 jobs unchanged. Not done: seeding new companies by themselves (Admin's New company
  could call it once 119 is in).
- **One subheader on every main-menu page (Coy, 2026-10-06: "notice that
  the logs page has a defined sub header. Each of the different pages on the

  scrolls (under `--sc-head`; its height is `--sc-subhead`, which Files'
  folder column sticks below); ☰ (phone) · the title (26px, 20 on a phone)
  and its count · the page's buttons on the right; one line on what the page
  is for; then the page's search bubble or filter rows. Dashboard, Jobs,
  Cash, Contacts, Customers, Price book, Vendors, Files, Logs and Roadmap use
  it (Files' description left its ⓘ for that line; Roadmap's paragraph is now
  the line). Threads keeps its own list header — its bucket name in colour
  and no description line (Coy, earlier). The Price book picked from a
  budget, and Files on a customer's tab, keep their own rows.
  **Since 2026-10-07 (Coy's notes on master):** the org's name (`orgs.name`,
  through `OrgNameContext` from the dashboard layout) is the band's big line,
  the page's title small and uppercase after it with its count, and the line

  "remove this line" on Logs, Jobs and the Price book. Customers, Vendors and
  Contacts fill the width (no 1156px cap). The Price book's find, By group /
  By vendor, Show retired, Columns and its count sit on the same row as its
  Cards · Assemblies tabs — By group / By vendor and Columns at the row's right on a desktop

  the page's read on one line, "Sacco Enterprises | JOBS", at ~11px; the ☰ is 28px, the height of

- **The Spaces navigation, step 1 (Claude Design "Dusty Home", Coy 2026-10-17: "map the new design to the live feedback site"; no migration).** Behind **Settings → Appearance → New navigation** (`user_preferences.prefs.new_nav`, set before first paint as `html[data-nav="spaces"]`); **on unless switched off** (Coy, 2026-10-17: "I'm not seeing it on feedback"), the switch there going back to the old rail. A 72px rail of **Spaces** — Dusty's emblem (Home), every open job (not complete, closed, lost or archived) as a two-letter tile in its Jobs-board stage color (look 1b; a hiviz edge on the one open), the threads Coy made (073) as collections, and a dashed + (New job) — beside a 280px drawer: on **Home** the app's pages (the menu Coy set) and the people in each bucket (Customers · Vendors · Personal · Unsorted, six each with All N ›; spam left out); in a **job** its Scope Builder tree (Summary · Takeoffs · Budget with Market rate and Proposed budget · **Scope tracker**, the Budget's Actual costs tab, once the job is won · Proposal · Files) and its **Phone** (one thread per number of each person on the job, its customer's people and its crew — Coy, 2026-10-16), **Email** (conversations filed on the job) and **Internal** (each person's notes) threads. `--sc-rail-w` is 352px (72px with the drawer folded, `spaces_folded`), so every page and sheet measured from the rail follows. **Threads under Spaces (Coy, 2026-10-17: "the old threads panel was nested inside the new one"):** the Threads page's own bucket rail is never drawn, and an open conversation fills the page with no list beside it (`.sc-old-thread-rail` / `.sc-old-thread-list` hidden under `html[data-nav="spaces"]`); `/dashboard/messages` with nothing open still lists the people of a bucket (the drawer's All N ›). **Settings is a gear at the rail's foot**; **on a desktop the drawer collapses** (Coy, 2026-10-17: "the inner drawer … should be collapsible on desktop") with « at its top right to a 64px strip beside the rail (Coy, 2026-10-18: "when I collapse the drawer it disappears"; frame 11) — › at its top opens it again, then the Space's pages as icons (Home: Buckets and Pages; a job: Summary, Takeoffs, Budget, Scope tracker, Proposal, Files), then its groups as small outlined chips in their colour (CU · VE · PE · UN, or PH · EM · IN), each opening the drawer; `--sc-rail-w` is 136px folded. **Rail tiles are silhouettes by kind** (Rail Silhouettes 1a, Coy: "different shapes/styles depending on if it's a customer / job / vendor"; `space-silhouette.tsx`): a job a clipboard in its stage colour, a thread made from a customer an address book (chalk), any other thread Coy made a folder (violet), a vendor a tag (drawn, no vendor Spaces yet); Home the emblem in a 52px rounded square, hiviz-ringed when open. Unread badges and live dots wait on unread counts. A phone's drawer is simply covered by the page picked; the header's ☰ is gone on a desktop; **on a phone the Spaces sit under the page** (Coy, 2026-10-17: "it should be underneath the pages") — the header's ☰ or a swipe right slides the page aside, leaving 56px of it to tap or swipe back, and picking anything slides it back over (while aside the page is a fixed layer scrolled to where the window was, the talk bar hidden). The swipe starts anywhere except on something that pans sideways itself (the plans, the map, a scrolling table or tab row, a text box, a sheet; `pansSideways()`), and always — the first 24px belong to the rail, caught before any row's own swipe (Threads, a customer's jobs, the Price book, email), so a row there never takes it. **The page follows the finger (Coy, 2026-10-18: "Need the page to slide over smooth from anywhere on the screen"):** past 8 px a sideways drag moves the page with the finger by transform alone, no transition while dragging — right on the page to open, left on the open Spaces or the page's edge to close — and on letting go it settles open or closed by a flick (over 0.5 px a ms) or past halfway, in 0.28 s; more up-and-down than sideways is a scroll (`usePageSlide`, which also holds the page aside). Threads and email rows don't swipe at all under it (Coy, same day). **No list on the Threads page for what the drawer lists** (Coy, same day: "there is no more page list of channels … they are now listed in the drawer"): with nobody open, All, Customers, Vendors, Personal and Unsorted show "Pick someone in the drawer" (Open the drawer on a phone), the drawer's All N opens the whole bucket in place, and a More group links Spam and Archived, which keep their list, as do the threads Coy made. **Threads is no longer a page in the drawer** (Coy, same day: "Remove the threads page entirely. Since it's already in the home drawer"): it is left out of Home's Pages under Spaces; its addresses stay, for an open conversation, Spam, Archived and the threads Coy made, and the old rail keeps it. **No back links at the top left** (Coy, 2026-10-18: "every other page is accessible by swiping the page over to the right to reveal the rail and drawer"): an open thread's ‹ Threads, a record page's ‹ Customers / ‹ Vendors, the contact card's ‹ Back and a job's ‹ Jobs / ‹ Customer carry `sc-back-link`, hidden under Spaces; the old navigation keeps them, and a flow's own ‹ Back (the Map's clip, the Camera) stays. **Shelves** (Coy, 2026-10-18: "inside the drawer for the Space, the groupings can be called 'Shelves' … renamed and customized similar to how on discord I create categories"; was Buckets above Pages): Home's drawer is shelves of pages — by default **Buckets** (Customers, Contacts, Vendors, Jobs), **Scope Builder** (the Scope Builder page; in a job Space its shelf is the job's own Summary · Takeoffs · Budget · Proposal tree) and **Toolbox** (Map, Camera, Price book, Files); a page on no shelf goes to a last shelf, **More** (Dashboard, Admin), so nothing is lost. **Arrange** on Home's header renames, adds (+ Shelf, 20 at most), removes (its pages go to More) and orders shelves, and moves or orders any page on them; **Put the default shelves back** resets. Kept on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.shelves`, no migration; `homeShelves()` / `cleanShelves()` in `spaces.ts`, tested), so per person, not per org. The folded strip's icons follow the shelves. **Logs and Roadmap left the drawer for Settings** — sections `/dashboard/settings/logs` and `/roadmap` (the same pages; their old addresses still work, and an onsite estimator keeps Roadmap). A job Space's shelves (Scope Builder, Phone, Email, Internal) aren't arranged yet. **On a phone there is no footer** (Coy, same day: "The phone footer does not stay because dusty icon is in the rail and to the right of the chat box"): its place is the **talk bar** (`talk-bar.tsx`, same 58px) — + attaches files to a chat with Dusty (`sc:dusty-attach`), a pill asks him about the page open, and his emblem on the right sends it or, empty, opens him; it isn't drawn on an open thread or email, whose own text box is the foot (`html[data-talk="off"]` sets `--sc-foot` to 0). An onsite estimator keeps the old footer. A desktop tile opens the job's Summary; a phone tile shows its drawer first. Dusty's column is open by default on a desktop (`--sc-dusty` 380px), open or closed kept on the device (`sc:dusty-col`); closed, a 44px strip with his emblem opens it. `src/lib/v34/spaces.ts` (tested, `tests/spaces.test.mjs`), `GET /api/spaces` (open jobs and collections; `?job=` its people's numbers and its emails — session client), `src/components/v34/spaces/spaces-nav.tsx`. **No header under Spaces (Coy, 2026-10-18: "remove the header entirely from mobile and desktop … we already have the dusty emblem in the rail that opens a home"):** `--sc-head-0` is 0 under `html[data-nav="spaces"]` (the View as strip stays), so the rail, drawer, page and Dusty's column run full height; the header's search, alerts bell and activity line sit in a bar at the top of the drawer (`.sc-spaces-bar`; icons alone on the folded strip). **Dusty's banner (Coy, 2026-10-21: "The dusty thoughts … should be moved to the very top of the site above everything else on a little row that is the full width of the page. It can scroll across slowly as a banner. And we can use different colors for different kinds of thoughts"; no migration):** the activity line left the drawer for a 24px dark band across the very top of the window (`DustyTicker`, `activity-line.tsx`; part of `--sc-head` as `--sc-ticker`, so the rail, drawer and Dusty's column start under it), sliding slowly left (45 px a second, two copies for a seamless loop) — the reader's warning, plans being read, the last day's background work, then his recent thoughts and dreams — each with a small label and its own colour: Thought green, Dream violet, Reading blue, Plans teal, Mail (emails and bids) yellow, Proposal amber, Due orange, Needs you red (`tickKind()`). It pauses under the pointer; a tap opens what it came from, a thought opens Dusty on it as Logs → Dusty does. Still under reduced motion (scrolled by hand). Settings → AI's thought-line switches turn it off. The old navigation keeps its line. **Dusty's right panel collapses** (Coy, same day: "the right panel (for chat and artifacts) should be collapsible"): » in his header on a desktop folds it to the 44px strip, which reads "Dusty · on <page>" with « to open it. **New Space asks what kind** (Coy, same day: "creating a new space starts a new job. But that is wrong. A space can currently be 1 of 4 different things"): the rail's + opens a dialog (`new-space.tsx`) — **Job** (the New job form), **Customer** or **Vendor** (one on file, or a new one by name through `POST /api/customers` / `/api/vendors`), **Collection** (a thread Coy makes, 073). A customer or vendor Space is a pin on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.spaces_pins`, 40 at most, `cleanPins()` / `addPin()`), drawn as an address book or a tag after the collections; its drawer is shelves of its page's tabs — a customer's **Account** (Summary, Contacts, Jobs, Threads, Payments, Schedule) and **Toolbox** (Files, Timeline), a vendor's **Supply** (Summary, Price sheets, Receipts, Bids, Jobs) and Toolbox — opening `?tab=` (the pages now follow `?tab=` as it changes), with Add to / Off the rail. **Shelves fold** (Coy, same day: "collapse/expand all Shelves … a settings button on the Shelf Group that allows me to add/remove whatever tools / channels"): every shelf and job group folds on its header, Collapse all / Expand all heads the drawer (kept on the device, `sc:shelves-closed`), and a gear on each of Home's shelves opens its editor — rename, move up or down, order, tick any page (tool) or channel (Customers, Vendors, Personal, Unsorted, More) onto it, delete; a channel on a shelf lists its people there, one on no shelf is a group of its own (`looseChannels()` / `offShelf()`, tested). **The rail is arranged** (Coy, 2026-10-18: "I need a way to remove and rearrange the Rail items"): **Arrange** (⇅, above the gear) lists every Space on the rail in order with ↑ ↓ and ×; on a desktop a tile is also dragged up or down the rail, and a right-click opens Arrange. × unpins a customer or vendor; a job or collection is only taken off the rail (the job and its thread are untouched) and listed under Off the rail with Put back; Default order undoes the moves. Kept on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.spaces_rail` {order, hidden}, `cleanRail()` / `orderTiles()` / `moveTile()` / `hideTile()` / `showTile()`, tested); a tile never moved keeps its place after the default tile before it, so a new job lands among the jobs, and a separator falls wherever the kind changes. **A job's Budget is three tools** (Coy, same day: "move these 3 tools … from being nested inside the budget tool and let it be its own tool window … on the shelf in the drawer"): the Scope Builder shelf is Summary · Takeoffs · Market rate · Proposed budget · Actual costs · Proposal · Files, each its own row and folded-strip icon (Actual costs on every job, as its tab was); under Spaces the Budget page's Market rate · Proposed budget · Actual costs row is hidden (`.sc-budget-tabs`, `.sc-job-subnav`), the old navigation keeps it. No migration. **A collection's tools and channels (Coy, 2026-10-18: "Collections aren't allowing me to actually pick tools/channels to add to the collection. it should be done inside the Space creator"):** New Space's Collection kind ticks tools (pages) and channels (the buckets, Spam and Archived) beside its name; the thread is made as before and the picks are kept on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.spaces_collections`, thread id → picks, no migration; `cleanCollectionPicks()` / `setPicks()` / `togglePick()`, tested). A collection's tile now opens its own drawer — People (its thread), Tools, then each channel's people — with **Tools & channels** to change the picks; the drawer stays while its own rows are opened. **Arrange deletes a collection** (Delete on its row, asked first, `DELETE /api/threads/custom/[id]`, the thread retired and its people untouched) — Coy's two rule threads (Email, Bid requests) are removed that way. **A vendor's page under Spaces** has no tab row (its tabs are the drawer's shelves, `.sc-record-tabs` hidden, a customer's too), and its Overview no longer shows Next steps or Where it stands · Dusty, nor a Next steps tab (Coy, same day); Threads joins the vendor's Supply shelf. **A vendor's cost book (Coy, same day: "Every vendor has it's own isolated cost book with Cost Groups and Cost items"; decision card: "View, by group")** is a view, not a second catalog: the vendor page's Prices tab (Supply shelf's **Cost book**) lists the items they price under the Price book's own groups (`group` on `GET /api/vendors/[id]`), each group folding, with their price, ★ default and the next-cheapest vendor; **Add or edit items in their cost book** opens their cards. One catalog, so Compare vendors and default prices still work; no migration. Groups carry across vendors (Coy: "I can sort the main cost book by group and see all the vendors that have cost items for that cost group … ask Dusty for any cost item and he'll know the group and see all the vendors that offer that item"): the Price book's By group row names the vendors who price something in it, and Dusty's Price book context lists each item's group and every vendor's price on it, ★ on the default (`priceBookBlock()`). **New Space in two steps, picking several (Coy, 2026-10-18: "it should slide to the next selection window (providing a back button at the top left), and the new window will be a searchable list … select multiple vendors, and multiple customers and multiple jobs … a 'Collection' Space … select from any number of Jobs, Customers, Vendors"; and "on mobile this space creator window should be full screen with the exit button at the top right"):** tapping a kind slides to its list (‹ Back top left, × top right) — every job not archived (`/api/jobs`), customer or vendor, searched by name and line, ticked several at a time (`ItemPicker`, `new-space.tsx`), with **+ New** at its top (a job opens the New job form; a customer or vendor is made by the typed name and ticked). **Add N to the rail** pins each one (`spaces_pins` now takes jobs too: an open job is already a tile and is only put back if taken off, a complete or lost one becomes a grey pinned job tile); one picked opens it. A **Collection** takes a name, any number of jobs, customers and vendors (All · Jobs · Customers · Vendors filter, kept on the account as `user_preferences.prefs.spaces_col_members`, `cleanMembers()` / `setMembers()` / `toggleItem()`, tested), their people put on its thread (`/api/threads/custom ?job= / ?customer=` previews, a vendor's own contact), and Tools & channels folded under them. Its drawer lists Jobs, Customers and Vendors as groups, and **Edit** there changes them (people of ones added join its thread) with the tools and channels. On a phone (under lg) the creator is the whole screen. No migration. **Channels and the Customer Account (Coy, 2026-10-18, six toolbar notes on HPC New Parking Lot):** every job, customer and vendor Space has a **Channels** shelf — **Phone** (one thread per number), **Email** (one thread per address of each person on it, opening their Email channel `?channel=email:<address>`, then any filed conversation from an address on no one, `emailThreads()`) and **Internal** (each person's notes) — folding one by one under the shelf; `GET /api/spaces?customer=` / `?vendor=` give a record Space its people with every email and its filed conversations. A job's Scope Builder shelf is **Customer Account**: Summary · Takeoffs · Market rate · Proposed budget · Actual costs · Proposal · **Schedule** · **Locations** · Files. **Schedule** (`/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/schedule`) holds the Appointments and Schedule cards, **Locations** (`…/locations`) the Job site map, the customer's and owner's addresses (Customer locations) and this job's and the customer's other jobs' (Job sites), each with Map; the Summary no longer has them, and **What's next is gone** — its one-time pull of the job's texted photos runs from the Summary itself. An onsite estimator is sent to the job page from both, as from the Summary. **Home's Jobs bucket is the Scope Builder layout** (`SPACES_HREF`, the page titled Jobs under Spaces), and the Scope Builder row and shelf are gone (`MERGED_PAGES`; a saved shelf drops it); the Jobs board keeps its address. **In the drawer, not beside it (Coy, 2026-10-19: "The people I added to this collection should be in the drawer. Not in an additional side panel"; "If it's a customer, this should say 'Customer Account'"; the job's view row "can be removed since it's already in the drawer"):** a collection's drawer lists its thread's people (`/api/threads?thread=`), each opening their conversation with the collection's drawer kept (`?t=<collection>`) — since later that day not under a "People" group but by what they are (Coy: "I don't want a people shelf … if I'm selecting a customer, it should be a customer"): Jobs, Customers, Vendors, Personal, Unsorted, the jobs, customers and vendors picked first, then each person on the thread under their bucket (`contact_type`) with their role (Sub, Cust. contact) and company, anyone a picked customer or vendor already covers left out, and the Threads page shows no list for a collection (a rule thread keeps its feed); a customer Space's first shelf is **Customer Account**; a job's Summary · Files · Takeoffs · Budget · Proposal row is hidden under Spaces (`.sc-job-tabs-nav`), the old navigation keeps it. No migration. **The drawer stays in your Space (Coy, 2026-10-19: "when I slide the drawer open from any page, it should go back to whatever space I'm in"):** a thread, an email, Files or any page that isn't a Space's own used to show Home's drawer, since the drawer followed the address. Now the Space whose drawer a row was picked from (a shelf, channel or tool), or whose own page was opened last, stays the drawer's on such pages; only a Home row, the Home tile or ‹ Customers / ‹ Vendors go back to Home. No migration. **Rail settings (Coy, 2026-10-21: "Let's call this the rail settings page. It can have 2 tabs 'on the rail', 'off the rail'. And you can put the settings for each rail icon on a row below the rail image and name").** Arrange (⇅ at the rail's foot) is now **Rail settings**, with **On the rail** and **Off the rail** tabs and their counts; each Work Space is its icon and its whole name (wrapped, never cut off), with ↻ · ◇ · Delete · ↑ ↓ × or Put back on a line of their own under it (`arrange-rail.tsx`). Nothing else changed: the same prefs, moves and icon redraws. **Taking things out of your own Work Space (Coy, 2026-10-21: "I have Elena Sacco there as a customer but I don't know why and I want to remove her"; no migration).** Elena, Lopez and Sam were put on Fort polk's thread by hand when it was made on Oct 18, before Edit listed a thread's own people, so Edit showed nothing ticked and no way to take her out. Edit on a Work Space you made now starts with its name (rename, `PATCH /api/threads/custom/[id]`) and **In it now** — every job, customer and vendor picked, every person on its thread and every tool, each with × (a person leaves the thread, `DELETE …/contacts?contact_id=`; a picked customer or vendor takes its people with it; a tool is unticked); Add jobs, customers and vendors, Collections, Tools and Threads follow. A shelf (Customers, Vendors, Tools) goes when nothing is left on it. Home's shelves are still renamed and removed with the gear on each. **A job, customer or vendor in your own Work Space is a shelf of its pages, not a link to its Space (Coy, 2026-10-22: "adding a customer to a space should not be attaching a link to the customer space, but referencing that customer (with all the pages and contacts and vendors associated with the customer) … Perhaps the job needs to be a shelf inside a custom space that brings all the pages to the space"; no migration).** Each one picked into it is a shelf named for it with its kind's tag: its pages in the order its kind's drawer has them (a job's Summary · Takeoffs · Market rate … Files; a customer's Summary · Contacts · Jobs …; a vendor's Supply), then its Phone · Email · Internal threads (`MemberShelf`, read from `/api/spaces?job=|customer=|vendor=` only while the shelf is open), the page open lit. Nothing is copied: the rows open the record's own pages. **The drawer stays your Work Space** on those pages — the Space you are in is kept when the page opened belongs to one of its members (`holdsSpace()`, `src/lib/v34/spaces.ts`, tested), and a tap on that job's, customer's or vendor's own tile on the rail still opens its own Space. Its people that a picked customer or vendor covers are under that shelf's channels; anyone else stays under their bucket. The folded strip shows a chip per member. **Work Spaces and Collections (Coy, 2026-10-21, answering the database-vs-UI audit, /mnt/project-files/navigation/database-vs-ui-audit.md §6; no migration).** The rail's items are **Work Spaces** (New Work Space, Arrange, the tips); one Coy makes (Fort polk) is a Work Space too — New Work Space's fourth kind reads **Your own** — and "Collection" is no longer its name. The four list pages (Customers, Contacts, Vendors, Jobs) are **Collections**: Home's first shelf reads Collections (a shelf saved as "Buckets" under its default id is read as Collections, `cleanShelves()`); a bucket is only how a person is filed, and "channel" is left to Phone · Email · Internal. **No "people" in the pickers (Coy, 2026-10-22: "Its just confusing if you call a customer 'people' and a job 'people' and contacts people. I want to call customers: customers"; no migration — "people" was never a table, only a label).** A shelf's gear and a Work Space's Edit list three sections (`pickSections()`, `COLLECTION_PAGES`, tested): **Collections** — Customers, Contacts, Vendors, Jobs, once each; **Tools** — Dashboard, Map, Camera, Price book, Files (never Logs, Roadmap or the Scope Builder, which aren't drawer rows under Spaces); **Threads** — Customer, Vendor, Personal and Unsorted threads (the people filed that way, listed in the drawer), and Spam and Archived. A Work Space's drawer puts the lists it picked under Collections, apart from its Tools. **A customer with an open job is a Work Space of its own**: `GET /api/spaces` returns `customers` (`openJobCustomers()`, `src/lib/v34/spaces.ts`, tested), each drawn as an address-book tile after the jobs and Coy's own Work Spaces, × taking it off the rail like a job; a customer he pinned keeps its pin, and every customer stays in the Customers collection. The job's shelf keeps Coy's name, Customer Account. **The org's layout, shared (migration 114, applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "apply 114, 115 and 116" — checked after: RLS on, 3 policies, authenticated select/insert/update and anon nothing, Sacco's row seeded from Coy's layout).** `org_layouts`: one row per org holding the layout keys (`shelves`, `spaces_pins`, `spaces_rail`, `spaces_collections`, `spaces_col_members` — `LAYOUT_KEYS`, `src/lib/v34/layout.ts`, tested); members read it under `get_user_org_ids()` (so an onsite estimator keeps their own), owners alone write it; seeded from the owner's saved layout. `GET /api/prefs` fills each layout key a person hasn't set from the org's (`withOrgLayout()`), so a new teammate starts with Coy's layout and what they change is theirs alone; an owner's layout change is written to the org's too. What anyone may see is still RLS on the jobs, customers and vendors. Before 114 the reads and writes fail quietly and everything is per account as before. Not built: a Reset to the company layout button. **A job's own contacts (migration 115, applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "apply 114, 115 and 116" — the database already had an empty `job_contacts` from an early migration (role customer · vendor · other, status, org-scoped policies, moved by `sc_merge_contacts`), so 115 extends it: `role` optional (its check kept), `side`, `is_primary`, `source`, `created_by` added, unique (job_id, contact_id); anon's leftover grants revoked. Checked after: RLS on, its 2 member policies, the three triggers, 58 rows = the 58 jobs with a person, each primary).** `job_contacts` (job, person, side customer · owner · vendor · other, one primary, a role, source default · coy · bid · dusty; RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`, a guard keeping job and person on the row's org, anon nothing): who is on THIS job, no longer every person of its customer. A new job with only a customer that has one person gets that person (`jobs_default_contact`); the job's person, and any new one, is put on it (`jobs_contact_follow`, which never takes anyone off); every job's person is backfilled as primary. Bid intake already sets the job's person to the estimator read off the forwarded email, so that person is pre-selected with no edge deploy (tagging them `bid` waits on one). The Summary's Contacts on this job lists who is on it, with **Take off** (not the primary) and, under it, the customer's and owner's other people with **+ On this job**; + Add contact puts the person on the customer and on the job (`POST` / `DELETE /api/jobs/[id]/contacts`, session client; `on_job` and `job_contacts_supported` on `GET /api/jobs/[id]`). Before 115 the card reads as before. **Vendor accounts with people (migration 116, applied 2026-10-22 on Coy's "apply 114, 115 and 116" — checked after: the live body was the one it was built on (md5), the new condition in it, still invoker, anon can't run it).** A vendor already has an account: filing someone vendor makes them a `customers` row with relation vendor or sub (32 such rows that day, one person each). The vendor page has a **People** tab listing that row's people, **+ Add person** (the pick is filed vendor if they aren't, then put on it with the customer page's own `sc_attach_contact`) and Take off (`sc_detach_contact`); `account` and `people` on `GET /api/vendors/[id]`. 116 changes one rule in `sc_classify_contact`: a person sharing an account is no longer split off when filed vendor on an account that is a confirmed vendor or sub, so a vendor's second person stays when filed again; personal, spam, or vendor on a customer's account split as before (§2f), so the lead guard is unchanged. Before 116 the tab works, but re-filing a second person splits them off. A vendor without an account (filed by Dusty, not kept) says to keep them first. **Dusty in the main window (Coy, 2026-10-19: "selecting the Dusty Home in the rail should open up dusty chat in the main window with the right panel becoming an artifacts panel"; no migration).** On a desktop the Home tile opens `/dashboard/dusty` (Home's drawer also leads with **Chat with Dusty**): his chat fills the page between the drawer and the right edge (`html[data-dusty-main]`), and the right column is **Artifacts** — what he made in the open chat, newest first, by kind (Plans · Tables · Drawings · Words · Files · Links — a drawing is its ```drawing block, drawn as in the chat, taken out before tables and links so its xmlns is no link): each plan he wrote with its steps by kind, the words he boxed for a page, every table, the files that came with a question (opening in Files) and the links in his answers. Read off the chat itself (`artifactsOf()`, `src/lib/dusty/artifacts.ts`, tested in `tests/dusty-artifacts.test.mjs`; the panel publishes its turns through `setDustyChat()` in `dusty-live.ts`), nothing stored apart from the conversation; a card opens to its words or table, and **Show it in the chat** (Apply it in the chat for a plan) scrolls to and flashes that answer. On a phone Home still opens his drawer; `/dashboard/dusty` shows the artifacts under the chat, with an Artifacts button in the chat's header. **Threads v3, first pass (Claude Design "Dusty Threads", Coy 2026-10-19: "a revision of how threads look and feel on desktop and mobile"; no migration).** Under Spaces a thread's header is the person's initials square, their name (to the contact card) and tag, and under it the channel open — "Cell · (225) 320-4143", an email address or Internal notes — with their customer or vendor; a green **Call** pill on every size; the channel tabs are small chips with no counts. The old phone breadcrumb and desktop name are left to the old navigation. **From <line> ▾** sat under the text box when the org had two lines or more; it is gone since 2026-10-24 (Coy: "remove the 'from quo Batonrougeconcreters' label in the footer of every thread"), the line picked in the thread's ⋯ (Call or text from) and on a desktop's Call from ▾. In the drawer each Phone thread shows its last call or text ("You: …", "Call · 3 min") and when (`shortWhen()`, Central; `last` on each number from `GET /api/spaces`, `phoneThreads()`, tested). Not built yet: unread counts and mute (they need a migration — what each person has read), the email timeline with subject dividers and Dusty's draft docked, the hold menu (Mark unread, Mute, Add to a Space, Move, Archive), and the person card beside the thread. **The Vendors list's trades in the drawer (Coy, 2026-10-19: "nested under the Vendors bucket in the drawer … stay collapsed … expand once the page is selected").** While the Vendors list is open its trades and kinds, with counts, sit under the Vendors row of Home's drawer (`vendor-filter.ts`, a small store the page fills and clears); a pick filters the list and, on a phone, slides the page back. Under Spaces the page's own trade column and chips are hidden and a chip shows the pick with ×. **Back to Home from a customer or vendor (Coy, 2026-10-19: "I need a back button here once I click into a customer or vendor"):** their drawer starts with ‹ Customers / ‹ Vendors, which shows Home's drawer again without leaving the page. Not built yet: Dusty's tool windows inside his panel, unread counts and live dots on tiles, the talk bar under every page, right-click / hold menus, deliverables. **Every drawer edited the same way, and says what it is (Coy, 2026-10-22, four toolbar notes: "Everything on the rail is supposed to be a kind of 'workspace' that has an editable drawer", "it should define what type of space it is … What is fort polk; a job or a customer?", "a gear icon further in the top right corner … across from the job name", and "Its confusing for this shelf to say 'customer account' when it's really a job"; no migration).** Every drawer's head is its name with a gear at the top right across from it, then a tag saying what kind of Space it is — Home, Job (and its stage, then its customer), Customer, Vendor, or **Your own Work Space** with what it holds ("1 customer · 1 person"), since one Coy made lists only what was picked into it, not every open job (`SpaceHead`).
