Home's gear is its Arrange; a Work Space of his own's is its Edit. **A job's, customer's or vendor's gear edits that kind's drawer:** its shelves renamed, moved, added and removed, each page moved up or down, to another shelf or out of the drawer (and put back), the Channels shelf moved, renamed or taken out, Put the default drawer back, and **On the rail / Take it off the rail** for this one (a hidden tile shown, else pinned). One layout per kind, so every job's drawer reads the same — kept as `user_preferences.prefs.space_layouts` and, as a layout key, shared from the org's layout (114) like Home's shelves (`defaultLayout()` / `cleanLayout()` / `placeRow()` / `removeKindShelf()` in `spaces.ts`, tested; a page the app adds later goes on the first shelf). **A job's first shelf is Job** (Summary · Takeoffs · Market rate · Proposed budget · Actual costs · Proposal · Schedule · Locations · Files); a customer's stays Customer Account. **The open Work Space reads as one piece with its drawer (Coy, 2026-10-22: "make it clear that a workspace is connected to the drawer under it"; no migration).** The open tile sits on a tab in its colour (a job's stage, a customer chalk, a vendor blue, your own violet, Home hiviz — `--sc-space-c`) that runs from the rail into the drawer, the drawer carries a 2px edge of it and its head an 8% tint with a bar on top, and the head starts with the kind's icon (clipboard, address book, truck). A Work Space you made has no kind tag, there being one kind. **The tab is the folder's tab (Coy, same day: "remove the inner border … just the tab and down the side of the drawer"):** the drawer's coloured edge is left out where the tab meets it (SpacesNav measures the open slot into `--sc-tab-a` / `--sc-tab-b`, the edge a 2px gradient), and the open tile and Home's emblem carry no ring or border of their own — nor a Dusty-drawn icon. **Shelf icons (Coy: "the standard shelfs would use standard icons"):** every shelf header has an icon — Collections, Toolbox, More, Job, Channels, Customer Account and Supply fixed (`shelfIcon()`, `src/lib/v34/spaces.ts`, tested), a shelf you made a folder until you pick one of `SHELF_ICONS` beside its name in its editor (kept as `icon` on the shelf in `shelves`, `space_layouts` or `spaces_col_shelves`). **+ Add and × on every shelf (Coy: "I still can't add or subtract things from a shelf"):** Home's Arrange and a kind's gear end each shelf with + Add to <shelf> (Collections, Tools and Threads, or that Space's pages) and give each row ×, which takes it off (to More on Home, out of the drawer for a kind). **The Map's layers panel folds (Coy: "This panel needs to be colapsable")** on a desktop with « to a strip, » to open it, kept on the device (`sc:map-layers-folded`).
- **A clear path to a new job (Coy, 2026-10-22: "the add new job button doesn't make a clear path to add a new job"; no migration).** The Jobs page (Scope Builder) has **+ New job** on its header again. The New job form reads Step 1 of 2 · Who is it for? (a line saying to pick the customer or skip, **Skip — no customer yet ›**, then the list) and Step 2 of 2 · Job name, Job site address and Type, all but the type optional, then **Create the job**; the name and address go to `POST /api/jobs` as `title` / `address`.
- **The New job page (Coy, 2026-10-23: "a page to create a job with a customer that is really easy to get to. And it will create a new space for the job with all the pages"; no migration).** `/dashboard/jobs/new` (`src/app/dashboard/jobs/new/page.tsx`), opened by a **New job** tile on the rail (a clipboard with a +, above the + for a new Work Space) and the Jobs page's **+ New job**: 1 · Customer — search Customers, or **+ New customer** (name, contact person, phone, email; `POST /api/customers`, its 409 on a known number or email shown); 2 · Job — name and job site address; 3 · Type (the customer's type picked to start). **Create the job** makes it (`POST /api/jobs`, then `PATCH {customer_id}` for a picked customer, which puts its primary on the job) and opens its Summary; as an open job it is a Work Space on the rail at once (`sc:spaces-reload`), with its Job shelf and Contacts. The old New job sheet stays for the Jobs board and Dusty's quick select.
- **Groups on the rail (Coy, 2026-10-22: "add workspaces to groups inside the rail by clicking and dragging one workspace on top of another workspace, just the same way Discord does it, and the same way android and apple phone can click and drag app icons into groups of apps"; no migration).** A Work Space is dragged on the rail — a mouse once it moves 6px, a finger after a 350ms hold (a quicker move scrolls), the page not sliding while it does — and a copy of it follows the pointer. Let go over the middle of another Work Space and the two become a **group**: one tile with up to four of their icons in a little grid, named from what is in it (Jobs, Customers, Vendors, Work Spaces, else Group). Over the top or bottom quarter of a tile it moves before or after it, as Arrange's ↑ ↓ did; onto a group it joins it; between the members of an open group it joins there; onto the + it leaves its group for the end. A group is dragged whole, before or after a tile, never into another. A tap opens a group in place on the rail, its members under a folder head on a tint (a tap on the head closes it; open or closed is kept on the device, `sc:rail-groups-open`); a closed group holding the open Space shows it open. A right-click, or a hold without moving, on a group opens its menu — the name, Open / Close, **Ungroup** — and on a tile opens Arrange as before. A group left with one Work Space is undone; taking a Work Space off the rail takes it out of its group. Kept with the rail's order as `spaces_rail.groups` [{id, name ≤ 30, keys}] (a key in one group at most, two keys or more, 40 groups), so it is shared from the org's layout (114) like the rest (`railItems()`, `groupTiles()`, `placeKeys()`, `ungroupTile()`, `renameGroup()`, `dissolveGroup()` in `spaces.ts`, tested; `rail-groups.tsx`). Arrange still lists every Work Space one by one.
- **Contacts, not channels (Coy, 2026-10-22: "Instead of adding a 'thread' I would rather select a 'Contact' … Any contact will have one single thread page (redesigned) nested under it" and "everything in the app should pivot around jobs"; no migration).** The Channels shelf (Phone · Email · Internal) is now **Contacts**: the people on the job, customer or vendor, one row each with their newest call or text under the name, opening their one thread (`/dashboard/messages/<id>` — every number, email and notes; Design will redo that page), and **+ Add contact** to the job's Summary, the customer's Contacts or the vendor's People (`contactThreads()`, `src/lib/v34/spaces.ts`, tested). A job's are its own contacts (115), else its customer's people, plus its person and crew. A customer's and vendor's drawer also has **Jobs** — theirs, open first, to each job's Summary (`jobs` on `GET /api/spaces?customer=|vendor=`; a vendor's from `job_vendors` across their account's people, 116). Customer Account is just Summary (it shows the contacts and jobs), Toolbox Files; Schedule and Payments left (they belong on the job), Contacts, Jobs, Timeline and Threads left as their own rows; a vendor's Supply is Summary · Cost book · Receipts · Bids. Saved layouts carry over: a shelf named Channels reads Contacts, and Jobs is put in before it (`cleanLayout()`); both are lists the gear can hide and put back. In your own Work Space each job, customer or vendor shelf lists its Jobs and Contacts the same way, and **+ Add** has **Contacts**: one person picked from the contact book onto its thread (a vendor being vetted). The shelf gear's Threads section is gone (Collections and Tools). Numbers on no customer or vendor are in Contacts (Unsorted on its role chips). **One line on the rail (Coy, same day: "keep the line under home and drop the others")** — the lines between jobs, your own Work Spaces and pinned ones are gone.
- **One set of lists under Collections (Coy, 2026-10-22: "I have the same lists shown below it on the home drawer … I would rather merge it all under collections … if I'm looking at a Customer list page … and THEN click into a customer, the drawer would change into an expanded list of customers with a back button on the top left … This same format would apply for all of the collection pages"; no migration).** Home's drawer no longer lists Customers · Vendors · Personal · Unsorted with their people under its shelves (`homeLooseGroups()` keeps only Spam and Archived, now inside the More shelf); the list pages, with their filters and search, are where the people are, and Personal and Unsorted people are on Contacts' role chips. A bucket put on a shelf with its gear still lists its people, and only then are the threads read. **The list in the drawer:** open Customers, Vendors, Contacts or Jobs from Collections, then a record from that page, and the drawer is that collection's list — ‹ Home at its top left, the collection's name and count, a search box, "All … — the list page", then each row (150 shown, the rest found by typing), the open record lit with its own shelves nested under it (`CollectionListDrawer`, `spaces-nav.tsx`; rows from the list page's own API, `collectionListRows()` / `filterListRows()`, `collectionListAt()` / `collectionRecordAt()` in `spaces.ts`, tested). On a phone, swiping back shows the same. The list stays while you go from record to record in it (kept across the nav's remounts); ‹ Home, a rail tile, or a page that is neither the list nor one of its records ends it, so a rail tile still opens the record's own Work Space. Jobs opened from Collections are the Scope Builder list (`SPACES_HREF.scope`). **Every contact opens their contact card** (Coy, 2026-10-22: "All of the contacts, when clicking into them should look like this. It's just a contact information page … the 'Contacts' list should be ONLY that: Contacts") — on Contacts and in the drawer's Contacts list alike, a vendor or a customer's person too (`/dashboard/messages/<id>?person=1`), so the list stays flat with no record's shelves under it; the card's **Customer** row (Vendor for someone filed vendor, opening their vendor page) is where they are connected: Change lists customers and vendors (a vendor's account, `account_id` on `GET /api/vendors`), and a pick files them that way if they aren't and puts them on it (`/api/customers/[id]/contacts`). The Customers and Vendors lists open the customer and vendor pages.
- **Shelf editors on a phone (Coy, 2026-10-22: "I can't read the pages that are listed on the shelf … group two rows of settings on mobile"; no migration).** Under lg every shelf editor (Home's Arrange and gear, a kind's gear, a Work Space's Edit) puts each row's page name on its own line, whole and wrapping, with its ↑ ↓, shelf picker and × right-aligned under it; a shelf's name box keeps at least 150px, its ↑ ↓ × going under it when they don't fit (`v34-shell.css`).
- **A customer's jobs open in its drawer (Coy, 2026-10-22: "if I click into a job, it gives me a new drawer for that job … I would prefer … all the pages for that job … listed and expandable underneath the job list for that customer in the customer drawer"; no migration).** In a customer's or vendor's drawer (and under a customer or vendor in your own Work Space) each job on the Jobs shelf folds open in place to the job's pages, in the job drawer's order, and its contacts with + Add contact (`JobRows` / `JobNest`, `spaces-nav.tsx`; the job read from `/api/spaces?job=` when opened); the job open is opened. On those pages the drawer stays the customer's (or the Work Space's): the stay is kept when the page is one of that customer's or vendor's jobs, read from its drawer's own list. The job's own tile on the rail still opens the job's drawer, and makes the job the Space you are in.
- **The gear is a cog (Coy, 2026-10-22: "right now they look like a Sun"; no migration).** The drawer heads', the shelves' and the rail foot's Settings gear, and Threads' colours gear, are one eight-toothed cog round a small hole (`currentColor`), no longer a ring with rays.
- **One + Add, and Delete in the gear (Coy, 2026-10-22: "How do I easily delete a work space? Also why is there an + add button AND an add shelf button. There should just be one place to add and name at the top"; no migration).** In a Work Space you made, the shelf box at the foot of the drawer and in its Edit are gone: + Add at the top opens one panel that starts with **New shelf** (a name and + Shelf), then jobs, customers and vendors, contacts, lists and tools. Its gear's Edit ends with **Delete this Work Space** — asked first, the same `DELETE /api/threads/custom/[id]` as Rail settings (the thread retired, its people and their threads kept), the drawer going back to Home.
- **Shelves set apart, and collections counted (Coy, 2026-10-22: "Show the count of customers, contacts, jobs and vendors to the right of each of those collections" and "I want all Shelves in every drawer to be more distinct visually like a line under the name of the shelf and an editable color for the line"; no migration).** Every shelf's name in every drawer (Home, a job's, customer's or vendor's, your own Work Space, and the groups with no saved shelf) has a 2px line under it, inset from the drawer's edges. Its colour is a role from the menu's colour book (accent by default; chalk blue, green, teal, violet, red, amber, unsorted orange, ink — `SHELF_LINES`, `shelfLine()`, `cleanShelfColor()` in `spaces.ts`, tested), so it follows every theme, picked with the swatch beside the shelf's icon in Home's Arrange and gear, a kind's gear and a Work Space's Edit. Kept as `color` on the shelf in `shelves`, `space_layouts` and `spaces_col_shelves`, so it shares from the org's layout (114) like the rest. A Customers, Contacts, Jobs or Vendors row in a drawer shows how many there are at its right, read from the list page's own API and kept three minutes (`CollCount`, the same read as the collection's drawer list); Jobs leave out lost ones, as the Scope Builder's list does.
- **Coy's mobile notes on threads and email (2026-10-24; no migration).** Under Spaces a thread's ⋯ has **Call or text from** (the org's lines, ✓ on the one in use) when the org has two or more. **Closing New email goes back where it was opened** — the person's thread (`/dashboard/messages/<contact>`), or the thread Coy made it from — not the inbox. **Attach from Files** in the email composer is a panel that slides up from above the text box, not a sheet: folders like the Files page (this job, this person, Photos, Plans, Specs, Vendor quotes, All files — `attachFolders()`; down the left on a desktop, chips across on a phone), the files with + / ✓, its search behind an icon so a phone's keyboard doesn't open by itself, Upload from this device and Done; Esc closes it.
- **The contact thread, step 1 (Claude Design "Contact Thread", 2026-10-23, saved at /mnt/project-files/navigation/contact-thread-design/; Coy, 2026-10-24: "did you see the new threads design from Design?"; no migration).** Under Spaces (not on the customer page's tab, not the old navigation) a contact's thread is one timeline of every channel: calls, voicemails and texts on every number, and their emails in and out, oldest first, a subject line wherever the subject changes (Re: / Fwd: left off), an email folded to "You" or the sender, its first line and the time, opening to its words with Reply · Reply all · Forward — each opening the email conversation with that composer open (`?mode=`). The header (`ContactThreadHead`, `contact-thread-head.tsx`): their initials in a round badge, the name to the contact card, "Customer: Gulfline Paving" (or Vendor) to its page, then round buttons — search, Notes with its count (desktop; in ⋯ on a phone), **Phone ▾** (each number to call, and a funnel to show only calls & texts) and **Email ▾** (New email, and a funnel for email only); one filter at a time, tapping it again shows everything, and a bar under the header says what is shown with its count, the numbers or addresses, Call or New email, and Show all. Search drops a pill under the header that searches what the filter shows — every word, any case — with its match count; Esc clears it. ⋯ holds **Text size** S · M · L · XL (1 – 1.5×), **Background** (Plain, Survey dots, Blueprint, Contour, Broom finish, First pour — faint, from the theme's colours), **Font** (Plex, Clear = Atkinson Hyperlegible, Round = Lexend, Serif = Source Serif 4), kept on the device for every thread (`sc:thread-read`), then Contact details, All notes, Where things stand (Dusty), the customer or vendor page, Move thread, New scope, Archive. The text box texts unless Email is the filter. `src/lib/v34/contact-thread.ts` (tested in `tests/contact-thread.test.mjs`); `/api/threads/[id]` now gives each email its direction and conversation. Still to build: the text box's "/" blocks, chips and Review & send, the emblem's tap and hold; the right panel (Contact · Notes · Dusty); Mute, which needs a migration.
- **Coy's six toolbar notes of 2026-10-24 (no migration).** **The job switcher** drops down under the page header (`Drawer drop`), its search kept but not focused, so a phone's keyboard doesn't open by itself. **A menu closes when you tap elsewhere** ("This should be true for every hamburger menu on the app"): `useDismiss()` (`src/lib/v34/use-dismiss.ts`; a tap outside or Esc) on the record pages' and thread's ⋯, the Price book's Sort and Columns, an appointment's Add to calendar, and a shelf's icon and colour picks; the menus that already closed so were left. **The drawer lights the tab on screen:** it reads the address as it changes (`useLiveSearch()`), so a vendor's Bid requests no longer shows Receipts and quotes picked. **The Jobs list (Scope Builder)** has **Filter · Sort**: Sort by last changed, newest, oldest, name or price (kept on the device, `sc:scope-sort`), and in its drawer a Stage filter (every stage but Lost, with counts) beside Market and Next step; **Board** opens the Jobs board (the kanban, `/dashboard/scope`). **No Dusty emblem on page headers under Spaces** — his chat is in the page (`.sc-dusty-page-btn` hidden under `data-nav="spaces"`); a customer's ⋯ now starts with Edit customer and New job, which were only under the emblem. **The Files folders** (All files, Recent, the kinds, Jobs · Customers · Vendors · Contacts) are under the drawer's Files row while the page is open, not a column on the page or a ☰ on a phone (`files-folders.ts`, published by the page like `vendor-filter.ts`); the old navigation keeps the column, and a customer's Files tab keeps its own.
- **One thread per contact, said plainly (Coy, 2026-10-22, toolbar notes; no migration).** A thread's header under Spaces reads "Jeremy Wright w/ Gulfline Paving", the company a link to their customer or vendor page (left out when it is their own name); the role tag is gone from it (who they are is on the contact card), and the line under the name is the channel alone. The Threads page with nobody open says "Swipe right to reveal the Drawer and Work Spaces." on a phone, and on a desktop "Select an item from the Shelf to the left to dig into a Customer, Vendor, Job or some other tool in your Shelves." Home's Arrange and a kind's gear read **Restore defaults**. The tour's Home step adds that the full list of Customers, Jobs, Vendors and Contacts is there too.
- **Every Work Space starts blank (Coy, 2026-10-22: "every new space will be a blank space whether I click a button or chat with dusty"; "I want the Customer Name and job name to behave like a shelf"; "There is no + button to add a tool, or channel or page or collection"; "Change to: Pin to Rail"; no migration).** New Work Space has no kinds: a name and **Create** make a thread with nobody on it (073), and its drawer opens on **+ Add** (jobs, customers, vendors, Collections, Tools, Threads). **Build it with Dusty** asks him; he answers with a `work_space` plan step {name, shelves, members — jobs, customers, vendors by full id from the CONTEXT, each on a shelf or at the top — tools}, applied on Coy's tap: `apply-plan.ts` makes the thread with their people and fires `sc:space-build`, which the drawer turns into its prefs (tested in `tests/dusty-plan.test.mjs`). **Or pin a job, customer or vendor to the rail ›** is the old picker, its button **Pin to Rail**; a kind's gear reads Pin to Rail / Unpin from Rail. **In a Work Space each job, customer and vendor is a shelf of its own** — its name folds open to its pages (a job's Job shelf and channels, a customer's Customer Account) — and Coy's own shelves group them (by area, by phase): `spaces_col_shelves` {thread id → [{id, name}]} (≤ 20), a member's `shelf` on its pin in `spaces_col_members` (≤ 120 now), + Shelf under the drawer, + on each shelf to add straight onto it, rename, move and delete (its members go to the top) in Edit (`cleanColShelves()` … `membersByShelf()`, `spaces.ts`, tested). Both are layout keys, so they share like the rest (114). A job's, customer's or vendor's drawer gear has **+ Add a page, tool or channel…** on each shelf: its own pages, the Collections and Tools (`p:<page>` rows), and the Channels shelf when it was taken out.
- **Work Space icons drawn by Dusty (migration 117, applied 2026-10-21 on Coy's "apply 117" — checked after: RLS on, one policy, anon no select, members may insert, 0 rows; Coy: "I'm leaning away from the 4 different silhouette space shapes and want to look at generating custom space icons/images for new and existing spaces in the rail" — he picked option 1 of the mockup, `/mnt/project-files/space-icons/dusty-icons-mockup.png`).** `space_icons`: one row per org per rail key (`job:` / `customer:` / `vendor:` / `col:` + id), `svg` (Dusty's drawing as cleaned by `parseDrawing()` → `drawingSvg()`, ≤ 20,000 characters), `about`, `source` dusty | shape; RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`, members read and write, anon nothing — one icon per Space for the whole company. `POST /api/spaces/icons {key}` reads a few plain words about the Space (a job's title, type, description, customer and section names; a customer's type and jobs; a vendor's kind, trades and what they price; a folder's name — never an id, number or street) and makes one tool-less model call (`ICON_SYSTEM`, Claude Sonnet unless `ICON_MODEL`): a 64 × 64 flat icon, 3–12 shapes, no words, no faces or real logos; `iconFromModel()` keeps it only if it is a square-ish drawing with shapes (`src/lib/space-icons.ts`, tested in `tests/space-icons.test.mjs`). The rail (`space-icon.tsx`) reads the icons once per tab and draws any Space on it that has none, two at a time, once per visit, so existing Spaces fill in and a new one gets its own; until then, and when Coy picks it, the silhouette shows. Drawn with React elements from the parsed tree, never as markup; the open one ringed in its colour. **Arrange** (⇅, or a right-click / press and hold on a tile) has ↻ to have Dusty draw it again and ◇ to keep the plain shape (`PATCH {key, source:"shape"}`). Before 117 `GET` says unsupported and nothing is drawn. Rehearsed (rolled back) as Sacco's owner: written and read; another org's row and a Dusty row with no drawing refused; a stranger sees none; anon refused. Not built: the company's logo from its website, an uploaded picture, initials as a choice.
- **The desktop rail folds to its icons (Coy, 2026-10-10: "a button … to reduce the menu panel to just the icons without the word descriptions. And if i hover over the icons it will show the word").** A button at the rail's foot folds it to icons and back (Collapse; 64px since the sidebar below, 56px before); hovering (or tabbing to) an icon shows its name in a tip beside it. Saved on the account (`user_preferences.prefs.rail_icons`, no migration) and set before first paint by `app/layout.tsx` as `html[data-rail="icons"]`; everything measured from the rail reads `--sc-rail-w`. The phone footer is unchanged.
- **The desktop menu is a sidebar, its icons in colour (Claude Design "Rail Icon Colors", Coy 2026-10-10: "redesign the menu on desktop and create an icon color picker panel").** One 40px row a page — the icon and its name (Barlow Condensed 600, 13px, uppercase) — 232px wide; the open page on the rail's active tint with the accent edge drawn as an inset shadow; **Collapse** at the foot folds it to 64px of icons (the fold above). Above it, **Menu settings** (sliders) slides a panel out from behind the rail (Coy, later that day: "a menu settings panel that slides out from the main menu") — the same editor as Settings → Menus (`/dashboard/settings/menus`), to reorder the menu and colour its icons, the rail changing as they are picked; × , Esc or a click on the page closes it (`MenuPanel`, `settings/menus-settings.tsx`; not for an onsite estimator). Each icon may take a colour from the book — Ink (none), Accent, Chalk blue, Green, Teal, Violet, Red, Amber, Unsorted orange — saved as a role, not a hex (`user_preferences.prefs.menu_colors`, `src/lib/v34/menu-colors.ts`, tested; no migration), so it follows every theme; the open page's icon is always ink, the name always the rail's ink. Never saved means the **Suggested** preset; **Mono** and **By kind** are the others. Picked in Settings → Menus: the presets, then nine swatches under each page. On the dark rails (Kraft Yard, Steel, Signal, Kraft (clean)) a colour is mixed 45% toward the rail's light ink so it doesn't sink into the rail (`--sc-icon-lift`; Design didn't draw these). **Admin** (Design's "1b Pulse" icon) sits between Logs and Roadmap for a platform admin only. **The phone keeps the icons' colours too (Coy, 2026-10-10)**: the footer's and the Toolbox's icons take the same book colour, the open page marked by its tinted pill; a page with none stays ink. Their labels are centred in their columns, a two-word one ("Scope Builder") set tighter so it fits on one line, wrapping centred on the narrowest phones (`tabLabel()`, `toolbox.tsx`; Coy, 2026-10-10).
- **Rail icons (Claude Design, 2026-10-10):** Dashboard is four tiles in a masonry grid and Scope Builder three steps joined into a flow, Jobs a clipboard with a tick and Roadmap a signpost with two boards (`NavIcon`, `primitives.tsx`; 1.5 stroke in `currentColor`, so every theme, the rail, the phone footer and the Toolbox follow). The landing page's phone demo footer uses the same Dashboard icon.
- **Initials squares in colour (Coy, 2026-10-06: "a variety of colors and
  we can go from there").** `initialsHue()` (`src/lib/v34/threads.ts`,
  tested) hashes a name into ten colours (`INITIALS_HUES`), so a person keeps
  theirs everywhere `InitialsBox` draws them (Threads, the record pages);
  mixed with the theme's card and ink so dark themes read. No name → the grey
  dashed #; the open one stays hi-viz.
- **Questions pinned on a sheet (Coy, 2026-10-10: "If I want to ask Jeremy a question about these
  sections I measured … where would he go to see my question … tag him and the communications would be
  pulled into the existing thread for that contact … he could open and see the full drawing and zoom
  into the areas of question"; no migration).** Takeoffs' toolbar has **Ask**: a click on the sheet (or
  **Ask about this** on a picked shape) drops a pin and opens a box — pick who (the job's person, their
  customer's people, the job's vendors, the org's members; an onsite estimator is one person with their
  contact), write the question, **Send**. Each person gets it as an **internal note on their own thread**
  (`contact_notes` with the job), "📍 AERIAL 2 · Curbs 2: @Jeremy … Is this 6" or 8"?" and a last line
  `Open on the sheet: /dashboard/scope/<job>/plans?step=takeoff&sheet=<id>&pin=x,y&z=3&m=<shape>`
  (`src/lib/plan-pins.ts`, tested; `GET/POST /api/jobs/[id]/takeoff/comments`, session client, every id
  checked). A teammate is tagged in it, so 081's trigger writes their notification and the activity line's
  "Tagged you on …" opens the link — an onsite estimator, who can't read notes, sees their own pins from
  those notifications. **Also text them the link** sends it through Quo on the same tap (§6 Texts send
  through Quo), a full link to the org's address. The link opens Takeoffs on that sheet, zoomed in, the
  shape picked, scrolled to a ringed pin with its notes. Pins on a sheet are numbered; a tap opens the
  spot's notes, **Open their thread ›**, and a reply goes to the same people. Thread notes show "Open on
  the sheet ›". An answer comes back as their text or call on the thread — not on the pin. Not built: an
  estimator replying on the pin in the app (needs a `plan_comments` table with their access, a
  migration), resolving a question, email, phone push, and pins on the job's sheet viewer;
  Claude Design brief at /mnt/project-files/takeoff-comments/claude-design-brief.md.
- **Takeoffs, and back to the customer (Coy, 2026-10-07: "let's rename this tab page instead of
  plan room. Let's call it takeoffs. I'm going to create a dedicated plan room page for general
  contractors to upload plans and see quotes").** Every label that said Plan room / Plan Room says
  **Takeoffs** (the switch, links, the estimator's page name, Dusty's prompts); the address stays
  `/dashboard/scope/[jobId]/plans` and the code keeps its names. On a phone its **Files · Takeoff**
  hang under the Takeoffs button (`subnav` on `JobHeaderRow` / `JobTabs`; since 2026-10-09 the step reads
  **Measurements**, and its line drops from the button's centre and splits to each tab — Coy), not on a row of their
  own. A job's **‹** goes to its customer's page on the **Jobs** tab
  (`/dashboard/customers/[id]?tab=jobs`; "‹ Customer" on a desktop) — the Jobs board when it has
  no customer, or for an onsite estimator.
  **The job switcher (Coy, 2026-10-08: "Jobtread has a job switcher panel that slides in on the
  right side … accessed by clicking on the job at the top. The job name needs to look
  clickable").** The job's name at the top of every job view is a button with a ▾ (underlined on
  hover); on the Budget, where the name is typed in place, the ▾ beside it. It opens a panel on the
  right: every job not archived, newest first, with its customer, city and stage, a search, the
  open job marked; a pick opens the same view on that job (`job-switcher.tsx`, `GET /api/jobs` —
  RLS decides, so an estimator sees theirs).
- **One job, three views (Coy, 2026-10-02: "no way … to get back from the
  proposal page" and "one job budget page, and I can toggle between that
  budget and the proposal").** **Plan room · Budget · Proposal** — in the
  order of the work (Coy, 2026-10-03: "Plan Room, Budget, Proposal. That is
  the work flow"); the same
  segmented switch (`job-tabs.tsx`) on the job page, the Plan Room header
  and the proposal toolbar, so each is one tap from the others. The
  proposal's slide-over **Job panel is gone**: the money moved to the
  Budget (margin and O.H. already there; **cost by type** as one bar and the
  **profit split with a sub** (since 2026-10-10 a **Commission cost** — 0 ·
  20 · 30 · 40 · 50%, 0 by default, the partner's share of the profit: "partner
  $X · you keep $Y"; Coy: the 20 / 80 split gave the sub 80%. Kept as
  `display.commission_pct` on the draft — `split`'s check allows only the old
  three, so no migration; an old 20_80 / 30_70 reads as 20% / 30%,
  `src/lib/commission.ts`, tested; `PATCH …/proposal {commission}`; the
  customer's copy never carries it) — saved on the proposal draft, never on the
  document, never changing the price — in the budget footer,
  `PriceSummary`), and what shapes the document stays on the proposal as a
  **Proposal settings** strip (project type, option, warranty, contract;
  folded on a phone, open on a desktop). The proposal's sub-line says the
  total and "rounded up to …", or why there is no price.
  **On a phone the four views are plain tabs across the full width** (Coy,
  2026-10-09: "not buttons but just tabs") — the open one in ink with an
  accent underline (`.sc-job-tabs` in `v34-shell.css`); a desktop keeps the
  segmented switch.
- **Each section's plan sheets (Coy, 2026-10-02: "I need to verify the
  scopes on the sheets the scopes were taken from … expand and view each
  sheet … on desktop, a panel to the left or right of the budget").** Under
  a section's bid line, **Check it on A2.1 · A2.2** — the sheets its line
  names, linked only when a sheet by that exact number was read off the
  plans (`sheetsFor()`, `src/lib/section-sheets.ts`, tested: A2.1 is never
  A2.10). A tap opens the **sheet viewer** (`sheet-viewer.tsx`): on a desktop
  a panel over the right ~58% of the page — the page itself narrows to the
  room left of it (`html[data-sheets]`), so the budget stays in view, and
  with Dusty docked it sits between the page and him — with the sheets down
  its side: this section's, the other sections', then the rest of the set
  — each sheet once, in the first group that names it (several sections
  share a sheet; the first build listed them over and over);
  on a phone the whole screen with the sheets as chips. Fit · − · + (ctrl +
  wheel), drag to pan, ‹ › and the arrow keys. **Measure in the Plan Room ›**
  opens Takeoff on that sheet (`?step=takeoff&sheet=`) — measuring stays
  there, with its scale rule. The job header's **Plan sheets (n)** opens the
  same panel on every section's sheets. Attaching a sheet a bid line didn't
  name needs a place to keep it (a column on `job_sections`) — not built.
  **The takeoffs show on the sheet (Coy, 2026-10-10: "why doesn't it show me the drawings I made
  on the plans?").** Every shape drawn on the open sheet in Takeoffs is drawn over it, each in its
  own colour (`shapeColor()`), in an SVG laid on the page in page points; **✓ Takeoffs** in the
  viewer's bar hides them. **And onto a section as a picture (same day: "pull that photo into the
  section with the lines I've drawn").** A section's Photos → **Copy** opens with **Takeoffs · the
  sheet with your markups**: each sheet or aerial with shapes on it (this area's first, aerials
  before plan pages; a preview of the part that holds them, on a phone only for aerials — a scanned
  page is decoded whole to draw any of it) and **+ Add**, which draws that part of the sheet with
  every shape in its colour into a JPEG (`sheetMarkup()` / `mapPicture()`, `area-visuals.tsx`) and
  saves it as a photo on the section (`files`, category photo, "Takeoffs on AERIAL · Oct 10.jpg") —
  under the area here and on the proposal like any photo. It is a picture of that day's lines;
  re-measuring doesn't redraw it. The job page's **+ Add a section** is the width of its words on a
  desktop.
  **Where on the sheet (Coy, 2026-10-09: "verify … where the sections are on the plan set sheets
  that the job scope was pulled from").** A section's **From the plans** box sits at the top of its
  card — its sheet buttons and the bid's line — and **See where on the sheet ›** opens the viewer
  with that line boxed wherever it is written on the sheet (`findOnSheet()` /
  `quotedLine()`, `src/lib/find-on-sheet.ts`, tested: the sheet's own text runs from pdf.js,
  `pageText()`; runs within a few lines of each other are one label; a place needs half the
  line's words; a drawing that labels it several times gets a box at each). **Zoom to it** /
  **Next** steps through them; a line not in the sheet's text says so (drawn, scanned or on another
  sheet). Scanned sheets' OCR words aren't searched yet. The old "Check it on" row under the
  photos is gone (Browse the set stays when no sheet matched).
- **Dusty reads the plans' words (2026-10-02).** Asked to check each scope on
  its sheet, he answered that the plan files were "not read" — he had only
  their names. His job context now carries **What the plans and specs say**:
  the bid reader's text of every page that mentions concrete, labelled
  `[file · p.N · A2.1 Title]` (drawings first, ~40k characters), with the
  instruction to quote the sheet and point out what disagrees, and to send
  Coy to the sheet under the section. What is drawn but not written isn't
  in it, and he says so.
- **Call notes → Dusty's quote (Coy, 2026-10-03: "make notes on a live
  call so that Dusty can finish putting the quote together from aerial
  imagery afterwards").** **Call notes** on the person page and the customer
  page opens a pad (`call-notes-pad.tsx`, drawn at the page's top level) that
  saves as Coy types: the first words make a `contact_notes` row
  (`POST /api/people/[id]/notes`), each pause updates it
  (`PATCH /api/notes/[id]`), and a copy stays on the device until the server
  has it. **Quo sends a call only after hang-up** (no ringing event reaches
  us — measured 2026-10-03), so the note waits on the person; once the call
  arrives it is attached to the call it was written during —
  `callForNote()` (`src/lib/call-notes.ts`, tested): the call's own window
  from Quo's `call.completed` (`createdAt` → `completedAt`, raw log §2a),
  from 2 minutes before it rang to 15 minutes after hang-up, a note during a
  call beating the tail of the one before. A note in no window stays a plain
  note; a call with no window takes none. Attaching is done by the reads that
  show notes (the pad, the person and customer timelines, Dusty's job
  context — `settleCallNotes()`, session client, notes from the last three
  days with no call yet, no migration). The pad then says "On the 3:54 PM
  call" with **Draft the quote**, also on every call card:
  `POST /api/messages/[id]/quote` finds the call's job, else the caller's or
  their customer's newest open job — or, on Coy's yes, starts one — opens
  it, and puts the question in Dusty's box for Coy to send. Dusty's job
  context carries **Calls about this job** (the newest four with summary,
  next steps and Coy's notes on each; transcripts of the newest two, marked
  machine-heard, Coy's notes winning) beside the Plan Room's measured
  aerial, and he answers with a budget plan (§3) Coy applies. A live
  call shows on its thread since 2026-10-06 (Incoming alerts, below).
- **Texted photos onto the job (Coy, 2026-10-03: "she texted pictures. I
  would like to be able to pull those pictures into the plan room so that I
  can compare them to the aerial … into the budget and plan room and
  proposal").** A texted photo stays in `message_media` (the text is never
  changed); **+ Job** copies it into the job's files —
  `/api/jobs/[id]/texted-photos` (GET the photos texted by the job's person
  and their customer's other people, each with `file_id` once on the job;
  POST `{media_ids}` or `{all:true}`), session client, only those people's
  inbound photos. The copy is a `job_files` row (`source='text'`,
  `message_id`, `source_url='message_media:<id>'` so it is copied once,
  category photo, **In plan room**, Dusty reads it), named "Texted by
  Johnette Wilson · Oct 2 3.41 PM.jpg" (`src/lib/texted-photos.ts`, tested).
  A photo not yet stored is first copied in from Quo (`captureMedia()`,
  `src/lib/message-media-server.ts`, shared with the thread's media route).
  As a job file it can be set to an area (shows under that section's
  Photos) and picked as the proposal's cover. **Where:** the job page's
  Files → From texts (**+ Job** on each, **Add all N to the job**, "On the
  job ✓"), and Takeoffs → Files → **Photos**: the ones texted but not yet on
  the job, "Texted by …", with **+ Job** / **Bring all N in**, above the
  job's photos (`texted-photos.tsx`; the Photos side panel and its button
  are gone since 2026-10-08 — Coy: "it's attached to the customer"). Only PDFs become sheets; a photo
  is for looking at beside the aerial, not for measuring.
- **A section's scope of work (Coy, 2026-10-10: "Where is the scope of work? I need to be able to see it
  in the budget as a bulleted line items and i need to be able to edit it in the draft proposal documents as
  well which would update the budget SOW").** Each section card on the budget has **Scope of work**, a bullet
  a line (Enter starts the next, Backspace on an empty one removes it, saved when a bullet is left —
  `ScopeBullets`, `scope-of-work.tsx`), and the draft proposal shows the same bullets under each area in the
  Scope's list, edited there the same way (`edits.scope` → `PATCH …/sections/[sid] {prep_notes}`), so the two
  never disagree. Kept in `job_sections.prep_notes`, no migration: Prep left the card on 2026-10-03 and no
  section held anything there (0 of 45, measured 2026-10-10); `sowLines()` / `sowText()`
  (`src/lib/scope-of-work.ts`, tested) strip typed bullets and blanks, 40 lines of 300 characters at most
  (the section PATCH now keeps 8,000 characters of it, 2,000 of the Notes). A final freezes it with its
  sections; the customer's link carries it as each area's `scope` (`publicSnapshot()`), while the Notes stay
  internal. Dusty reads it as the section's "scope of work". The proposal's scope introduction is unchanged. **Long bullets wrap and a line can be removed (Coy, 2026-10-13: "the scope of work … doesn't fit on the width of the PDF. It's cut off and I don't have the ability to remove lines").** Each bullet is a box that grows to its words, the PDF prints the words themselves (a `sc-print-only` copy), and × beside a bullet removes it, on the budget and the draft proposal alike. Dusty's scope is shorter: 4 to 8 bullets of 10 words or fewer, unknowns said after the list, not as bullets (`sowQuestion()`). **Dusty writes it (same evening, Coy: "the AI emblem so that Dusty can write the scope of work"):** the
  emblem beside Scope of work on the budget's section card opens Dusty and asks him for that section's
  bullets (its size, notes, items and the plans; no prices); **Use this** under his answer puts its list
  items in as the bullets (`sowFromAnswer()`, tested), Coy's tap. **The tap says what it did (2026-10-11, Coy: "it doesn't do anything on mobile")** — it had saved (the Walker High dugout slabs' five bullets were on the section), but the phone's chat covers the page, so nothing showed. Now the button reads ✓ Used and a line under it says "✓ Saved as …'s scope of work" (on a phone "Close the chat to see it"), or, when the answer held no bullets (or terms) to use, says so in red; a page's `apply` returns false for that (`DustyUseTarget`, `dusty-live.ts`).
- **The customer picks add-ons (Coy, 2026-10-10: "a check box … so the customer can select it to add it
  to the total").** On the customer's link, the Preview and a final, each optional add-on (or a group that
  is all add-ons) has a tick box; ticked ones show as "Add-ons you picked" and a **Total with your
  add-ons** under the Total. **Signed with them (migration 099, applied 2026-10-10 on Coy's "apply everything" — checked after: both columns and their check, the guard covers them, anon may run sc_sign_proposal and sc_accept_proposal and still has no grant on documents, no link signed yet):** on the
  link the page holds the ticks (`DocVM.addonPick`), the sign bar and sheet show the total with them, and
  `POST /api/p/[token]` sends their ids to `sc_sign_proposal()` — 097's door with `p_addons`, each id checked
  against the link's own copy (an add-on item, else refused). `documents.signed_addons` ({id, name, sell} as
  the customer saw them) and `signed_addons_total` are kept beside the signature, `signed_total` is the base
  plus them, the note says "with add-ons: Gravel apron ($450.00)", and the contract on the Won card is the
  signed total (`contractFor()`, `SignedLink.total`). Once signed the ticks stay as signed, locked ("Total
  signed for"). `sc_accept_proposal()` keeps its arguments and calls the new one with none; the guards cover
  the new columns. Before 099 a signature with add-ons ticked is refused in words (untick them, or reply to
  the contractor); one without goes through 097 as before. Rehearsed (rolled back) on Wilson Driveway's P-3
  with a made-up add-on: an unknown id refused; signed $10,100 = $9,650 + $450, the add-on on the row and in
  the note, the job won; the old door still signs; a member forging `signed_addons` refused.
- **Photos on the proposal (Coy, 2026-10-10).** An area's thumbnails are one row (scrolling sideways), the
  section's photos also sit small in its Pricing header (up to five, then +N), and a tap opens them full
  screen in a panel that slides up over the page — ‹ ›, a swipe, Esc or × — on the customer's link too,
  never a new window (`Thumbs` / `PhotoViewer`, `proposal/document.tsx`).
  **The same day:** a section's Photos row reads **Add Images to this Section** and carries **+ Clip** beside
  Camera; Details from the plans shows only once something is clipped. The job's name at the top of every job
  view is set in the face of the wordmark's "crete" (Inter 300) — Coy: "a thinner font".
- **Section Notes replace Prep and Access (Coy, 2026-10-03, two Vercel
  notes: "Prep can be removed from the sections" and "Access can be removed
  … replaced by 'notes'. The notes can be typed or dictated verbally").**
  Every section has one **Notes** box — typed, or **Say it** (records with
  the chat's recorder, Groq writes it down through `/api/dusty/transcribe`,
  the words are added to the box). Kept in `job_sections.access_notes`, no
  migration: measured that day, no section had anything in `prep_notes` or
  `access_notes`. Section dictation fills Notes with whatever was said that
  isn't a field (`sanitizeDictation()` no longer writes `prep_notes`), and
  Dusty reads them as the section's notes.
- **Job header (2026-09-28)** — four tappable chips, each opening its list:
  **Status** (the seven stages, with W.I.P.'s won / scheduled / pouring),
  **Type**, **Source** and **Vendors (n)** (crew on the job, add by subtype)
  — `src/components/v34/job-chips.tsx`.
- **Receipts are camera-first, always.** Photograph, never type. Dusty reads
  vendor, amount, date and clarifies conversationally. Invoices and payments
  stay quick structured forms.
- **The composer never appears to send.** (2026-09-27; texts send since
  2026-10-05, below) Email has no send path. The composer (thread screen, and compact in the row expand)
  writes, keeps a per-device draft and copies it for pasting into Quo; Send is
  shown switched off with its reason. **The box is empty** — no placeholder
  words (Coy, 2026-10-05); it is labelled for screen readers only. **Notes save** (2026-09-28, above). The
  **mic dictates** into the box with the browser's own speech recognition —
  Chrome sends the audio to Google to transcribe; nothing of ours stores it;
  off, with the reason, where the browser has none. The composer's Call and +
  attach are gone (Coy: the Call is at the top; attach did nothing).
  **+ and Send are back in the box (Coy, 2026-10-09: "add attachments like
  any other chat" and "I need a send button").** A + inside the text box on
  the left picks photos or files; each is saved on the person's Files at once
  (`POST /api/files {contact_id}`), shown as a chip with ×. The send arrow is
  always at the far right, off until there is something to send. Quo's API
  sends words only (its docs list SMS; media only on reads), so a text with
  files goes out through the phone's share sheet (`navigator.share` with the
  files and the words) — Coy picks Quo there, and the text comes back through
  its webhook like any other; with no share sheet it says to send them from
  the Quo app. The words alone still send through Quo from here. **The box grows with its words (Coy, 2026-10-13: "does not allow me to view everything I've typed out")** — typed, dictated or filled in (a proposal text from the Preview), up to about eight lines or 40% of the screen, then it scrolls inside (`composer.tsx`); it was one line high with a 120px cap. **Laid out like Dusty's box (Coy, same day: "more like the design of the dusty text box")**: one card, the words two lines to start with no border of their own, under them + (attach) on the left and the mic and a round send (Save for a note, the email composer for email) on the right; Enter is a new line and Ctrl (⌘) + Enter sends, as Dusty's does ("Ctrl + Enter to send" beside it on a desktop). An earlier composer appended messages locally and faked a Dusty reply
  a second later; that must not come back. Email's Send is still off.
- **Proposal links by "/" and from the proposal page (Coy, 2026-10-07: "I
  should be able to send the proposal from this page … the option to copy the
  link … pull that into a channel into threads with a / command").** The
  proposal toolbar's button is **Preview & send** (the Preview's Send text,
  Email it and Copy link), and **Copy link** beside it copies the live link of
  a final version (a draft opens the Preview, which freezes it first). In a
  thread's text box, **/** (at the start or after a space) lists the person's
  and their customer's jobs whose newest proposal is final (`GET
  /api/proposals?contact=`); a pick swaps the "/word" for its link, made if
  none is live (`proposalLink()`), and Send marks that link sent by text.
- **Chat bubbles (Coy, 2026-10-07: "remove this line … Keep the timestamp but
  move it to the right. Remove the word 'OUT' and 'IN'")** — a text's bubble
  carries its words, its photos and its time, right-aligned; no IN / OUT and
  no "via" line. Call cards keep "● via Quo".
- **Sending proposals — Threads, the Preview and Dusty (Coy, 2026-10-05: "a
  clean way to actually send proposals via text and email manually and for
  dusty to send them. And I want him to watch how i do it and learn and
  improve").** One road, `sendProposal()` (`src/lib/v34/send-proposal.ts`),
  always Coy's tap: the link (`POST /api/jobs/[id]/proposal/link {auto:true}`
  — the live one for the newest final, else a new one; a draft newer than the
  last final is frozen first, as Preview does; with no browser the cover is
  the proposal's own, each area's photos are its job photos, and no map
  pictures), the words with `{link}` / `{first}` / `{title}` filled
  (`src/lib/proposal-send.ts`, tested), a text through Quo (`POST /api/texts`,
  §6 Texts) or an email in Coy's own mail app (mailto: — **ScopeCrete has no
  outbound email yet**), then the link marked sent. **Threads:** (the
  composer's Proposal button and its idle "Drafts stay on this device" line
  are gone since 2026-10-09 — Coy asks Dusty, whose Proposals to send now
  lists the jobs of every person on the customer, filed on it or not; "/" in
  the text box still puts a link in. `ProposalSendSheet` is kept, unused.)
  Before that the composer's **Proposal** opened a sheet — the person's and their customer's
  jobs with a proposal (`GET /api/proposals?contact=`), Text · Email, the
  words, **Preview** (the customer's page `/p/<token>` in a sheet; Coy signed
  in isn't counted as a view), Send. A text with a proposal link shows
  "Proposal P-2 · opened 3 times · Preview" under it (`?token=`). **The
  Preview's** Text it now sends through Quo with words Coy can change ("Or
  open it in your Messages app" keeps the old sms:). **Dusty:** every chat
  carries **The business** (orgs.name — he had called it "Baton Rouge
  Concreters", the lead-gen relay's name, also in his text example) and
  **Proposals to send** — each job's proposal, its full job id, version,
  draft/final, total, link URL, sent and opened — and the plan step
  `send_proposal` {job, via text | email, body with {link}, subject, contact?};
  the card shows the words in a box Coy can change before Send.
  **Learning:** each send is kept in Coy's own `user_preferences.prefs.proposal_sends`
  (newest 12; his words as a pattern, and Dusty's draft beside them when
  Coy changed it — `POST /api/proposal-sends`, no migration). The next send
  starts from his last words for that channel, and Dusty is shown them, where
  Coy changed his draft, and the last texts with a /p/ link in them (sent
  from the Quo app too), and told to write that way.
  **A name never carries over (Coy, 2026-10-06: Johnette Wilson's send sheet
  offered "Hi Gulfline, …").** The Preview greeted by the customer's name
  and the send kept only the person's, so "Gulfline" was saved as his
  words. Now every name the words could use (the person's, the customer's)
  becomes {first}, and whoever the words open by greeting ("Hi …", "Hey Ms
  …", "Good morning …") is the slot whatever their name (`greetingSlot()`,
  tested) — applied to saved patterns as they are used, so the old one
  heals. Dusty is shown the patterns without the job they were for, and
  texts with a link as patterns too.
  **The Preview greets the person and shows the link (Coy, 2026-10-10: "Hi Gulfline, … {link}").**
  The Preview's words greet the job's person (Jeremy), not the customer's name, and carry the live
  link itself once one exists — {link} only before it is made. **Copy link** says Copied only when
  the clipboard took it, else shows the link to copy by hand: a failed copy had left Jeremy's invite
  link on the clipboard. **An invite is remembered on the device only for the person it is for**
  (signed out, or signed in with its email) and is forgotten once the dashboard finds an org — an
  owner opening a teammate's invite to check it no longer gets sent back to it later.
- **Texts send through Quo (Coy, 2026-10-05: "send text messages through the
  app through quo manually or through dusty"; decision card: "Build both").**
  10DLC was not in fact waiting: 129 texts Coy sent from the Quo website line
  since 2026-08-27 came back from Quo `delivered`, so that line is carrier
  registered (inferred from the log; Coy confirms in Quo → US Carrier
  Registration). Quo's API also needs **prepaid API credits** ($0.01 a
  segment, Quo → Plans & Billing) and `QUO_API_KEY` (in Vercel). The
  composer's **Send** (text channel; Copy stays) posts to `POST /api/texts`
  {contact_id, body, to?, line_id?} — session client, org from the session:
  `to` must be one of the person's own numbers (`contacts.phone` /
  `contact_phones`), never one of the org's lines (default: the number they
  last used); the line must be one of the org's **Quo** lines (default: the
  Quo line they last reached, else the primary — `pickLine()`,
  `src/lib/texting.ts`, tested); then `sendQuoText()` (`src/lib/quo.ts`,
  `POST https://api.quo.com/v1/messages`, `QUO_API_BASE` overrides the host),
  never retried. Nothing is shown as sent until Quo takes it, and the text
  appears in the thread only when Quo's webhook brings it back
  (`message.delivered` → `quo_events` → `messages`, §2a) — the app writes no
  message itself. After a send (the composer or Dusty's card) the open
  thread checks again at 3, 8 and 16 s (`expectMessage()`, `live-threads.ts`)
  rather than waiting for the 15 s poll. Quo's refusals are put in words (`quoError()`: key, credits,
  registration). The hint shows the segment count. **Dusty** proposes a text
  only when Coy asks: plan step `text` {contact — a full id from his context,
  body, to only when Coy names a number} (`src/lib/dusty/plan.ts`, tested);
  the card shows the words and who they go to, and its button reads **Send**;
  Coy's tap sends it through the same route (`applyRecords()`). §10's rule
  holds: every text is Coy's tap. Testing sends only to a phone of Coy's that
  is not one of the org's lines, never a customer.

- **The site camera and Sites (migration 113, applied 2026-10-21 on Coy's "apply 113" — checked after: RLS on `sites` with one member policy, authenticated may select, insert and update and anon nothing, both guard triggers, the eight `files` columns, the source check takes 'capture', the five indexes, all 132 files `storage_provider='supabase'`; Coy: "our own little company
  cam app for Scopecrete … take pictures on site really quickly and then pull those projects or jobs into
  Scopecrete", "zoom into the address, and it automatically creates a project … I can add a name or not … and
  go ahead and snap pictures and take video", and "what about the large data required for photos and videos").**
  The full design is /mnt/project-files/photo-capture/design.md. **Camera** (`/dashboard/camera`, after Map on
  the rail, in the Toolbox on a phone; `camera-page.tsx`) is part of the PWA, not a native app: a full-screen
  viewfinder (`getUserMedia`, the back camera; the phone's own camera when refused), Photo · Video (60 s, 720p),
  Before · During · After · Issue and a note for the next shot. **Where shots go** is the strip at the top: the
  job or site you stand at, from the phone's GPS against open jobs' points (090, else their customer's) and
  sites — within 150 m, or twice a poor fix, at most 500 m; picked when one is clearly nearest, else offered
  (`matchPlace()`, `src/lib/capture.ts`, tested in `tests/capture.test.mjs`); a job's section can be picked;
  none is the **Photo inbox**. `?job=` / `?site=` open on one. **A site** (`sites`) is a pin and its address
  (+ New site here: the map on satellite with a centre pin, the address read back by Mapbox, a name optional),
  never a job and never counted — no money without a job (§2). **Make it a job** opens the New job form with
  the site (`/dashboard/scope?new=1&site=`); `POST /api/jobs {site_id}` takes its address, point and name,
  sets `sites.job_id` and moves its photos onto the job; later shots there go to the job. **The queue**
  (`src/lib/v34/capture-queue.ts`): each shot is kept in IndexedDB the moment it is taken (a photo shrunk in
  the browser to 2,048 px JPEG, ~400 KB, and a 400 px thumbnail; the browser re-encodes, so no EXIF location
  rides on a picture), then sent while the app is open — `POST /api/capture/files` (the shot's own id,
  `files.client_id`, makes a retry find its row), the file and thumbnail straight to `job-files` storage,
  `/api/files/[id]/done` — retried with backoff, on coming back online and on opening the app; iOS has no
  background sync, so a closed app sends nothing until next time. The full file leaves the phone only once the
  server has it. Shots are `files` rows `source='capture'`, category photo, with `taken_at`, `lat` / `lng`,
  `gps_accuracy_m`, `thumb_path`, `site_id` and `storage_provider`; **Dusty doesn't read them by himself**
  (`read_state='skipped'`, "Dusty reads it when asked") — he is asked. **Storage:** Supabase now (Pro: 100 GB,
  then ~$0.021/GB and ~$0.09/GB egress); `storage_provider` lets photos and video move to Cloudflare R2 (no
  egress fees) when video makes egress the cost. APIs `/api/capture/files` (GET by job, site, inbox or the last
  day; POST), `/api/capture/nearby`, `/api/sites[/id]` — session client, org from the session, every id
  checked. **Before 113 was applied** the page worked and the shots wait on the phone ("held", tried every five
  minutes): the routes answer `waiting` and `files`' read columns are unchanged (the capture routes read their
  own, `SHOT_COLUMNS`). Onsite estimators read no sites (RLS by `get_user_org_ids()`), and the page is not
  theirs — **except the Camera: an estimator with Photos & files has it since 2026-10-23** (Coy:
  Jeremy, Gulfline's owner and Sacco's estimator, taking photos on Sacco's jobs; no migration).
  It is on their menu (`menuPages()`, `redirectFor()`); shots go only onto one of their jobs — no
  + New site, no Photo inbox, a shot with no job asks for one — under 081's `files_field` and
  storage policies; an upload link falls back from upsert to a plain one, and a refused thumbnail
  never holds a shot back. That is how photos cross companies: one login in both, switched in
  Settings. Not built yet (Roadmap cards): the Photo inbox and job galleries on their own pages, markup,
  before/after pairs, a customer timeline, moving storage to R2.
- **A camera link for a customer (migration 128, written 2026-10-24, NOT applied — rehearsed on the live data and rolled back; Coy:
  "a clean way to send a link to the camera that would be linked to a job so that a customer can just click the link in a text
  message and open a camera on their phone and capture images and video just like in the sections on the budget? And any images
  and video would be uploaded to the files for that job/contact").** One private link, `scopecrete.ai/cam/<token>` (no login,
  noindex, like /p/, /b/ and /c/), for a job and the person it is for — or a person alone — and optionally one of the job's
  sections; it lasts 30 days unless turned off. It opens the Camera page's viewfinder with nothing to pick (`camera-link-page.tsx`):
  "Photos for Sacco Enterprises · <job> · <section>", Hi <first name>, Photo · Video (60 s), ✎ Note, and **Phone camera** for the
  phone's own camera or library. Every shot is kept on the phone first, in the same IndexedDB queue as the Camera page
  (`capture-queue.ts`; a shot carries `link`), sent to storage **as anon** (the anon key as bearer) into the link's own folder
  `job-files/{org}/camera/{link}/<shot id>.<ext>` with its thumbnail, then recorded by `POST /api/cam/<token>` →
  `sc_camera_shot()`. **Migration 128:** `camera_links` (job, section, person, a 32-character random token, expires, revoked,
  views, shots; members read, make and may set only `revoked_at`; a guard keeps the job, section and person on the link's org;
  anon no grant) and three SECURITY DEFINER doors, the only things anon may run: `sc_camera_link(token)` (what the page shows, the
  opening counted unless the reader is in the org), `sc_camera_upload_ok(name)` behind storage policy `camera_link_uploads` (an
  insert only under a live link's folder, 400 objects a day) and `sc_camera_shot()` — the object must be there, a photo (JPEG, PNG,
  WebP) or a video (MP4, WebM, MOV), and the phone's own id for the shot makes a retry the same file. It is a `files` row on the
  job, section, person and their customer, source `capture`, category photo, named "Photo from Jeremy · Oct 24 2.31 PM.jpg", its
  note "From Jeremy's camera link — <their note>"; Dusty reads the photos (`read_state='waiting'`). The first shot of a visit
  (none for two hours) leaves an internal note on the person's thread: "📷 Jeremy is sending photos and video from the camera link
  for <job>. They land in Files as they arrive." **Coy's side:** a **Camera link** card on the job's Summary (pick who, from the
  job's contacts, and the whole job or a section) and on a contact card (them alone) — **Text a camera link** makes the link (the
  live one for that job, person and section is reused) and opens their thread with the words in the text box
  (`cameraMessage()`, `src/lib/camera-link.ts`, tested), Coy taps Send (§10); each live link shows who it is for, "Opened 2 times ·
  5 shots sent", days left, Text it · Copy link · See them in Files · Open ↗ · Turn off. APIs `/api/camera-links` (GET `?job=` /
  `?contact=`, POST) and `/api/camera-links/[id]` (PATCH `{revoke}`) — session client, org from the session, every id checked;
  `/api/cam/[token]` with the request's own client, no service role. Before 128 the card says it waits for it. Rehearsed (rolled
  back) on Sacco's newest job as anon: the page's facts and one view counted, an upload into the link's folder allowed and into
  another refused, the table refused, a shot recorded as "Photo from Jeremy · … .jpg" with one note, the same shot again the same
  file, a path outside the folder refused. Not built: GPS on a customer's shots, and a section's own Photos row offering a link.
- **Appointments (migration 110, applied 2026-10-13 on Coy's "apply 110" — checked after: RLS on, one member policy, the guard trigger, authenticated may select, insert and update and anon nothing, 0 rows; Coy: "I need a way for Dusty
  to keep track of my schedule for each job and customer contact lead … add that to my calendar
  through Scopecrete and to send confirmation text messages through Scopecrete via AI").**
  `appointments`: one row per appointment on a job and/or a person (or a customer) — kind (site
  visit · consultation · pour · meeting · other), title, `starts_at` / `ends_at` (UTC, entered and
  shown in Central), where, a note, status scheduled → confirmed → done, or canceled with its
  reason; `source` coy | dusty. Never deleted. RLS by `get_user_org_ids()` (members insert, read,
  update; anon nothing), a guard keeping the job, person and customer on the row's org. Pure rules
  in `src/lib/appointments.ts` (Central ↔ UTC across DST, the words, the confirmation text, the
  .ics, the Google link; tested in `tests/appointments.test.mjs`); reads in
  `appointments-server.ts`. APIs `/api/appointments` (GET by job / contact / customer / upcoming,
  POST — the person, customer, address and title filled from the job), `/api/appointments/[id]`
  (PATCH status, reschedule — a move goes back to scheduled — and words), `…/[id]/ics` — session
  client, org from the session. **Where:** an **Appointments** card on the job's Summary (under
  What's next) and on the contact card (above Notes): upcoming first, past and canceled behind a
  toggle, **+ Schedule** (kind, day, from / to, where, note). Each one: **Add to calendar ▾** —
  Google Calendar (a prefilled event link) or Phone or Outlook (an .ics with a reminder an hour
  before) — **nothing is synced back**: a two-way Google sync would need Coy's Google sign-in with
  calendar access, a stored token and a sync worker; an always-current calendar feed his phone
  subscribes to is the smaller next step. **Confirm by text** (the emblem) asks Dusty for a text
  step; **Write it in their thread** puts the words in the thread's text box (`draftKey`); either
  way Coy taps Send and it goes through Quo (§6 Texts, §10). **They confirmed**, Change, Done,
  Cancel, Put it back on. **Dusty:** his context carries **Appointments** (today's date, a week back
  to 60 days ahead, each by id); plan steps `appointment` {job (full id or job1) and/or contact,
  kind, title, day, start, end — only times Coy said, the day worked out from today} and
  `update_appointment` {id, status or a new day and time, reason}, applied on Coy's tap through
  the same APIs. Had 110 been missing, the card would say it waits for it.
- **Incoming alerts and live calls (migration 086, applied 2026-10-06 on
  Coy's "apply 086"; quo-intake v11, Coy
  2026-10-06: "There needs to be a way for me to be notified inside the
  scopecrete app for incoming emails, text messages and phone calls. And if
  there is an active phone call I would like that thread channel to show it's
  active.").** Dylan's forwarded bid that morning had been filed and made its
  job within two seconds; nothing told Coy it had come.
  - **The bell** in the header (not for an onsite estimator): the count of
    texts, calls and emails not yet seen, and a list of the last three days,
    newest first, each opening its thread (an email opens in Files). Seen when
    the list is opened (`sc:alerts-seen`, per device). Spam Coy or Dusty filed
    raises nothing; quarantined email neither.
  - **Pop-ups**: each one that lands while the app is open shows top right
    (across the top on a phone) for 8 s, a tap opens it — never for the thread
    already open. With the browser's permission ("Also alert me when
    ScopeCrete is in the background" in the list) a desktop browser shows a
    system notification too. A phone in the background needs Web Push and a
    service worker (§8: none yet) — not built.
  - **A live call**: Quo's `call.ringing` (and `call.answered`, if it ever
    sends one) are kept in **`live_calls`** — one row per call, ended by its
    `call.completed`, lost after two hours. Not in `quo_events`: every row there
    is projected into `messages` (§2a), and the call's message still comes from
    `call.completed` alone. Written only by quo-intake; members read under
    `get_user_org_ids()`; anon nothing. While live: a green card that stays
    ("Ringing", then "On a call · 3:07 · via Main" — Quo doesn't say when a
    call is answered, so after a minute of ringing it is taken as answered),
    the bell goes green, the Threads row shows it in place of its age, and the
    open thread marks that number's channel tab and puts a green strip over
    the conversation.
  - **One watcher per tab** (`src/lib/v34/alerts-live.ts`): Realtime on inbound
    `messages`, `emails` and `live_calls` (086 adds the last two to
    `supabase_realtime`) as a nudge, and `/api/alerts?since=` every 30 s while
    the tab is visible — at most 30 messages, 15 emails and the open live
    calls newer than the last read. `src/lib/alerts.ts` shapes them (tested,
    `tests/alerts.test.mjs`); `incoming-alerts.tsx` draws the bell, the
    pop-ups and the live badge.
  - **086 applied 2026-10-06**; checked after: RLS on `live_calls`, one
    select policy, authenticated may only select, anon nothing, and
    `live_calls` and `emails` in `supabase_realtime` beside `messages`.
  - **quo-intake v11 deployed 2026-10-06 on Coy's "Deploy quo-intake"**
    (version 70, built on the deployed v69 read back first; the deployed copy's
    long history comments are shortened, the code is the repo's). Checked
    after: it boots, an unsigned call.ringing gets 401 and stores nothing.
  - **What Coy turns on:** in Quo's webhook settings tick **call.ringing** (no ring has ever reached
    quo_events — Quo wasn't sending it). Until then the bell and pop-ups work
    for texts, calls (when they end) and emails; live calls don't show.
- **Feedback and the Roadmap (migration 084, applied 2026-10-05 on Coy's
  "Apply 084").** Coy: *"I need to have a place for
  internal users and other orgs to give feedback on the app and we need a
  pipeline page … where people can see … what features are recommended and
  then people can vote on those recommended features and comment on them and
  see what's being worked on similar to how JobTread does it I like their
  Roadmap page."* **Platform data, not an org's** — every org sees the same
  board, so `roadmap_items`, `roadmap_votes`, `roadmap_comments` are NOT
  under `get_user_org_ids()`: RLS on, no policies, no grants; everything goes
  through SECURITY DEFINER `sc_roadmap_*` functions (execute for
  authenticated only) that check the caller. **`platform_admins`** is new —
  the people who run ScopeCrete itself, not an org's owner; seeded with Coy's
  two accounts by email (admin@saccobuilt.com, coysacco@gmail.com);
  `sc_is_platform_admin()`. The start of the super-user SaaS admin.
  - **Feedback** (`sc_roadmap_submit`, any member of any org, estimators
    too; ten a day each): one box, its first line the title, a kind (Idea ·
    Something's wrong · Other) and the page it was sent from. It lands as
    **new — seen only by its writer and Coy**, because feedback can name an
    org's customers and jobs; nothing one org writes reaches another until
    Coy publishes it, and he can reword it first (what they sent is kept in
    `original_title` / `original_body`, shown only to him).
  - **The board** — Under consideration · Planned · In progress · Released
    (`considering` · `planned` · `in_progress` · `released`), published
    items only, with vote counts and comments; `declined` ("Not planned")
    is hidden again. One vote per person, on what isn't released yet
    (`sc_roadmap_vote`); comments (`sc_roadmap_comment`) carry the writer's
    name and their org's name, read when written, and only their writer or
    Coy removes one. A writer's name and org on an item show only once
    published.
  - **Coy only** (`sc_roadmap_admin`, `sc_roadmap_add`): the status, the
    words, a note shown on the item ("In the next update"), and items of his
    own straight onto the board. The page's **Inbox** lists new feedback from
    every org.
  - **Where:** `/dashboard/roadmap` (`src/components/v34/roadmap.tsx`), on
    the rail after Logs and in the Toolbox, open to estimators too
    (`menuPages()` / `redirectFor()`); columns when the pane is 900px+, a
    column at a time on a phone; search, Most votes / Newest. The Roadmap's own
    **Give feedback** opens the form (Settings carried a Give feedback row until 2026-10-10 —
    Coy: "we already have the roadmaps page icon (button) in the main menu"). APIs
    `/api/roadmap` (GET list + `admin`, POST feedback or `{add:true}`),
    `/api/roadmap/[id]` (PATCH `{vote}` or Coy's change),
    `/api/roadmap/[id]/comments` (GET, POST, DELETE `?comment=`) — session
    client; until 084 is applied the page says it is waiting for it.
    `src/lib/roadmap.ts`, tested in `tests/roadmap.test.mjs`.
  - Rehearsed (rolled back) on the live database: a member's feedback seen
    by them and Coy only, no vote on it, no admin change, no direct table
    read; Coy rewords and publishes it (original kept), adds his own; a
    member votes once (a second vote is a no-op), comments, unvotes; a
    declined item leaves the board; a signed-in user in no org sees nothing
    and can't send; anon refused. **Applied 2026-10-05** (`execute_sql`);
    checked after: RLS on all four tables, 2 platform admins, anon can't run
    `sc_roadmap_list`, authenticated has no select on `roadmap_items` and
    can't run `sc_roadmap_who`.
  - **The first card, Planned: AI Front Desk** (Coy, 2026-10-05) — a chat
    assistant on a contractor's website or as its own link for a Facebook or
    Google ad, asking in order for name, address, job type, job scope, then a
    phone number or email (one required); each finished chat a hot lead in
    Threads marked as from AI Front Desk, saved where the AI answering service
    saves, by chat instead of a call. Nothing of it is built.
  - **Also Planned (2026-10-06, Coy's notes, "coming soon"):** Street view
    on the customer's Location (needs a Google Maps key) and Invoices —
    taken off the app until built; later that day **Cash flow** (Coy:
    "add the descriptions of what it should be able to do"), its card
    describing §4–§5's timeline, cash vs accrual, runway and burn.
  - Not built: telling Coy about new feedback (activity line or email), or
    telling a writer when their item moves.
